# Changelog

All notable changes are documented in the [GitHub Releases](https://github.com/Parslee-ai/car-releases/releases) for this repo.
This file is a chronological index linking to each release's full notes.

## [v0.60.0] — 2026-10-31

### Changes

- `car doctor` recognizes the `commons` and `topology` folders that the shared contribution graph and coordination-topology records write under the state root, instead of offering them up as leftover debris. Self-healing's `[[org]] roots` expand `~` on Windows too, where the home directory comes from `USERPROFILE` rather than `HOME`.

- Walled agents on a Mac can run Apple's developer tools (`git`, `python3`, `clang` in `/usr/bin`) when Xcode is installed but nobody ran `xcode-select -s`. The wall granted the Command Line Tools in that case, while `xcrun` ran Xcode's tools, so every one of them was refused. It now resolves the active developer directory the way `xcode-select -p` does.

See the [release notes][v0.60.0].

## [v0.59.0] — tagged, never published; shipped in [v0.60.0]

v0.59.0 was tagged but never released. Every change below first reached users in v0.60.0.

### Changes

- The `a2a.start` listener no longer answers requests from web pages open in the operator's browser. Its JSON-RPC and stream routes now answer `403` to a request carrying a non-loopback `Origin`, on every bind, and, on a loopback bind, also to a request whose `Host` is not a loopback name, which is how a DNS-rebinding page reaches `127.0.0.1`. Before, any page could POST to the listener and reach its runtime's tools. Requests with no `Origin` (every CLI and SDK caller) are served as before. A loopback listener also serves the host of an explicitly supplied `public_url`, so a tunnel or reverse proxy that forwards the public host name keeps working. A listener started with `allow_non_loopback_bind` does not check `Host`, and the Agent Card routes stay public. For embedders: every `car_a2a::http` router, including the peer-authenticated one, refuses a foreign `Origin`. Only the new `build_router_bound_to(dispatcher, bound, public_url)` and `build_router_with_auth_bound_to(dispatcher, auth, bound, public_url)` also check `Host` when `bound` is a loopback address; `build_router`, `build_router_with_auth`, `serve` and `serve_with_auth` check `Origin` only. On those, a DNS-rebinding page can still make same-origin GET requests, which carry no `Origin`, so on a loopback bind it can read the `/a2a/stream/:task_id` task stream (read-only; writes are POSTs, which always carry `Origin`). A loopback embedder that isn't behind a tunnel should use `build_router_bound_to` or `build_router_with_auth_bound_to`, which also blocks a DNS-rebinding page from reading `/a2a/stream`. `a2a.start` already does. The `Origin` rule and the loopback set are now one implementation in `car_a2a::loopback`, shared with the MCP endpoint and the dashboard's sign-in routes; the MCP endpoint's own `Host` check is unchanged and also accepts any IP literal. As a result, the dashboard's sign-in routes also refuse an `Origin` header that is not valid UTF-8.

- A supervised agent now controls only A2UI surfaces it actually created: when a surface is deleted, recreated on the same id (by the host, an A2A peer or an agent) or expires, the agent that owned it loses that ownership, so it can no longer update or delete a surface the host later put on that id. A test also pins that `workflow.run` and `multi.*` build their runtimes without the operator's connector tools, so a walled agent cannot reach one through them.

- Every connection the `car` CLI opens with an agent's credential (its raw handshake and every `DaemonClient` it creates) is now an additional session: `session.auth { agent_id, token, attach: false }` has the agent's scope and method allowlist but no longer takes over the agent's live connection, so a `car` command run from an agent's shell no longer detaches the agent or ends its chats when it exits. The one exception is the supervised serve process itself (`car do --serve`, with or without `--governed-host`), which is the agent and still attaches. The SDK default is unchanged: a supervised process attaches. A second Rust process inside an agent that should not take over its connection calls `set_additional_agent_sessions(true)` from `car-daemon-client`; a Node or Python helper has no such option and must send `attach: false` in its own `session.auth`.

- `agents.stop` on an adopted agent whose pid cannot be verified as its process (an unreadable start token) now fails with "stop of agent <id> declined" instead of reporting `stopped`. The agent stays running and supervised and keeps its pid file. The token read failure is transient, so retrying the stop is the remedy; Windows records no start token and never declines. If the SIGKILL after the grace period was withheld, the error says the SIGTERM may already have been sent, and the exit it causes leaves the agent `stopped` instead of restarting it. `agents.restart` and `agents.remove` return the same error rather than restarting or dropping the spec of a live process, and shutdown's `stop_all` lists it as still running. A stopped agent's pid file is now cleared only once its process is gone, and a SIGKILL whose exit is not observed leaves the agent's `pid` in place so `stop_all` reports it rather than calling it gone. The `start_all` warning for an agent already running outside supervision now says what `agents.start` actually does (car#3081).

- CarHost's Build an agent screen (Command Deck, New Agent) now keeps the build it starts. After CarHost quits or restarts mid-build, the screen says it is checking on that build instead of showing a blank start screen, and finds it once CAR is connected; leaving the screen before then keeps the build. A build that ended comes back as its answers on the review: one CAR's quit or restart ended says "CAR quit or restarted while this was building, so nothing was added.", and one that ended some other way says how, as the in-chat build card already did. Choose Create build plan (Save & rebuild for an edit) to build it again. A build still running is followed, and a start whose reply hadn't come back is looked for once its start window has passed: followed if it is running, named if it ended, and if CAR still can't say, the screen says so with Try again. The message stays, across relaunches too, until you build again or let those answers go (discard them, choose another starting point, or open another agent to edit). If CAR is connected but can't reach the build, the screen says so and offers Try again. Answers you haven't built yet are still not kept after quitting.

- CarHost Chat: a saved agent that was saved with a model runs on that model; a chat with it shows that model in its model menu, which can't change it, and names the model that answered on its replies. Chats still send their own model choice, which applies to agents saved without one. A saved agent can't take attached files yet: its chat keeps any dropped or pasted file in the message box, says "<agent> can't take attached files yet. Remove them to send." and holds Send until they are removed, and Regenerate, Edit & Resend and Send again of a message with files say the same and change nothing. An Edit & Resend that is refused before it starts puts back every message it replaced. Clicking a "CAR … needs you" approval banner opens the chat at the waiting approval card, also when the click launches CAR, or Approvals when the chat no longer shows it.

- The `car` CLI's raw daemon connections and `car-host` now present the agent credential (`CAR_AGENT_ID` + `CAR_AGENT_TOKEN`, with `attach: false`) inside a supervised agent even when `CAR_AUTH_TOKEN` is also set, matching the SDK daemon client. Before, the env token won and the command ran as a generic daemon session outside the agent's identity and method allowlist; `car-host` also read the daemon token file from inside an agent, which it no longer does. A process with only one agent variable uses `CAR_AUTH_TOKEN` when set and is otherwise refused with an error naming the agent credential. Visible effect for an unscoped agent on the Mac, whose supervisor passes `CAR_AUTH_TOKEN`: its raw CLI commands now run as that agent, so `runs.list` shows only its own runs, and `car code` approve, cancel or subscribe on runs it did not start (including runs left unowned from before this upgrade) are refused. The CAR app can still act on those runs.
- Inside a supervised agent (`CAR_AGENT_ID` or `CAR_AGENT_TOKEN` set) the `car` CLI never presents CAR's local host credential (car#3072). `car start`, `car stop`, `car restart`, `car runs cancel`, `car board` and other dual-role calls go out under the agent's own identity, and the daemon decides what that identity may do. `car install`, `car uninstall`, `car ui` and other host-only commands are refused with "this command needs the operator (host); a supervised agent can't run it". The CLI's operator secret broker is not installed in an agent, so an agent's CLI reads secrets from the store directly, and on macOS that can show keychain prompts. The `car agent new` and `car agent run --scheduled` refusals now also apply to a process with only `CAR_AGENT_TOKEN` set. The operator's CLI is unchanged.

- Building an agent whose folder CAR can't use (often one in Downloads, Documents or Desktop, which macOS guards) no longer hangs for 15 minutes and then says only "The build stopped". When macOS refuses CAR the folder, the build fails at once and says how to fix it: allow CarHost in System Settings > Privacy & Security > Files and Folders, or pick a folder outside Downloads, Documents and Desktop. When making, saving in or copying the folder gets no answer for 45 seconds, the build fails and says that it waited and got no answer, that macOS may have asked whether CarHost can use the folder (allow it in Files and Folders), and that otherwise the disk should be checked or another folder picked. This holds for every copy of the folder the build makes for its test runs, not only the first: a folder that fails part way stops the build, instead of being counted as the agent failing a test and designed again. A build that fails this way has stopped, and it no longer goes on to "Designing the agent" once the folder becomes usable. A folder whose own permissions keep CAR from reading it now says to change those permissions, instead of telling you to move files out of it. The build's final check reports a folder that fails part way as a folder problem, not as the agent failing its scenarios. `declagents.run_scenarios` ("Run scenarios") returns an error naming the folder and the fix when CAR can't use the agent's folder for a scenario, and does not return the results of cases that already ran in that call (car#3176).

- Agent ids are unique ignoring letter case. `agents.upsert`, `agents.install` (WebSocket, NAPI, PyO3) and `car agents install` refuse a new id that differs from an existing agent's only in case, such as `Bob` beside `bob`, with an error naming the existing agent. On a case-insensitive file system such as APFS the two would otherwise share one folder, token, binary, logs and saved memory. Re-installing the same id works as before, and existing registrations are not changed.

- A supervised agent's secret values no longer stay in its log files after the process that received them is gone, and old values no longer reappear in `agents.tail_log` after a secret is rotated or removed, or after the agent or the daemon restarts. Each start of an agent (a run) now writes files of its own, `<id>.<run>.stdout.raw` and `<id>.<run>.stderr.raw`, created new and owner-only, beside a `<id>.<run>.run.json` record of salted fingerprints (never values) of everything the run is handed. While the run is alive, `agents.tail_log` redacts every value the daemon handed the agent in memory. When the process is gone (stop, restart, remove, a crash or exit, or daemon shutdown), the daemon reads each raw file from its first byte, writes it with every handed value replaced by `[redacted]` to a fsynced temporary, renames that to `<id>.<run>.<stream>.log`, a name that does not exist yet, and only then unlinks the raw file. Nothing is truncated or renamed over an existing file, so this works the same on a local disk and on an SMB/Azure Files state root, and a value split across writes is still found; where the file system has neither a no-replace rename nor hard links (an SMB mount on macOS) a run's end fails closed, keeping the raw file served redacted and reporting the failure. A process that outlives the agent and still holds a raw file open writes into the unlinked file: those late writes through its open descriptor are dropped, and never reach a file the tail, a feedback bundle or a later run reads. The copy stops at the length the raw file had when the run's end began, and the twin's rename refuses an existing name; a `.log` that is a symbolic link or has a second name is never trusted or served. A daemon that died without a shutdown leaves its runs for the next one, which recovers them at startup: it redacts a run again when the agent's declared secrets, resolved now, cover every value the run's record names, and otherwise withholds the raw files under `<id>.<run>.<stream>.withheld`, which are never served, never put in a feedback bundle, never deleted automatically, and are named in a `[car: …]` line at the end of the tail. A process adopted from an earlier daemon names its run in its pid record; its output is served redacted only when the values resolved at adoption cover that run, and is withheld when it ends otherwise. The tail reads every kept run in order, oldest first; the daemon keeps the last 20 finished runs per agent (set another number with `CAR_AGENT_LOG_RUNS_KEPT`), a run is kept or deleted whole, and the newest run of the running daemon is always kept. `agents.list` `log_path`/`stderr_log_path` and the tail's `stdout_path`/`stderr_path` now name the newest finished run's redacted log, never a running run's raw file, a log from before the upgrade, or a symbolic link. While a run is alive, the tail redacts each file's window whole, so a value spanning lines is hidden, reads ahead of a window that starts mid-file so a value it cuts is hidden, and hides the start of a value still being written at the end of the file; feedback bundles do the same. A run whose redacted log cannot be written keeps its raw file, served redacted, reports the failure in the agent's `status_reason` or as the new `log_scrub_error` field of the `agents.remove` result, and is retried by the next stop, removal or shutdown. **One-time effect of the upgrade:** an agent's logs from before it (`<id>.stdout.log`/`<id>.stderr.log`, and the copies an earlier scrub set aside) are withheld on its first recovery, whatever the earlier scrub marker says: they are not shown in the dashboard, `agents.tail_log` or feedback bundles, and they stay on disk as `<id>.0000000000000-00000000-0.<stream>.withheld` for the operator. An earlier scrub could miss a value split across writes, a `secret.get` reply, or a secret since rotated or no longer declared, and nothing now can tell those logs apart, so none is published. `secret.get` from a supervised agent now hands a value over only after recording it durably against a run of the agent this daemon holds, and otherwise fails with `log_record_error`: when the daemon holds no run of the agent (a process that outlived an earlier daemon and is not adopted), when a run has no record or its record cannot be read right now, or when the value cannot be written into it. So no run record understates what a raw file holds. A process adopted from before the upgrade is given a record at adoption, so it keeps receiving values, and its logs stay withheld. Two daemons sharing an SMB/Azure Files state root remain unsupported (car#3634). Feedback bundles read only finished runs' redacted logs and the raw file of a run whose values are known: the daemon includes a run it supervises with its values redacted, and `car feedback`, which runs without the daemon, only a run that was handed no values, so `car feedback` no longer includes the live log of an agent that was handed a secret until that run ends. Because each run's files are created new, symlinking `<id>.stdout.log` to `/dev/null` no longer discards an agent's output. A value printed in a form no text match finds (encoded or transformed), or split by the agent into pieces too short to match alone, is still not redacted (car#3150, car#3180, car#3589, car#3620, car#3637).

- Supervised-agent logs are now owner-only (the `logs/` directory 0700, each `<id>.stdout.log` and `<id>.stderr.log` 0600, tightened on daemon start for logs left by older releases), and `agents.tail_log` redacts known secret shapes (API keys, `ghp_` tokens, AWS key ids, `Bearer` tokens, `NAME_KEY=value` assignments) before returning lines. The files on disk still hold exactly what the agent printed, because the agent writes them directly and keeps running if the daemon dies; Parslee-ai/car#3090 stays open for that. The redactor also masks 40+ character hex or token-like runs (which includes git SHAs and digests) and rewrites `/Users/<name>` to `~` in the returned lines. A symlinked log or `logs/` directory is used as before and never chmod'ed.

- A supervised agent can no longer start an outside coding CLI (Claude Code, Codex, and others) through `foreman.run`, or through `coder.start` with an `external` or `foreman` engine, unless it is answering a chat the host started with it. It names that chat with the new `chat_session_id` parameter (NAPI `CoderStartOptions.chatSessionId` and `foremanRun(..., chatSessionId?)`, PyO3 `chat_session_id=`), the same rule `agents.invoke_external` already applies. An agent can confirm such a coder run (`coder.confirm_contract`, which starts the outside CLI) only while that chat turn is still running. `heal.run`, whose default engine is Foreman, follows the same rule and takes the same parameter. Without it, an agent's `coder.start` with `auto` runs on the native engine, which stays available to agents. An agent can never run the Foreman engine on a contract it supplied (`coder.start` with `foreman` and `contract` is refused and points at the native engine, where a supplied contract keeps running in the check sandbox; confirming a Foreman run with an edited contract is refused the same way), and an agent's `verify_command` and `union_verify_command` on `foreman.run` are always refused, because the daemon would run them without the approval the agent's own shell commands need. Hosts, CarHost and other callers that are not bound to an agent are unchanged. (car#2445)

- Daemon: removing an agent (`agents.remove`, `car uninstall`, the CarHost Agents settings, the SDKs' `agents_remove`) no longer reports success when its `manifest.toml` under `~/.car/agents/` cannot be deleted. That is every manifest there declaring the agent's id, since the next boot loads a manifest by the id it declares whatever its folder is called; a manifest there that cannot be read also fails the removal, because boot repairs its permissions and may load it as this agent. Before, the agent was dropped from the daemon's memory while the file stayed, so the next boot loaded it back. Now the file is deleted before the agent is dropped: when that fails the call returns an error naming the file, and the agent stays installed until a later removal can delete it (the removal has already stopped it, and does not start it again). The same holds for a worker whose `spec.toml` cannot be deleted. On an install that still has the legacy `agents.json`, its entry is rewritten before the manifest is deleted, and a failed rewrite also leaves the agent installed. Removing an agent also no longer deletes the folders of agents tracked on disk only (`pure_data` and `health_url`-only installs), which every removal of another agent used to delete.

- Daemon: removing an agent (`agents.remove`, `car uninstall`, the CarHost Agents settings, the SDKs' `agents_remove`) no longer returns an error after the agent is already gone. Before, a removal deleted the agent's files and then rewrote every other agent's `manifest.toml` from memory; a failure writing one of those turned the completed removal into an error, and a retry then reported nothing removed. A removal now rewrites no other agent's `manifest.toml` (so a hand edit in another agent's manifest also survives it), and removing a supervised agent reports `removed: true` once the files the next boot would load it from are deleted. The same holds for a worker whose other files, such as its handed-over values, cannot all be deleted after its `spec.toml` is gone: the removal succeeds and is recorded, the daemon logs the failure, and the files it could not delete stay on disk.

- An agent's run no longer counts as finished when a change it tried failed. If `write_file`, `edit_file`, `shell`, or any tool marked as one that changes something (the spreadsheet and Word tools, and generating a Parslee document to the connected drive, among them) returns an error, and no later call of the same tool succeeds, the run ends with "The agent stopped without finishing", naming the step that failed and the tool's error, even when the agent's last answer says "Done." CarHost shows the run as failed, routing learns it as a failure, and a test case with that error fails instead of passing on its answer text. Before, only the spreadsheet and Word tools in an agent that keeps a folder worked this way. A later successful change to the same file clears the failure whichever tool makes it, so an agent that follows a refusal pointing it to another tool (a spreadsheet written with `write_file` instead of `append_spreadsheet_row`) still finishes. A failed read or search, a web request that only reads (`http_request` with GET, HEAD or OPTIONS), and a command that ran and exited non-zero still leave the run to the agent's answer. An agent with a goal check is not stopped by such a failure: its next try is told what failed, and the run fails only if the last try still has it. A failed test case now reports the run's error rather than "expected output containing …", which read wrongly when the answer did contain the text.

- An agent's run is now told the local date, time and time zone when it starts, so a run asked to write a timestamp or "today's" date no longer makes one up. A scheduled run is also told the time it was scheduled for, in the schedule's own time zone when it uses one other than the Mac's, and a missed time run by hand is told which time it is for. The test runs while an agent is being built get the date and time too.

- Build an Agent: an agent now runs on the model it was built with. Picking a model to build with saves it as the agent's model, so its test cases, Run now, chats with it and routed runs all use it. Rebuilding on Automatic, or on the same model, keeps the saved model and its reasoning effort; picking a different one replaces it.
- If an agent's model isn't available when it runs, CAR never quietly uses another one. With no other model connected, Parslee's default route (`parslee/advisor`) answers instead, strictly, with no on-device fallback. Whenever Parslee served a turn of the run, the result carries `stood_in: { for_model, on_model, notice }` beside an unchanged `output`, even if the run then failed for another reason; the agent page shows "Ran on Parslee because <model> isn't available.", and `car agent run` prints it on stderr. When Parslee never answered, there is only the error. With other models connected (a Codex CLI counts when its sign-in is confirmed), or when Parslee itself isn't available, the run stops with "This agent runs on <model>, which isn't available. Reconnect it, or edit the agent to pick another model." That holds only when every credential state is known. When CAR can't tell whether a model is connected (a secret-store error, or a Codex CLI whose sign-in is unknown, which is always the case on Windows), it never reads that as "unavailable": it tries the agent's model first, before Parslee's availability is considered, and a failure ends with the same suggestion.
- Edit agent opens on the saved model, and its Inference card reads "Runs on <model>, the model it was built with".
- Edit agent: leaving without changes just leaves, with no dialog. When something changed, the dialog asks "Discard your changes?" and says "Your saved agent stays exactly as it is.", with "Discard changes" and "Keep editing". The bottom-bar exit reads "Leave without saving", and an edit's later exits (Start fresh, Back to Agents) use the same words.

- A supervised agent on macOS and Windows can no longer answer, through `host.resolve_approval`, an approval meant for the person. Before this, an agent could open a second connection and approve its own parked high-risk call, or another agent's. A session bound to an agent now gets an error for two kinds of row, and the row is left as it is: any row the high-risk-method gate raised (`ws.method:<method>`), whoever's call it parks, and a system-level approval the agent raised itself (`host.request_approval` with `system_level: true`). Each refusal is recorded as a new `approval.resolve_refused` host event (its `reason` is `gate_row` or `own_system_row`) and as a warning in the daemon log. This matches Linux, where a walled agent cannot call the method at all. CarHost and the other host apps, the daemon-token operator, `car-host approve` and the iMessage and Slack approval transports resolve these rows as before. A session-owned approval (`system_level: false`) is the agent's own record, not a human gate, so its owning connection can still resolve it. That is how an agent records a decision the person made in the agent's own UI, and how it completes a decision CarHost forwards to it through `approval.resolve_requested`. `host.dismiss_approval` is also closed to the same gap: an agent re-authenticating on its connection could, for a moment, dismiss another agent's resolved approval and receive the whole row; it is now held to its own rows throughout, and the method's capability role reads `agent`.

- A session bound to a supervised agent can no longer install, replace or remove an OS schedule (#3440). `scheduler.os_install` and `scheduler.os_uninstall` now refuse it on every platform, macOS and Windows included ("… is not available to a supervised agent: agents get no runtime scheduling; …"), following CAR in the Cloud outcomes D1: an agent's schedule is the `cadence` its author declared for it (declarative agents), it changes only with a new version, and only a person pauses or removes it. `tasks.schedule` and `tasks.unschedule` refuse it the same way, including on a daemon with no host token installed, where their host-role gate let every session through. `scheduler.os_install` and `scheduler.os_uninstall` now carry role `operator_only` (`tasks.schedule` and `tasks.unschedule` stay `host`); host and operator sessions and the CLI keep all four, and `scheduler.os_reconcile` is unchanged. The two methods leave the set an agent token's `method_allowlist` may name: a new scope naming either is refused, and an existing agent whose saved scope names one keeps working, with that entry dropped and a warning naming it.
- `scheduler.run` and `scheduler.run_loop` from a session bound to a supervised agent still run, but no longer read or write the shared task store (#3440, D1). Before, the posted task was saved there, so an agent could plant an interval command task the daemon's command scheduler then ran every interval, or overwrite another task's id with a manual trigger so `scheduler.os_reconcile` removed that task's OS schedule. An agent's runs are no longer deduped across calls; host and operator sessions and the CLI are unchanged.

- Agents CAR runs no longer inherit the daemon's environment. A supervised agent starts with a short allowlist of ordinary process variables (PATH, HOME, USER, the locale and temp-dir variables, SHELL, TERM, CAR_HOME and, on Windows, the system variables a program needs to start), the `CAR_*` variables the supervisor sets, and its literal `env`; provider keys the daemon holds do not reach it. An agent that needs a key lists its name in the manifest, `secrets = ["ANTHROPIC_API_KEY"]` (`agents.upsert` takes `secrets: string[]`), and the daemon fetches it from the secret store at every start, with a policy check and a value-free audit row per read. A missing secret, a policy denial or a locked store blocks the start with a reason naming the secret or store in `agents.list` (`status_reason`); upsert rejects an invalid, reserved or empty name and a name that is also a literal `env` key. A supervised agent's `secret.get` now refuses names it did not declare with `not_declared`, and `secretGet` / `secret_get` in a supervised Node or Python agent go through the daemon and never read the keychain themselves. On a Linux machine with no Secret Service, the store is one owner-only file per key under `~/.car/secrets/`, and a declared secret resolves from there. An agent that declares nothing is flagged in the daemon log and by a `secrets_notice` in `agents.list`, `car inspect` prints its declared names, and `car doctor` lists agents that started in the last 30 days without a declaration. `agents.tail_log` shows a delivered secret value as `[redacted]`. An agent adopted after an unclean daemon death keeps its old environment until it next restarts. CAR's threat model is protecting people from the agents they create on their own computers: an agent gets whatever CAR deliberately gives it, which is its declared secrets. Agents run as the same OS user as the person and CAR does not wall them off from that user's files, so a declaration is not a boundary against an agent that reads the secret store, `~/.car/secrets` or the daemon token file directly. The pass-through list also keeps `SSH_AUTH_SOCK`, the proxy variables and the CA-bundle variables; `secret.list`, `secret.status`, `secret.put` and `secret.delete` are refused to a supervised agent's session; `car install` prints the secret names a manifest will receive.

- A process running as a supervised agent (`CAR_AGENT_ID` or `CAR_AGENT_TOKEN` set) no longer silently falls back to the daemon-wide token. The daemon client (NAPI, PyO3, `agent_loop`) and the `car` CLI's raw handshake present the agent credential, use `CAR_AUTH_TOKEN` only when the supervisor put it in the environment (unscoped agents on the Mac are unchanged), and never read the token file; a process with only one of the two agent variables now gets an error naming the agent credential. Processes that are not agents behave as before.

- Build an Agent: an agent now runs on the schedule chosen for it, even while CAR is closed. The answer to "When should this work be requested?" is saved as the agent's schedule when it is built (every N minutes, hourly, daily, weekdays, weekends, or named days at a time; "only when I ask" stays manual), and the daemon installs a durable OS schedule that runs `car agent run --scheduled <id>`. On macOS that is a launchd job. On Linux it is a crontab line (an interval there must be every N minutes dividing the hour evenly, every N hours dividing the day evenly, or every day; any other interval is refused there); on Windows a Task Scheduler task for an interval of whole minutes up to 31 days or a single time on one day or every day, with no log file. An interval (every N minutes or hours) on Windows runs on Task Scheduler's own timer: it is not checked for lateness, never records a missed time, and may shift by an hour after a daylight-saving change. A daily or single-day schedule keeps its clock time there and follows the 15-minute rule. Other schedules (on Windows, any fixed-zone schedule, intervals included), and any schedule under a non-default `CAR_HOME` or a `CAR_DECLAGENTS_PATH` registry on Linux or Windows, are saved and reported as not installed, with the reason, which `declagents.runs` keeps showing. When the platform's scheduler cannot take a schedule (judged by the same check that installs it), CAR removes the agent's earlier job (if that removal fails, the schedule's note says so, and a launch still refuses to run it), `declagents.runs` shows no next run or missed times for it, and neither a scheduled launch nor Run now on one of its times runs it. A schedule runs on the Mac's own clock, or on a named zone's wall clock (`ET`, `America/New_York`, or the builder's zone) wherever the Mac is. An answer CAR cannot read saves no schedule and the approval says so; the build still succeeds.
- Schedules can be paused and changed without touching the agent: `declagents.pause` stops only the schedule (Run now and chat keep working), and `declagents.set_schedule` replaces it and saves it as the builder answer, so rebuilding the agent keeps it. Both are host-management only. Removing or disabling an agent takes its schedule down; approving a build keeps a pause made while it ran; the daemon repairs schedules at boot, including one that still points at a `car` an update replaced. Each CAR state folder keeps its own schedules and never replaces or removes another folder's; a job carries only the `CAR_HOME` and `CAR_DECLAGENTS_PATH` its daemon was started with, so the default folder's jobs run as the default folder. Cadences are validated when an agent is saved (a schedule repeats at most once a minute, and cron numbers are plain digits), and the cadence is no longer part of an agent's fingerprint. For an agent not made with Build an Agent, approving an edit keeps a schedule set with `declagents.set_schedule` while the edit was built, unless the edit itself changed the schedule; for a Build an Agent agent, that change also changes its builder answer, so an edit started before it is refused as out of date and must be rebuilt.
- Every scheduled run leaves a record (`agent-runs/<agent>/<run>.json` beside the agent registry, `~/.car` by default; on macOS and Linux the files are readable only by your user account, on Windows they take the folder's permissions, and the host reads them through `declagents.runs`, which is host-management only) with its output, error, serving model and scheduled and actual times. A schedule with clock times (a cron, or an interval that fits the clock such as every 15 minutes or every 2 hours) runs a time only within 15 minutes of it: after the Mac wakes from sleep, the most recent time runs if it is under 15 minutes old, and earlier times, or a time the Mac was shut down at, appear as missed in `declagents.runs` (a launch 15 minutes or more late also records its time as missed), with no catch-up run. An interval that does not fit the clock, such as every 7 minutes, cannot tell a late launch from an on-time one, so after sleep it runs once on wake, and it reports no missed times. A time from before the schedule started or resumed is never run or missed, and within one CAR state folder two launches of one time run it once; a registry shared by several state folders (through `CAR_DECLAGENTS_PATH`) is not supported for scheduling. If the daemon stops between taking a time and writing its record, that time shows as missed, or as failed once a later launch of it finds it taken; it never runs twice. `declagents.runs` (host-management only) returns the records plus each scheduled agent's next run, last run and missed times (the latest 20); records come as short previews, the newest 50 per agent by default, paged with `limit` and `before`, and `run_id` returns one in full. Run now on a missed time (`declagents.invoke { trigger: "manual", scheduled_for }`) records the run against that time, so it is no longer missed and shows as run by hand. When the daemon cannot be reached, or stops mid-run, the scheduled run records the failure itself. A Mail agent whose Mail Automation permission is not granted fails its scheduled run with a plain message instead of running without Mail.
- `schedule.suggest` now treats a `timezone` param as a fixed zone (`timezone_mode: "fixed"`), reports a name that is not an IANA zone as `reason`, accepts more phrases (`every day`, `weekends`, several named days, `am`/`pm` times, `only when I ask`, a trailing zone), and returns a plain-words `summary`.

- A supervised agent on macOS and Windows no longer reads every agent's approvals and host events. `host.approvals`, `host.events` and `host.subscribe` (its snapshot and its live `host.event` stream) now show a session bound to an agent only the records attributed to that agent, the way `host.agents` already showed it only its own row; before, an agent got every other agent's approval rows, including their call arguments in `details.params_preview`, and every agent's host events. The approval the high-risk-method gate raises for an agent's own call is now attributed to that agent, so it stays in that agent's `host.approvals`; an agent approving its own gated call is now recorded as a self-resolution (an `approval.self_resolved` host event and a warning in the daemon log), as it already was for `host.request_approval` rows. Host apps (CarHost, the iOS, Android and Windows hosts), the daemon-token operator and the CLI still see everything. Watcher agents stay possible: the new host-management-only `agent_permissions.set_observer { agent_id, enabled }` grants one agent the full view of those three methods: its `host.subscribe` snapshot then lists every agent, while `host.agents` stays own-row. Set it in CarHost under Settings → Agent Permissions ("Can see other agents' approvals and events"; the card's Reset also withdraws it), with `car approvals set-observer <agent> on|off`, or through the generated wrappers `agentPermissionsSetObserver` / `agent_permissions_set_observer`. It is off for every agent by default, an agent cannot grant itself, and it is stored as `observers` in `agent-permissions.json` beside the tier and tool overrides, so `agent_permissions.get` lists it and it outlives a daemon restart. A grant or withdrawal takes effect at once on that agent's open subscription. The watcher view applies on macOS and Windows; on Linux a walled agent still cannot call these three methods at all, so for a walled agent the grant changes nothing there (CAR's own assistant runs unwalled on Linux and is scoped like any agent-bound session). Downgrading: while a watcher is granted, a daemon, `car` CLI or embedded runtime older than this release reading the same `~/.car` treats `agent-permissions.json` as damaged, so nothing runs on a tier allow and every gated call asks, unattended calls (goal checks, replays) are refused, and no answer or permission change can be saved. Upgrading again, or withdrawing the grant first, avoids or reverses it.

- An Anthropic `stop_reason: refusal` now lets an auto-routed request fall through to the next candidate, as Google, Vertex, Bedrock `content_filtered` and OpenAI Responses refusals already do: it is one vendor's ruling on its own model, so the next model is a different ruling, not a replay. It is still typed `ContentRefused`, still not charged to the model, and a request pinned with `strict_model` still gets `-32007`. A gateway's refusal (`parslee`) and a Bedrock guardrail still end the chain.

- When a Claude model declines a request (`stop_reason: "refusal"`), CAR now returns a content refusal (`ContentRefused`, JSON-RPC `-32007`) instead of an empty successful answer, on both the streaming and non-streaming paths. A refusal ends the fallback chain rather than being answered by another model, and the API key is not marked as failing for it. Claude Opus 5 refuses some ordinary requests this way, such as tracing a small stack-machine program. The benchmark harness grades a refusal as the model's (empty) answer rather than as a run that failed to complete.

- `host.request_approval` takes an optional `a2ui` (`{ components, dataModel? }`) so an agent can draw its approval as an A2UI component tree instead of relying on the host to make sense of `details`. CarHost renders it inside the approval card, under CarHost's own header and Approve and Deny buttons. The daemon refuses the request unless the tree passes `car_a2ui::approval_surface`: display components only (`Text`, `Row`, `Column`, `List`, `Card`, `Divider`, `Image`, `Icon`, `Chart`, `Spacer`, `Badge`), a `root`, and at most 64 KiB. A `Button` or any other input is refused, because an agent's own Approve button would be a forged control. The row stores the checked surface in a new `HostApproval.a2ui` field. `action` and `details` are unchanged and still required, since the Windows tray, the mobile cards and the iMessage and Slack prompts show those. A daemon older than this release ignores `a2ui`, so an agent that sends it still gets its `details` rendered there.

- CarHost Chat: an approval card names the real folder for every path shape. A folder path with a tab or another control character used to skip resolution. For list_dir, find_files and grep_files it was shown as typed, so a symlink in it could send the operation elsewhere. For write_file and edit_file it had no folder line. Such paths are now resolved like any other, and the control characters show as their codes (for example `⟨U+0009⟩`). "Runs in:" for a command and the default folder of a search are resolved the same way. A path holding a NUL character names no folder, because the file system would cut it short there and a write refuses it. A relative folder with no absolute working folder to resolve it under names no folder, as a file path already did. So does a command's "Runs in:" folder that isn't an absolute path. Fixes #2929.

- CarHost Chat: an approval card's "Folder:" line now names the folder the file operation actually reaches. Before, the card shortened a path by reading its text. A path that went through a symlink and then `..` (for example `link/../report.txt`) could therefore show a different folder from the one the write lands in. The folder is now resolved the way the daemon reaches it. The deepest part that exists has its symlinks followed first. Folders that don't exist yet are named where they will be created. A file that is itself a symlink is named by the folder of what it points to. When macOS privacy settings keep CarHost from looking into a folder the daemon may still write to (such as Documents), the card names that folder exactly as the agent gave it. When a folder can't be checked for lack of permission, the card shows no folder line rather than a guess. This covers write_file and edit_file, and list_dir, find_files and grep_files, with a relative or an absolute folder. Fixes #2911.

- Approving an Agent-project build no longer reports `merged` while its agent is unregistered. `coder.approve_merge` now checks the agent and the built spec before committing, and holds the daemon's registry and schedule locks until the build is registered. A `declagents.*` change made in the daemon during the approval waits until the build is registered; meanwhile every agent-registry write and schedule sync or launch in the daemon waits for that one local git commit. Two cases now take the commit back off the project's `main` and refuse, with the session still waiting for approval so approving again retries:
  - the registration is refused after the commit because the agent changed from outside the daemon (a second daemon sharing `declagents.json`, or a hand edit);
  - the registry file could not be read or written.
- An approval interrupted between its commit and its registration finishes when approved again, instead of failing with "the worktree is clean". If its agent changed meanwhile, the approval is refused and the commit is taken back.
- A spec that cannot be saved against its agent is refused before anything is committed.

- Approval prompts no longer let an agent forge lines that look like trusted approval fields. Slack, iMessage and Windows tray summaries now collapse a parameter's name onto one line the way they already collapsed its value, and print it quoted (`"path": /etc/hosts`). A parameter named `Agent`, or one whose name contains a line break, can no longer pass for the summary's own `Agent:` line. Bidirectional control characters are dropped from those lines. Slack now escapes `&`, `<` and `>` in the action and summary, so agent text can't post a link captioned however it likes or ping `<!channel>`. The Windows tray now collapses the approval's action, agent id and an agent-written `summary`/`reason` onto one line each, as iMessage and Slack already did. The agent's own summary keeps the full 700-character summary length.
- CarHost on macOS, CarHost on iOS and the `car-server` web dashboard now escape U+2028, U+2029 and U+0085 inside the parameters JSON, and keep the action headline to one line (at most two lines on screen). The macOS Command Deck now shows a mail or message approval the way the Dashboard does: each field in its labelled column, with the body in its own labelled block. On iOS and Android, the approval card collapses every kind of line break and drops bidirectional controls. The Risk and Reason rows now come only from the approval's own top-level fields, so a tool parameter named `risk` no longer sets the card's Risk. Android, which has no raw details view, still shows such a parameter as its own quoted row (`"risk": None`); on iOS it stays under Request details.

- The flagship assistant (`car do`) can no longer read or write CAR's secret store through its file tools. On macOS and Linux, `read_file`, `write_file`, `edit_file` and `grep_files` now refuse everything under `<CAR_HOME>/secrets/`, as they already refused the daemon token files. Because `car do` works from `/`, a model could previously read a stored key by its path. The path is judged by what it resolves to, so a relative path, `..`, a symbolic link (dangling ones included) or a hard link to a key is refused too, and a `grep_files` search over an ordinary folder leaves out any protected file it meets, so a link to a key adds no matches. `list_dir` or `find_files` naming a token file or the secrets folder itself is refused; listing their parent still shows the names, and `find_files` leaves out every name inside the secrets folder. On Windows the secrets folder is not guarded, and the refusal names only the token files. For embedders, `car_engine::agent_basics::execute_with_ledger_guarded_hiding` is `execute_with_ledger_guarded` plus a second guard whose refused files `find_files` leaves out of its results.

- CarCoder's cross-vendor audit now excludes every vendor whose model wrote any part of the change, not only the last one. Before this, a change one vendor wrote and another repaired could be audited by the first vendor's model. This covers:
  - daemon sessions, Build an Agent sessions and `car code-task`, including repair rounds after a failed check;
  - mixed changes: foreman's red union repaired by the native loop, and an external CLI that ran a round before the native loop took over. That CLI is recorded on the session as a co-writer (a later hand-over adds to the record), and its vendor is excluded beside the native writers'. A CLI that served a later round after a native round is excluded too;
  - sessions reopened after a daemon restart, which keep their saved writers.
- The audit attestation keeps `builder_family` (the last writer's vendor) and adds `builder_families`, which lists every writer's vendor. Delivery is refused when the auditor's vendor is in that list. Attestations recorded before this release have no `builder_families` and are still checked against `builder_family`.
- Sessions that delivered before can now be refused:
  - An earlier writer the catalog cannot place now refuses delivery, the way an unplaceable last writer already did. An `auto` Build an Agent session is saved marked not independently checked instead.
  - When a Build an Agent build's passing spec came from a model that reported no name, `authored_by` keeps the earlier writers and ends with `unknown-builder`, so the build is treated as having an unplaceable builder.
  - A co-writing external CLI whose vendor cannot be placed refuses delivery, for example an unpinned Codex that read the user's own configuration, or a distributed foreman run.
- When every configured auditor comes from a writer's vendor, the refusal names all of those vendors.

- Build an Agent no longer fails when no second AI model is available to review the build: if the agent's own tests passed, it is saved, runs normally, and is marked "Saved without an independent check" on the finish screen and its page (`verification: { status: "not_checked" }` on the registered spec). Other coder runs end with a reason naming what is missing — `inference_unavailable` (left out of A/B scoring, as before) when the Parslee gateway doesn't serve its auditor routes, `configuration` when no auditor is eligible — and self-heal backs the item off instead of rebuilding it every tick; headless `car code-task` books both as `config_error` rather than a failed contract. The automatic auditor choice also never picks an on-device model, even one registered under a name like `google/…`, and skips Parslee routes the gateway doesn't serve (this only helps when another auditor follows them, which today's ordering doesn't produce).

- car-server's dashboard routes now also require the TCP peer to be loopback and refuse requests carrying proxy headers, so a forged `Host: 127.0.0.1` from another machine gets 403. Starting car-server with a non-loopback `--host` logs a warning that remote browsers cannot sign in and remote access needs an ssh tunnel; a reverse proxy in front of it does not work.

- When CAR turns on extended thinking by itself (complex prompts and coding turns), it now adds the thinking budget on top of the caller's `max_tokens` wherever the provider counts reasoning against `max_tokens` (Anthropic, and OpenAI reasoning models). Before, a caller that set its own `max_tokens` could get an empty answer from an adaptive-thinking model: Claude Sonnet 5 and Opus 5 spent the whole 3,000-token allowance thinking on car-judged's proof tasks and answered nothing. Providers that don't charge reasoning that way are unchanged.

- Build an Agent: an agent built with the model left on **Automatic** no longer fails at the end with "builder model 'unknown-builder' is not in the model catalog … delivery refused". The build now records the model that actually wrote and repaired the agent (it shows in the session's `authored_by`), and the final independent check picks a reviewer from a different company than that model. If CAR still cannot tell which company makes the builder's model and the reviewer is chosen automatically (`auditor_model = "auto"`, the default), the agent is saved marked "not independently checked", with the reason "The AI model that built it couldn't be identified, so CAR couldn't choose a model from a different company to review it.", instead of the build failing. With a reviewer chosen by name, and in other `coder.start` sessions, an unknown builder model is still refused, but now as soon as verification starts, before any outcome is graded, and in a plain sentence instead of the internal name. `car code-task` gives the same plain sentence.

- CarHost: the Automation permission row in setup and in Settings now says what it covers, and no longer says it lets agents drive Mail, Calendar and Notes. The row is normally the System Events permission, which scripts use to type and click in other apps; Mail and Notes each need their own Automation permission, and CAR's calendar tools use the Calendar permission. While Settings shows the Email starter agent, the Automation row is Mail's permission instead: it shows Mail's status, its Request button asks macOS about Mail, and its text says it is Mail's. Before, the row showed System Events' status there, so with System Events allowed and Mail not, it offered Open Settings instead of a Mail request (#3250).

- The daemon's agent file tools no longer reach CAR's own credential and authority files (#3390). On every WS session (whether it called `agents.register_basics` or reached the built-ins through a client-registered tool whose callback answers "unknown tool"), the fresh runtime behind a non-shared `a2a.start`, and the embedded A2A dispatcher's default runtime, `read_file`, `write_file`, `edit_file`, `grep_files`, `list_dir` and `find_files` refuse a path that resolves to the `auth-token` or `host-token` copies, `env`, anything under `secrets/`, this host's peer identity key `peer-identity.key`, the approval journal the daemon records to (`approvals.jsonl` under the state root, or the path given to `ServerStateConfig::with_approval_journal`) or `agent-permissions.json`, and CAR's host configuration: `agents.json` and each `agents/<id>/manifest.toml` (the supervisor's configured manifest, the per-agent manifests and the `workers/<handle>/spec.toml` files beside it too, under `--agents-manifest` / `CAR_AGENTS_MANIFEST`, including on a daemon that only observes that manifest) and `workers/<handle>/spec.toml` (a new one too, which would add a supervised agent or worker), `declagents.json` (or `CAR_DECLAGENTS_PATH`), the scheduled-task store `tasks/`, and `messaging.json`. The check is on what the path resolves to, not how it is spelled: absolute, relative, `..`, a symlink or dangling symlink, a hard link (macOS and Linux), and a name that does not exist yet but that the volume folds to a protected one (`RUN/HOST-TOKEN`, `approvalſ.jsonl`; on Windows also a trailing dot or space or `:stream`). Before, a client holding only the daemon token could read the host token and write the standing-approval journal through these tools. The refusal names the class of file, never its contents; `grep_files` over a folder holding one of these files still runs and leaves it out. A new name containing non-ASCII characters directly in the state root, `run/`, the platform token folder or `secrets/` is refused too, the rule #3225 applies to the memory files; existing files, ASCII new names there, and non-ASCII names elsewhere are unaffected. What this does not protect: the user's own files stay readable and writable by a daemon-token client's file tools. Every other path behaves as before.
- car-engine adds `Runtime::set_local_path_guard` and the `agent_basics::LocalPathGuard` type, plus `agent_basics::execute_with_ledger_guarded`: a refusal the built-in file tools apply on the runtime's built-in fallback path while the substrate is local (each path tool's target, `.` when no path is given, and each file `grep_files` reads, which is skipped rather than refused). `set_local_path_guard` replaces any guard installed earlier. A `Runtime` an application builds in its own process is unguarded unless it installs one.

- Bedrock models honor the AWS-standard endpoint overrides `AWS_ENDPOINT_URL_BEDROCK_RUNTIME` and `AWS_ENDPOINT_URL` (service-specific wins), so a VPC, FIPS or other custom Bedrock runtime endpoint can be used. With neither set, calls still go to `https://bedrock-runtime.<region>.amazonaws.com`. Requests are still SigV4-signed, now for the endpoint's host and any path prefix it carries. Plain `http` is accepted only for localhost, 127.0.0.0/8 and `::1`; every other endpoint must be `https`. The override is read only from the process environment, not from `~/.car/env` or `car keys`.

- Bedrock: a model's region is now checked against the AWS region shape (lowercase letters, digits and hyphens, such as `us-east-1`) before it is placed in the `bedrock-runtime.<region>.amazonaws.com` host name. A region containing `.`, `/`, `:`, `@` or similar could send SigV4-signed requests (access key ID, session token and the prompt) to a different host. `models.register` now refuses such a row, and inference refuses one already in `models.json` before anything is sent. A streamed call to a Bedrock model, which has no streaming path, is now refused with `UnsupportedMode` (`mode: "streaming"`) instead of sending the prompt unsigned to `<endpoint>/converse` (#3064).

- Fixed Bedrock calls to model ids containing ":" (nearly all of them, e.g. `anthropic.claude-3-5-sonnet-20240620-v1:0`) being rejected with SignatureDoesNotMatch: the SigV4 signer now URI-encodes each path segment twice in the canonical request, as AWS requires for every service except S3.

- The model catalog's CAR benchmark scores are now pooled per weight precision, not across a model's quantizations. A 4-bit build and an 8-bit build of the same model can behave very differently: GLM-4.7-Flash at 4 bits loops endlessly and scored 0.081 on `car-judged`, while the 8-bit build answers. The old pooling would have put that 0.081 on the 8-bit row too. Builds of the same precision still share a score across providers and formats, so an MLX 4-bit result still ranks the Ollama `Q4_K_M` row. A catalog row whose id names no precision takes it from its `quantization` field. Today's published scores are unchanged by this.

- `car code` and `car board` now exit when their terminal goes away (the window or tab is closed, or the pty that drives them closes) and on SIGTERM afterwards. Before, the board kept running with no terminal, spun one core at about 99% CPU, kept polling the daemon, and ignored SIGTERM, so only `kill -9` stopped it.
- The `car code` contract-approval menu and its yes/no prompts (such as "Publish the branch?") now cancel the task and exit as a hangup (129, or SIGHUP) when their terminal closes. Before, they could spin the same way, out of reach of SIGTERM, or crash with exit 101 writing the cancel line to the closed terminal, and closing the terminal while the menu was still drawing left the task waiting for an answer. These prompts now read keys on a separate thread, so input that never returns can no longer keep Ctrl-C, SIGTERM or SIGHUP from ending them.

- The stable release bookkeeping pull request now stamps the Windows host lockfile and carries the title the merge queue accepts, so it merges without hand fixes.

- A browser session dropped without being closed no longer SIGKILLs a live process when it could not record Chromium's start time at launch: that pid may belong to an unrelated process by then. The launch record stays, and the next orphan sweep matches the process by its exact profile argument before signalling it.

- A Chromium that CAR launched no longer outlives a CAR process that dies without closing it (car-releases#99). Before, a daemon, agent or runtime that was SIGKILLed or crashed left its Chromium running, because macOS has no parent-death signal. That Chromium held the profile's `SingletonLock`, so the next launch on the persistent profile fell back to a throwaway one and started signed out. Every launch now writes a record under `$CAR_HOME/run/browser/`, which `shutdown()` and drop delete. The daemon at startup, and every browser launch, terminates each recorded Chromium whose owning process is gone. It sends SIGTERM, then SIGKILL, and clears the profile's stale `SingletonLock`/`SingletonSocket`/`SingletonCookie`. A process is signalled only after two checks: its start time matches the record, and its argv contains the exact `--user-data-dir=<dir>` argument. When the start time matches but the argv does not, the process is left alone and its record kept for a later pass. A substring match on the path is never enough, so a `tail -f` or an editor open on a file in the profile is never killed. Consumers can drop their own `pgrep`/`SIGKILL` cleanup. macOS and Linux only; Windows is not covered. There is no `browser.sessions`/`browser.reap` RPC; startup and pre-launch reaping replace it. Rust embedders can call `car_browser::orphans::reap_orphans()` directly.
- `browserClose()` / `browser_close()` / `browser.close` has reset the browser session since v0.5.1, and now has a regression test (car-releases#100). Chromium shuts down, and the next `browserRun` launches a fresh browser with the options it passes, `headed` and `extraArgs` included. Navigate and observe work against the new browser as they would in a new process. Consumers that navigate the live tab to `about:blank` instead of closing can call `browserClose()`.
- `extraArgs` / `extra_args` on `browserRun` / `browser_run` / `browser.run` (and `LaunchOptions::extra_args`) now reach Chromium. Each flag, passed as documented (`--use-fake-ui-for-media-stream`), arrived on Chromium's command line as `----use-fake-ui-for-media-stream`, which Chromium ignores, so no extra flag passed this way had any effect. The leading `--` is now stripped before chromiumoxide adds its own. A flag passed without the `--` keeps working.
- A dropped browser no longer SIGKILLs a pid that a later process inherited after `shutdown()` already reaped Chromium; drop now checks the pid's start time first, and keeps the launch record when Chromium outlives the kill.

- CarHost Chat (macOS): you can build an agent without leaving the chat. When you describe work you'll want done again, Parslee Core offers to make it a standing agent (or, when your first message already asks for repeating work, says it is setting one up), asks only what you need to decide (the job, one or two real examples and what a good result contains, what it may use, whether it may read your Mail, what it must never do or keep private, and its name), and shows a draft in the chat. The draft lists the examples you gave, which the build gets when it writes its tests, and the model it is built with and then runs on, its folder and its schedule, each marked Recommended until you change it. Nothing is created until you choose **Build it**, which approves the draft and builds it while the card shows each step. The card then lists the tests the build ran, passed or failed, and whether a second AI model reviewed the work or it was not independently checked. **Add to my agents** is the one step that adds it; then you can run it on your first example, and the card says when that run is going and how it ended, or chat with it. Asking to change one of your agents shows what will change, including when a rebuild takes away its Parslee cloud tools, with the same two clicks. Every copy of a card in the chat is one card, so building from one shows on all of them. If the app relaunches during a build, the card finds the same build again; a build that stopped (CAR restarted under it, it was discarded, or it failed) says why, the same after a relaunch, and offers Build it again, while a build CAR can't reach offers only Try again. A draft can't be built while another build of the same agent is running or starting, from a chat or the Build an Agent screen; if CAR's reply to a start is lost, the card offers Try again, which looks for the one build of that draft created while the start could still be under way, rather than starting another; that time runs from 5 seconds before the start request was sent to 210 seconds after it (the request's 180-second reply limit plus 30 seconds). If the app relaunches while a start request is out, the card shows the build starting until that time has passed, then offers Try again. Once it has passed with no build of the agent running, and CAR can say when every finished build of that draft was created, the card says so, or says how a build of that draft created in that time ended (it failed or was stopped), and offers Build it again. If a build of that draft created in that time has been added to your agents, Try again shows the agent as added. A start refused before anything was sent (another build of the agent is running or starting, or the name is taken) says the build didn't start and keeps the draft. For a change to one of your agents, the card loads the agent first and offers Build it only once it shows what changes; if the agent can't be loaded it says so and offers Try again, and a rebuild after a stop goes ahead only when the agent is as the card last showed it, otherwise the card shows the change again. A start held back on the Build an Agent screen returns to its review, or to the edit screen, with your answers and a note naming that screen's own button (Create build plan, or Save & rebuild for an edit); the note clears when you go back, pick a template or change an answer.
- The chat assistant gets a tool for this, `propose_agent`, that only proposes: it builds and adds nothing. It adds no new grant kind, so it never needs a permission of its own; a cautious approval setting, or a rule for that exact tool, can still ask first. It refuses a draft holding an answer the assistant marks as partly yours, too vague or assumed, or quotes with fewer than three words, including turning on Mail, so the assistant asks you instead. The app also checks that each answer quotes at least three of your own words from one of your messages before it lets you build; that shows the words are yours, not that the answer means what you said, so the card shows every answer. It is offered only in a personal chat with Parslee Core, and only from a host that shows the draft card: `agents.chat` takes a new `shows_agent_drafts` flag, which the macOS app sends and iOS, Android and Windows do not. It is read every turn: a turn without it gets no tool, no mention of standing agents, and a refusal if the assistant calls the tool anyway. Once the agent is added it runs on that schedule on its own when CAR can read and install the schedule (“only when I ask” never does), and Add shows a note otherwise; turning the agent off stops it.
- `agents.chat` approval events: the built-in assistant's `approval_pending` now carries `call_id`. For a gated tool call it is the id that call's `tool_call` carries, so a host can tell which call an approval is about; a goal check's approval, or one for a call inside a delegated child, carries an id no `tool_call` event names. CarHost uses it to tie an approval to its draft card, and on a card whose check never answered, or whose showing was declined or ended, **Ask for the draft again** adds the request to that chat's message box after anything already typed, rather than opening a new chat.

- The README, `llms.txt` and the website's title and hero now describe CAR as the runtime a business runs its agents on, and no longer list the built-in assistant (`car do`) as a reason to adopt CAR; it stays documented in ASSISTANT.md.

- Calendar events now carry `external_id`, the calendar server's identity for the item: `EKCalendarItem.calendarItemExternalIdentifier` from EventKit (on `calendarEvents`/`calendar.events` and on the `event` that `calendarCreateEvent`/`calendarUpdateEvent` and `calendar.create_event`/`calendar.update_event` return), and `iCalUId` from the Microsoft Graph backend. The existing `id` is scoped to one EventKit store, so the same meeting accepted on two accounts arrived as two unrelated events; `external_id` is what to compare. It is not unique on its own: every occurrence of a recurring EventKit event shares it, so key on `(external_id, start)`. EventKit can omit it, or report a value that later changes, for an event that has not yet synced to its server. The field is absent rather than `null` when unknown, and older clients ignore it. (car-releases#101)

- `bench-consolidate.py` no longer lets a contended speed calibration hold a model's catalog speed below what its own tasks decoded at. Calibrations are cached per model and machine, so one taken while the machine was busy carried into every later run: gpt-oss-20b's 42.2 tok/s calibration rode along on two runs that decoded at 62 and 74, and the catalog row had to be hand-pinned to 77.6 after every consolidation. Each calibrated run now counts the faster of its calibration and its task decode rate, because contention only ever slows a measurement. gpt-oss-20b, re-measured on an idle machine, comes out at 74.3 tok/s from the data alone (car-judged 0.545 over 3 runs). Four remote rows rise slightly for the same reason (claude-opus-4-8 83.4 → 93.9, gpt-4.1-mini 119.2 → 148.3, gpt-5.4-mini 198.9 → 220.5), as does qwen3.5-9b (50.1 → 51.3).

- `car claude` no longer starts a session without its collaboration tools. When the CAR plugin is disabled in Claude Code it is enabled for that session only (your own setting is unchanged); when it is not installed, `car claude` says how to install it. Against a daemon older than the CLI, it starts without the methods that daemon does not know (the org inbox) instead of failing to enroll.

- Starting `car claude` no longer deletes a registered agent just because its id starts with `claude-session-`. The startup sweep now removes only session agents a `car claude` launcher enrolled (their spec carries `CAR_LAUNCHER_PID`) that nothing holds (no live launcher or `claude`, no attached connection, no supervisor running it), and only the files a launcher wrote: the files of an agent it just removed, and credentials that name their own agent once no agent claims them. Another agent's `run/<id>.pid` lock, the supervisor's `run/<id>.supervisor.pid`, and any other file under the prefix are left alone. Every agent `car claude` has enrolled since it shipped carries the marker, so its sessions are still cleaned up; a session agent without it is never removed by guess, and `car uninstall <id>` removes one by hand (car#2789).

- The `car do` assistant shell can no longer reach the user-session services that run a process or read a file outside its sandbox, so `systemd-run --user cat ~/.car/auth-token` no longer reads CAR's token. On Linux it hides the systemd user manager (`/run/user/<uid>/systemd`), the session bus (`/run/user/<uid>/bus`, the socket `DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS` names, and `~/.dbus`), rootless Docker and Podman sockets (including `DOCKER_HOST`, `CONTAINER_HOST` and `/var/run/docker.sock`), the Docker Desktop, OrbStack, Colima, Rancher Desktop, Lima and Podman machine sockets under HOME, and tmux, screen, zellij and WezTerm multiplexer sockets. A socket behind a symbolic link is hidden at its target too, and a mask that cannot be laid refuses the command. Where Landlock's abstract-socket scope applies (Linux 6.12 or later with CAR's binary), the shell runs under it, which also cuts X11's abstract socket (X11 clients fall back to its path socket). Every command is refused when the session bus is an abstract socket and the scope cannot apply, or when the bus is on TCP. gpg-agent's `gpg-agent.conf`, `scdaemon.conf` and `common.conf` are read-only to the shell. The shell no longer inherits CAR's open descriptors. On macOS it cannot connect to the same sockets (and `$TMPDIR/podman`, Podman 5's machine API socket directory), move or hard-link them, rename gpg's home, or write those gpg files, and it can neither read nor write screen's directories (also `$TMPDIR/.screen`, where stock `/usr/bin/screen` keeps its sessions as named pipes, which no socket rule covers); other files in masked directories, such as `~/.lima` keys, stay readable on macOS (launchd already refused jobs from the sandboxed shell). What stops working in the `car do` shell and in mandatory gate checks: `systemctl --user` and `systemd-run --user`, session-bus clients, rootless and desktop Docker and Podman, and attaching to tmux, screen, zellij or WezTerm. ssh-agent and gpg-agent stay reachable, so `git push` over ssh and signed commits keep working, and a prompt on `/dev/tty` still reads its answer. This is best-effort containment of a cooperative agent, not confinement. A shell that can write HOME can still move a masked socket out of reach by renaming a directory above it (HOME itself on macOS, where HOME's parent is user-writable; on Linux any masked directory, from its next command), leave code that runs later outside the sandbox (systemd user units, shell rc files, autostart entries, `~/Library/LaunchAgents`, a crontab), and reach the services it keeps that run commands: the terminal (`TIOCSTI` where the kernel allows it), X11 and Wayland, macOS `open`, Apple Events, `osascript` and unrestricted mach/XPC services such as `shortcuts run`, Emacs and VS Code servers, window-manager control sockets, the accessibility bus, and a localhost sshd with readable keys.

- CarHost shows a text-message approval from an agent as the message itself: the recipient, the words that would be sent, and the email it answers (sender, subject, received time, account). Before, a Milo `message_send` approval rendered as a JSON dump cut off after ten lines, and because its keys were sorted the cut came before the message text, so the card gave no way to judge what was being approved. When the agent gives a reason for the text, it appears first, as Why. `email_reply` approvals render as an email (From, To, Cc, Subject, Body), and `human_request` approvals show the question, its context, the options, and what Approve answers, since Approve and Deny are the only buttons. Approvals the host has no layout for, and the parameters of a Chat tool call, no longer render as JSON on any approval card: each field is a labelled row (a nested object's fields read `Source email · Sender`, a list of plain values is one row), long text gets its own block, Milo's internal approval id is not shown, and past 40 rows the card says how many more were left out. Agents opt in by setting `details.kind` on `host.request_approval`; the accepted shapes are documented under that method.

- CarHost: when a list fails to refresh, its rows from the last load stay on screen marked "Last known", with a note saying they couldn't be refreshed. Their live status is greyed out, and the actions on those rows are disabled until a load succeeds. Hovering a disabled control says why. Before, those actions stayed enabled: in Settings → Agents a click acted on old status, and saving "Process settings" wrote the old values over the agent's current settings. This applies to Settings → Agents (Start, Stop, Restart, Process settings, Remove), Settings → Connectors (Refresh, Remove), Settings → Secrets (delete), and the Build tab's agent list (Run, Enabled, Remove). A "Process settings" editor that is already open can't save while its list is stale, and a delete confirmation already open in Secrets closes. Secrets has a Reload button. Its OpenRouter key shows "Unknown" after a first load that fails, never "Not stored" or an old "Stored". A first load that fails no longer says the list is empty, and a slower refresh that finishes after a newer one no longer overwrites it.

- CarHost Build tab: a Run dialog that is already open when the agent list fails to refresh now says the list couldn't be refreshed, and its Run button is disabled. Before, it could still start the agent from the outdated row. The dialog has its own Refresh button, which keeps what you typed, and Run comes back once the list refreshes.
- CarHost: when two refreshes of a list overlap, an older one that finishes last no longer overwrites the newer one's outcome. A late failure no longer shows an error after a newer success, and a late success no longer clears a newer failure's error. This applies to the Build tab's agent list and to Settings → Agents, Connectors and Secrets, and in Secrets also to the "keychain not available" notice.

- CarHost Chat: when the agent picked for a chat is removed and a different agent is registered under the same id (another process, or an external CLI swapped for a supervised agent), Chat now clears the pick and says so above the message box, instead of sending the next message to the new agent. Each chat records the agent behind each id it talked to, so this is caught also when the change happened while CarHost was closed. The chat's last reply, recorded agent and roster no longer pick that id back by themselves until you pick the agent in that chat, and a queued message or a Send again goes only to the agent it was written for. A renamed agent, and Parslee Core after CAR repairs its command, stay the same agent.
- Declarative agents now carry a daemon-assigned `registration_id` on `declagents.list`, `declagents.get` and their `agents.list` rows. A new one is minted when an id is registered anew (including after `declagents.remove`), and it is kept across a rebuild, rename or `declagents.revert`, so CarHost can tell an agent removed and rebuilt from the same project apart from the one it replaced. A value sent by a caller is ignored; a spec saved before this gets one when it is next replaced; the content fingerprint is unchanged.

- CarHost Chat keeps what you wrote and haven't sent through a quit or a crash. Messages queued during a reply, and each chat's message-box draft (its text, images and other attached files), are saved in the chat's own file, with a copy of each attachment beside the chat's other attachments. After a relaunch the chat shows its queued messages as queued, held for you: they go after your next message, or use Edit to send one now; they never send on their own, since the reply they waited for is gone. The message box shows its draft as it was, and closing the Chat window keeps it too. Text typed with no chat open is kept in one unattached-draft file beside the chats and comes back the first time you open an empty New chat box (or have no chat open) after a relaunch, never into the chat that opens at launch; typing there never creates a chat (only Send does), and that file is cleared on Send, when the text is erased, and on Remove All; text in the no-chat box is dropped by a dashboard tab switch, as before. That case keeps text only: images or files attached with no chat open are not kept.

- CarHost Chat's completion receipt lists a turn's recorded software checks (local verification, CI / CD, deployment, health and the rest) beside its desktop actions. A turn that used desktop tools and also ran checks used to show only the desktop actions; its headline now names both counts. When the daemon left desktop actions out of the receipt (it lists at most 100), the headline says how many ("N actions shown (K more not shown), M of those verified") and the receipt adds a line counting them, instead of reading as complete.
- Forking or duplicating a chat names an image whose saved file is already gone ("The copy is missing …"), and Export / Export All count it among the images whose Markdown links point at nothing. Both used to skip such an image without a word.
- Onboarding's About-you page treats a name or role of only spaces as blank, so when your saved profile loads late it fills those fields instead of keeping the spaces, and Save no longer erases the stored name or role.
- After a failed write in the chat rail (a pinned-order change, a rename, a pin or archive), CarHost rewrites the chats that edit touched back to what the rail shows at once, and again when it quits for any it couldn't rewrite yet, and says the change wasn't saved. A pinned-order change no longer stays half applied on disk after a normal quit and relaunch; a crash between the writes can still leave one chat with the new order.
- When no model list has loaded yet on CarHost's connection to CAR, a message with images to a chosen model (a Parslee model included) is still sent, and Chat says so: "Couldn't check whether this model reads images: the model list didn't load. They're sent anyway; pick Automatic if the reply can't see them." The warning shows above the message box and after Send, Send again, Regenerate and Edit & Resend. A model list refresh that fails after one worked keeps what the last one said about which models read images, so a model known not to read them still gets "This model can't read images" before Send. Automatic, an agent with its own model, and messages without images are not affected.
