- An agent card's Run now that was still running when CarHost quit now says how it ended after a relaunch, read from Work (a run CarHost was waiting on reads as not finished), and offers Run now again if the run never reached Work. It used to say only that the example was sent.

- A development CarHost no longer stays on "starting" forever when the dev-binary installer ignores SIGTERM at its 90-second bound: the installer and everything it started get SIGTERM, then SIGKILL 2 seconds later, and the timeout is reported as before with the previous copy running. No child of a killed refresh is left holding the install lock, and an installer that exits while a child still holds its output no longer holds startup either.

- CarHost's file-drop and paste tests no longer fail on a loaded machine: a test whose outcome is a time limit now passes that limit by hand and checks the limit that was armed, rather than racing the clock (#2827). The app behaves as before.

- CarHost: when CarHost attaches to a car-server daemon that another CarHost started and that is shutting down, it waits a fixed window and starts its own daemon if that daemon exits within the window. On a heavily loaded machine, a check that began inside the window could finish after it, so a daemon that exited after the window still brought CarHost's own daemon up. CarHost now checks the window again before looking: past the window it stays attached to that daemon, as documented.

- CarHost Chat says a message's images went unchecked ("Couldn't check whether this model reads images: … They're sent anyway") or that it was sent without an image or file only once CAR has taken the message. Before, Chat showed these notes as the message started going out, so a message CAR refused, one that never left CarHost, one stopped while its files were still being read, or one whose connection dropped before CAR answered could still say its images were sent. Those now say nothing about their images. When CAR couldn't confirm a message started but later confirms it, or its reply starts arriving after all, the note appears then; a late refusal or error still shows none. Sending images works as before. Fixes #3617.

- A daemon CAR starts for you now binds the loopback address the configured endpoint names. With `CAR_DAEMON_URL=ws://[::1]:<port>` and no daemon running, CarHost, the CLI's daemon auto-start, and `car-host --start-server` used to start `car-server` on `127.0.0.1`, so the client could not reach the daemon it had just launched; they now pass `--host ::1`. `car-server --host ::1` also hands supervised agents a valid `CAR_DAEMON_URL` (`ws://[::1]:<port>`) instead of an unparseable `ws://::1:<port>`.

- CarHost Settings → General: turning on Launch at login no longer shows a silent "Off" when macOS still reports the old not-registered state right after the request. CarHost keeps re-reading the status (about 1.5 seconds at most). If macOS still reports no login item after that and the request did not fail, the row says macOS hasn't confirmed CAR's login item yet, and it shows On once macOS publishes it; only a request that failed says to turn it on again or reinstall CAR. Turning it off and landing on not registered still reads "Off".

- A CarHost chat reply that went quiet while another chat was on screen no longer loses what it streams when it carries on after CarHost stopped waiting for it. Everything that arrives in the 15 minutes CarHost keeps listening, text and tool steps alike, is saved to the chat as it arrives, so switching back, coming back after those 15 minutes or relaunching CarHost shows it.
- Editing a message whose reply has an outcome CarHost can't tell now cancels that first attempt before the edited message goes out, as Send again already did. Editing or regenerating over a reply that went quiet (CarHost stopped waiting for it, and it may still be running) cancels that reply's turn first too. Only the new message's reply runs and is shown.
- Text in a CarHost chat reply can be selected across paragraphs and lists in one drag, and ⌘C copies the selection as plain text. Headings, tables, quotes and code blocks still start a new selection. Within such a run, a list item that wraps continues under its bullet or number rather than under the item's first word, an ordered list's numbers are no longer monospaced, and VoiceOver reads the run as one element, list markers included.
- In Build an agent, the questions, the answers you already gave and the review list can be selected and copied.

- CarHost: a row another Chat window saves while a reply is being regenerated or sent again now reaches the chat file at once, after the reply being replaced, instead of living only in memory until the new reply is in (a quit or crash in between lost it). A Send again or Edit & Resend refused before it started likewise keeps rows another window added meanwhile, instead of dropping them with the attempt.

- CarHost (macOS): relaunching CarHost right after a force quit no longer leaves chat offline. The previous CarHost's daemon can hold the port for about 15 s while it shuts down; CarHost used to attach to it and then stay daemon-less once it exited, until Settings → Daemon → Restart daemon or another relaunch. When the daemon on the port is a CarHost-spawned one whose CarHost is gone (it carries `--exit-with-parent` and that process has exited), or a legacy LaunchAgent CarHost just retired, CarHost now checks the port every second for up to 30 s and starts its own daemon once it is free; if it is still held after that, CarHost stays attached as before. A daemon you started yourself (`cargo run -p car-server`), or one belonging to another running CarHost, is still left alone.

- CarHost now retires a loaded legacy `ai.parslee.car.daemon` or `ai.parslee.car-server` LaunchAgent that names CarHost's port even when launchd is between restarts of its server: it reports no process, or only the job's own `/usr/bin/env` launch chain before car-server starts. Before, a wrapper-based legacy job caught in that gap was not recognized, so CarHost started its own daemon while launchd stayed ready to restart the old one on the same port. A job under those labels that is running some other program while CarHost checks it is still left alone.

- CarHost (macOS): an agent's page in **Scheduled** now has **Pause** and **Resume**, and **Run now**. Pause stops only the schedule; Run now and chat still work while it is paused, and times that pass while it is paused aren't listed as missed after Resume. Run now opens a short instruction you can edit before it is sent ("This is a run you started by hand. Do the work you are set up to do now, covering what is new since your last run, and report what you did."), and the result is saved in **Work**, with **See in Work** on the page when it ends. Each time listed as missed (the latest 20) has its own **Run now**, whose instruction names that time (in English, with its date, year and zone; for a schedule that keeps a fixed zone's clock, on that clock, with the Mac's time beside it, dated when that is another day). CAR's runtime records the run against the time: the time is no longer counted or listed as missed, and its history shows it once, as the run by hand, also when the runtime had recorded the time as missed. While it runs, and until the page has been read again, that time shows as running and its Run now can't be pressed again; a time that isn't missed any more, or was already run by hand, is refused, and the runtime's words are shown on the page. **Retry as new work** in Work names the same missed time again, so the runtime decides whether it may still run for it. A plain Run now is not added to the schedule's history. A schedule reported as not set up on this Mac can still have its missed times run by hand, as the runtime accepts; a paused schedule lists no missed times. Pause, Resume and Run now are unavailable while CAR isn't connected, until the page has been read on this visit and connection, when that read failed, after the runtime refuses this connection, and while the agent is off; the page says why. When the runtime no longer has a schedule for the agent, the page says so and offers none of them. If Run now is pressed in its sheet after the page's actions closed, or for a time that is no longer listed as missed, nothing is sent and the page says why. After each action the page is read again, so it shows what the runtime now has.

- CarHost (macOS): an agent's page in **Scheduled** now has **Change schedule…**, and the Scheduled list has **Schedule an agent…** for an agent that is on and runs only when asked. Both open the same sheet: how often (every so many minutes or hours, every day, weekdays, weekends, chosen days, or **Only when I ask**), the time of day, and the clock, either **This Mac's time** (recommended) or **a fixed time zone** you pick, which keeps that zone's clock and its daylight-saving changes wherever the Mac is. A new schedule starts at weekdays at 9:00 on this Mac's time; a change starts from the schedule as it is. You can also type the schedule in words ("weekdays at 8:30 ET", "every 2 hours"): CAR reads it once you stop typing and fills in the choices, switching the clock to a zone you named, or shows its reason, word for word, when it can't read it. Before you save, the sheet shows CAR's own reading of the schedule, and for an interval that doesn't line up with the clock (such as every 7 minutes, every 90 minutes or every 5 hours) it says that no times are listed as Missed for it. Save opens only once CAR has checked exactly what it would send; when CAR can't be asked, the sheet says it couldn't check (never that CAR didn't understand) and checks again when CAR is back, on **Retry**, or on Save. If the agent's page reads a different schedule while the sheet is open, the sheet says what it is now and saves nothing until you start from it or choose to replace it. Save also reads the schedule again just before sending, and sends nothing if it was changed, paused or resumed since the sheet last saw it; from **Schedule an agent…** that read checks the agent is still on and still has no schedule. **Cancel** stops a save that is still on those reads; once the change itself is on its way, Cancel is off until it is in and the page (or the list) has been read again. Save also sends nothing to an agent turned off since the page last read it. Only when a save changes the schedule's times does the sheet say it starts the schedule afresh: times it missed before the change are no longer offered to run by hand, while runs already recorded, missed ones included, stay in its history. Saving the same times keeps the missed times. A schedule these choices can't show exactly is named in the sheet, with its own words in the typing field. A paused schedule stays paused after a change to other times (the sheet follows the page if it is paused or resumed elsewhere). **Only when I ask** removes the schedule: the agent leaves Scheduled, its page says so, and Run now and chat stay on the agent's page. Change schedule is unavailable whenever Pause, Resume and Run now are (CAR not connected, the page not yet read on this visit and connection, a failed read, a refused connection, the agent off) and while a Pause, Resume or change is on its way; if CAR's runtime refuses the change, the sheet stays open with its words. With no agents at all, the empty Scheduled list offers **Build an agent**.

- CarHost (macOS): a **Scheduled** row sits in the sidebar right below Home and above Tools, always there and never pinned or unpinned. It lists every agent that runs on a schedule, with the schedule in words, its next run (an estimate for an interval that doesn't line up with the clock, such as every 7 minutes), how its last run went, how many scheduled times it missed (CAR reports the latest 20, shown as 20+), and whether it is paused, off, or saved but not set up to run on its own, with CAR's reason. A schedule that keeps a named zone's clock says so, and its times are shown in the Mac's own time. Opening one shows its runs newest first: each run's status, the time it was for, when it started and finished, and how long it took; a run opens to show its output, error, the model that answered and any stand-in notice. The list shows the first 2 KB of a run's output and of its error; when either was cut, the run says so and **Show in Finder** shows its record file, which holds the whole run while it is among the agent's latest 200 (when the registry's path isn't absolute or can't be read right now, the run says where the record is kept instead of offering the button, and it says the same when the file can't be found). A run is never read in full over the connection. Missed times are rows of their own, saying the agent didn't run then, for example because the Mac was asleep, off or signed out. The screen reads `declagents.runs` one agent at a time, with no records for the list and 25 previews per page of history, so no reply carries every agent's history. It reads when the screen opens and every 30 seconds while it is on screen, an open agent's page every 5 seconds while one of its runs is running, and at once when the connection comes back or the window can be seen again. It starts no read while another screen is shown, while its window is minimized, hidden or covered, while CAR isn't connected, or after the runtime refuses this connection the host-only method (until the connection comes back or the screen is opened again); a read under way when the screen is left or the window is hidden stops before its next request, whatever started it. Changing a schedule from this screen comes later.
- CarHost's connection to CAR's runtime now takes in messages up to 16 MiB, up from URLSessionWebSocketTask's 1 MiB default. A larger message ends the whole connection, failing every pending request and cutting off running chat turns, so the limit is a backstop for replies with no size bound of their own.

- CarHost (macOS): finished scheduled runs are also saved in **Work**. When you open Work, it reads each of your agents' 25 most recent run records (scheduled, missed and by-hand alike), so a run that finished while CAR was closed is there if its record is among its agent's newest 25; older ones reach Work when the agent's page in **Scheduled** shows them (**Show older runs**). Only runs that finished, done or failed, are saved; a run still going and a missed time (nothing ran) are not. A missed time run by hand is not saved either: one started from CarHost already has a Work item from its **Run now**, and if CAR lost that run's reply or was quit before it came, that item says its outcome is unknown while the run's record, with how it ended, stays in the agent's history in Scheduled; one sent from another client has no Work item and stays only in that history. Each run is saved once, however many windows or launches find it. A saved run is titled with the time it was scheduled for ("Scheduled run for Tue, Oct 13, 8:30 AM") and shows no "Original request", since the title is not what the agent was sent; it keeps its status (done, no output, or failed with its error and any output it made), the model that answered and any stand-in notice, and its start and finish times. A saved run that failed or made no output counts like any other Work in the agent's status on Home and Agents. When CAR cut a run's output or error to its first 2 KB, Work says CAR lists only that much and where the whole run is kept, never calls it "No output" or reason-less from a blank first 2 KB, offers **Show in Finder** for its record file (and says so when the file can't be shown), and its copy button says it copies the part of the output Work saved, or the status shown when Work saved no output. Work's read asks nothing while its window is minimized, hidden or covered, while CAR isn't connected, or after the runtime refuses it (it reads once when that changes, during the same visit), and stops before its next request when you leave Work. The Scheduled screen's own reads save the finished runs they show, with no request of their own, and its history footer now adds "Finished scheduled runs are also saved in Work when Work or this page reads them." **Retry as new work** on a failed scheduled run sends Run now's instruction rather than the run's title. On work you ran by hand for a missed time, Retry sends the same words for the same time again and CAR's rule of one by-hand run per missed time decides: an attempt that never reached CAR runs for that time, and one CAR already took is refused in its words. Once CAR has refused that time (already run by hand, no longer missed, or its schedule paused or cleared), the button is **Run now (not for that time)**, which sends Run now's plain instruction with no time.

- CarHost Settings now shows what is actually running. Settings → Daemon names the daemon CarHost is using (its bundled car-server as a separate process, a launch agent, another car-server it does not supervise, an external `CAR_DAEMON_URL` endpoint, or none) with its port, instead of always saying "Embedded (in-process)", and shows WS auth and the approval gate as on only for the daemon CarHost started. Its old "Runtime status" section is now "In-app runtime", and the menu bar's first line says "In-app runtime: …", because both report the runtime inside CarHost, not the daemon. Settings → General shows "Off" for launch at login when it was never turned on, and a plain sentence when macOS did not register CAR, instead of "Service not registered (build issue?)". Settings → Approval Channels no longer says "Nothing leaves your Mac" for iMessage: it says what the chat reads stays on the Mac with a local chat model and goes to the provider with a cloud model (only the line for the selected chat model when Settings knows it), and that other agents with Messages access use their own models. Onboarding's "Private by default" is now "Local or cloud, your choice", and its approval line says risky actions ask for approval by default.

- macOS (CarHost): setup no longer requires a Parslee account. Setup finishes with any working way to run a model: Parslee sign-in (still shown first and recommended), a local model that has passed CAR's load check, or your own provider key added through the Models library's Add key… (a key stored only in Settings → Secrets does not count as a setup route). Connecting only Claude Code does not finish setup, since it cannot answer Parslee Core. A signed-out first run that finishes on a local model or a stored key gets a model from that route selected for CAR Chat, is not shown setup again at launch, and the Model step updates as soon as you come back from setting a route up elsewhere. Chat's sign-in card now appears when you are signed out with no usable model chosen (it previously never appeared, because Chat never loaded the signed-out state it checked).

- `auth.status` now returns `generation`, the durable auth-state generation read when the check began (the counter `auth.authority_hint` publishes; it is not the read generation `auth.credential.event` carries, and it is omitted while that hint is `unknown`). CarHost drops an answer older than a signed-out hint it has already applied, so a sign-out that lands while Verify is checking is no longer undone by Verify's late "signed in" answer.
- CarHost no longer stops an unrelated program that a legacy CAR launchd job's `/usr/bin/env` happens to run on CAR's port. While the server is between restarts, a live process now counts as the job's own launch chain only when it is env itself or the wrapper the job declares, with a `car-server` beside it.
- A schedule change CAR accepted but could not read back is no longer reported as saved. The sheet stays open and says the schedule shown may be out of date.
- Deleting a code task whose conversation CAR refused to close (a reply that just started, an internal failure) now keeps the task, as an unreachable daemon already did. Only a conversation that is already closed lets the deletion go ahead.
- The "not independently checked" note on an agent's page is read again after a failed read, including when CAR reconnects, instead of staying hidden until the agent's registration changes.
- An agent's approval mode stays locked while one of its replies may still be running, including a reply the stall watchdog has written off, or whose connection dropped, while CAR may still be running it; the lock ends when that reply's outcome is known or its linger ends. Settings → Agent Permissions applies the same lock as the message box, and says which agent is replying. A reply that starts while Settings or the message box is sending an approval change waits until that change is applied. That holds within this CarHost: a change made from another client, or a run a schedule starts, isn't held, because CAR's runtime has no turn-aware guard of its own.

- CarHost Chat: a saved agent's reply that Parslee answered because the agent's own model wasn't available now says so beside the reply ("Answered by parslee/advisor · Ran on Parslee because <model> isn't available."), as Run now and Scheduled already did, including on a reply that then fails. CarHost for iPhone shows the same notice in its chat.
- CarHost Edit agent shows one Model section instead of three. It opens on the model the agent was built with, and that model's row in the picker is marked "(as built)"; Automatic (the default for a new agent) is still offered, and says it keeps running on the built-with model. What it runs on is shown beneath, and the advice on other ways to run the work is folded under "Other ways CAR could run this work". Every model and option offered before is still there.
- An in-chat agent build card reopened after CarHost quit mid-build no longer waits for Try again once CAR is connected: it checks the build again by itself and says how it ended. A build that ended because CAR quit or restarted under it now reads "CAR quit or restarted while this was building, so nothing was added." instead of "didn't finish", whether the card was following the build when it ended or finds out after a relaunch.
- Reopening setup after a relaunch shows a Parslee sign-in that setup already completed as done ("Signed in earlier"), rather than "Ready to verify", as long as the saved credential is still there, including on a Mac set up before this release. If a Verify then fails, the step shows as to-do again. CAR still reads the Keychain only when you choose Verify or something needs the sign-in.
- CarHost for iPhone no longer says an approval was approved or denied, or removes it, when the daemon forwarded the answer to the session that asked and that session hasn't decided yet. The approval queue says "Answer sent. Waiting for the session that asked to decide.", an answer sent while offline says it is queued, and the approval stays listed until it is resolved.

- CarHost: pressing Stop (or Esc) while Parslee Core waits on an approval card nobody approved now records the turn's Work item as **Stopped**, and the agent no longer shows **Needs attention** / **Outcome unknown** for it, because nothing ran. CarHost takes this from the daemon's answer to the cancel, never from the turn's frames: only `cancelled: true` with `dispatched: false` counts, and any other answer, a missing field, or no answer still reads Outcome unknown. Deleting the chat while it waits does the same. A turn of any agent stopped before its request went out is Stopped too. A Work status written by a newer CarHost now reads as Outcome unknown instead of hiding the row.
- `agents.chat.cancel` now returns `dispatched: bool` beside `cancelled` when the bundled assistant answers it: whether any call of the turn (a tool call, a delegated child's call or a goal check) passed the point where it runs. The daemon asks the assistant with an `agent.chat.cancel` request instead of a notification; every other agent still gets the notification, and its reply carries no `dispatched`, which hosts must read as unknown. The daemon also stops an in-daemon turn before removing its route and closing its approvals, so an approval answered from another surface at that moment is refused rather than run.

- CarHost (macOS): an error reply from the daemon that no request is waiting for no longer closes CarHost's daemon connection. Before, an error that arrived after CarHost had already timed out the request, or the daemon's parse-error reply (which carries `"id": null`), disconnected CarHost, and every chat turn running on that connection was lost, because the daemon sends a turn's frames only to the connection that started it. Such a reply is now logged and dropped, the same way an unmatched successful reply already was. A rejected `host.subscribe` still ends the connection.

- CarHost Chat: clicking a chat that is waiting on an approval, while another chat is shown, no longer crashes CarHost. The chat opens at its waiting card, or Approvals opens when the card is no longer in the chat, as before. Fixes #3307.

- A nightly's Linux and Windows catch-up lanes now count as attached only once their wheel (manylinux x86_64, win_amd64) is on the release, the same rule `car-release status`, promotion admission and `scripts/verify-release.sh` all read, so a nightly can no longer pass admission and then fail the stable promotion's wheel relabel after the tag is pushed. The Linux catch-up upload step fails, and writes no `catch-up-linux.txt`, when the x86_64 wheel is missing, and the verifier no longer suggests a macOS backfill when only Linux, Windows, pip or npm items are missing.

- A device certificate that cannot be renewed no longer fails quietly. `car auth status` shows why next to the certificate, with the fix (for an expired login, `car auth login --add` as that email). `car lattice directory` names the certificate problem instead of an unrelated org-scope error, and lists held orgs it had to leave out under `unavailable`. Signing a login in again now retries its certificate at once instead of waiting out a back-off that only a daemon restart cleared. A first failed issuance is logged as a warning.

- CarHost Chat: a saved attachment record can no longer make Chat read, send or copy a file outside that chat's attachments folder. Before, a chat file edited by hand or by another program could name a `..` path or a symlink, and Regenerate, Edit & Resend or an external CLI turn then put that file's text in the message, or handed over its path. A record is now read only when it names one plain file in its own chat's folder, opened without following a link. A resend goes without any other, and the chat says so: "Sent without “notes.txt”: its saved copy isn't a file in this chat's attachments folder, so it was not read." Fork, duplicate and export do not copy such a file, and count it among the files they could not copy. The message bubble shows no preview for it and opens nothing. Real saved files are read, sent and copied as before. Fixes #2808.
- CarHost Chat: closing a Chat window while it is saving a message's files no longer leaves a staged or saved copy behind when that save fails or finishes too late. Once the save ends, what it left is removed, also for a save that ran past its time limit and whose window closed before its result came in. A window that stays open keeps the copy, so Send can try again.

- Chat: asking Parslee Core to change an agent you added earlier in the same chat now shows a change to that agent ("Change to …", with What changes before Build it) instead of a new draft by the same name. The schedule, folder and model you chose stay as the agent has them unless you ask to change them. A separate agent still works; it needs a name of its own.

- CarHost Chat keeps the conversation when a chat moves to a new daemon session. A chat moves to a new session after Stop, Regenerate, Edit and resend, a turn that ends in an error, a sign-in card, a stall or a failed send, a change to its verified goal, quitting the app while a reply runs, a turn to another agent, and a send from a screen whose copy of the chat is out of date. The first message under the new session now carries the chat's earlier messages and answers, so the agent still sees them: Regenerate on "now make it half as long" shortens the sentence again instead of answering as if the chat had just started. Attached files are not carried: a message with attachments goes with a note naming them, and after a switch of agents, another agent's answers are marked as that agent's. A window whose copy of the chat is out of date carries the messages another window saved since. Only the newest 200 messages, up to 256 KiB of text, are carried; when older ones are left out, the agent is told how many. `agents.chat` takes this as new optional `history` (`[{ role: "user" | "assistant", content }]`, oldest first) and `history_omitted` (older messages the host left out) fields, honored only from a host-management session and ignored unread from any other caller, and forwards them to the agent's `agent.chat` handler with `history_omitted` adding what the daemon left out. The built-in assistant seeds a new session's thread from it and fits it to the model's context window like any other turn; a session that already has a thread ignores it.

- CarHost Chat (macOS): the "Jump to latest" pill no longer covers the newest lines of the transcript. While it shows, the transcript ends above it, so a draft card's notes and the ends of questions stay readable. The composer's character count, shown past 2,000 characters, likewise gets its own line instead of sitting on the end of the last typed line, and Coder's "start outcome unknown" banner keeps its "Check records" and "Delete task…" buttons clear of the browser globe button.

- CarHost Chat: once the chat's latest agent draft card knows its build has ended, the work panel's Progress section says how, under the assistant's task list and in the card's own words, for example "Added to your agents" or "This build was stopped, so nothing was added." Before, the panel showed only the task list, so a step such as "Check the built agent" stayed open after the build had stopped or the agent had been added. The task list itself stays as the assistant wrote it.

- CarHost Chat: reopening the Chat window (or opening a second Chat screen) while a message's files are still saving no longer deletes them. The clean-up that removes attachment files no message names now skips a chat whose files are still saving, including a save past its time limit whose result hasn't landed, and keeps every copy a send in progress saved and every copy a composer holds for its next Send. If a saved copy is gone anyway (removed outside CAR), the message no longer goes without it: an image is saved again by that Send, and any other file stops the send, named to attach again.

- Each `agents.chat` turn sent to an attached agent now has a daemon-minted `turn_id`, sent with `agent.chat` and returned in the `agents.chat` ack. An agent that echoes it on its `agent.chat.event` frames has them matched to that turn: when the host's next turn on the same `session_id` replaces a running one, the replaced turn's late terminal frame no longer ends the newer turn's route or reaches its host, and the newer turn's reply arrives. The replaced turn runs to completion; of its frames, only a card it raises after it was replaced (and that card's close) still reaches the host, so it can be answered. `car do --serve` echoes it; the Node and Python bindings send it with the new `chatTurnEvent` / `chat_turn_event`, and their cancelled event for a turn stopped while queued carries it. An agent's approval cards are scoped by turn the same way: two turns' cards under one approval id stay two cards, a close naming a turn closes only that turn's card, and `agent.chat.approve` carries the card's `turn_id`. Agents that send no `turn_id` are matched by `session_id`, as before (#2668).

- Parslee Core in Chat now works in the conversation's folder on the local host (a Parslee Core running in a Docker sandbox ignores the folder, because the container holds no host folders). A relative `write_file`, `shell` command or generated image used to resolve against the daemon's working directory (`/` on a Mac) and fail on the read-only system volume. CarHost now sends the folder with every Parslee Core turn as the new optional `agents.chat` `cwd`: the conversation's own folder, or else `~/Documents/Parslee/<conversation name>`, chosen at the conversation's first turn and kept with it, so a rename never splits its files and two chats with the same name get separate folders. Each such turn tells the model where the conversation's files go. Generated images, audio and recordings are reported by their full path, so the Outputs panel opens them. The folder is made only when something is first written there. It never widens what a tool may touch: absolute paths and approvals behave as before, and the daemon honors `cwd` only from the person's own host.

- Coder checks now resolve tools in your login PATH's order. The protected check PATH used to put root-owned system directories ahead of admitted Homebrew and nvm directories, so `python3` in a check was macOS's Xcode shim even when your shell runs Homebrew's, with your packages, pytest included. Admitted entries now keep their login-PATH position. Admission is unchanged: Homebrew and nvm still require enforced write confinement and no overlap with a writable root, and a default-alias nvm version still goes ahead of Homebrew so a Homebrew `node` cannot shadow it.
- The `vllm-mlx/gemma-4-26b-a4b-it` catalog row now declares the `code` capability, so CAR's best-measured local model (car-judged 0.658) is eligible for local code routing and as the unattended self-healing coder.

- Added `scripts/lab/check-release-actors.sh <since-iso>`, a read-only probe that lists every release author, asset uploader, build workflow actor, crates.io publisher and npm publisher since a date and exits non-zero naming any person.

- Fixed coder checks being allowed to write outside the worktree through a here-document or, on Windows, an apostrophe. A `cd` written inside a here-doc body no longer makes a later relative write look as if it lands in the worktree; the body is judged as a program of its own, since a shell may run it. A here-doc body with an unquoted delimiter that holds `$(…)` or a backtick substitution is refused (quote the delimiter, `<<'EOF'`), and so is a here-doc missing its delimiter line or whose delimiter holds `$`, a backtick or a parenthesis. A body line ending in `\r` no longer ends a body whose delimiter has none. On Windows, where cmd has no here-docs and does not quote with apostrophes, every line stays a command and a `>` between apostrophes is checked as the redirection it is; a Windows check also refuses any `%NAME%` or `!NAME!` reference other than the runtime's own roots and `%CD%`, because cmd expands it before it reads redirections. Protected-path discovery and the check-replay matcher likewise no longer let a here-doc `cd` re-root the files a check runs.
- A Windows check refuses a `set` of `%CD%` or a runtime-root variable, and reads `%NAME%` pairs left to right as cmd does, so a reference like `%_X%CD%` whose first pair is another variable is no longer missed.
- Behaviour change: Windows check commands can no longer use `%VARIABLE%` references (or `!VARIABLE!`) other than CAR's runtime folders (`%CAR_CHECK_TMPDIR%`, `%CAR_CHECK_EVIDENCE_DIR%`, `%CAR_HOME%`, `%CARGO_TARGET_DIR%`), `%CD%`, the OS paths `%SystemRoot%`, `%windir%` and `%SystemDrive%` (so `%SystemRoot%\System32\findstr.exe` still works), and `%INTERCEPTOR_GROUP%`, `%HOME%`, `%USERPROFILE%`, `%RUSTUP_HOME%` and `%CARGO_HOME%`; write the value out literally instead. cmd expands a variable before it reads redirections, so its value could carry one the inspector never saw.
- On Windows an apostrophe is read both as cmd reads it (ordinary text) and as a quote, since a program cmd starts (`bash -c '…'`, `sed '…'`) may quote with it; a check is refused if either reading refuses it (a governed-mode refusal from cmd's reading says to use double quotes or a script file), and protected-path discovery protects what either reading names as read, never a file the check writes.
- A here-doc inside a command substitution is refused in a coder check, and by the destructive-command guard, because its body can close the surrounding quotes early. On Windows a check holding `^` (cmd's escape character) or a `\"` inside double quotes (where cmd ends the string) is refused, since either can hide a `&`-chained command.
- A coder check also refuses a here-doc piped into a command that changes directory or starts a shell or interpreter (`cat <<'E' | (cd .. && sh)`; give the shell the here-doc directly, `sh <<'E'`), a here-doc body line ending in `\` under an unquoted delimiter (the shell joins it with the next line, a delimiter line included), and, outside Windows, any carriage return (save the command with Unix line endings).
- A failed `cd` no longer lets a later write be judged from the directory the `cd` named: a write is judged from every directory the shell may be in. Only a command chained with `&&` after a `cd` is judged from its target alone, so `cd ..; cd /worktree; echo x > f` is refused, and once a check holds `||` or a negated `cd`, a later `&&` no longer counts either. The refusal says to chain each `cd` to the commands after it with `&&`. The write check inspects command text only; it does not follow a script piped or redirected into a shell, a script file, or an inline `-c`/`-e` program.
- A coder check's write target or `cd` operand holding a positional or special shell parameter (`$1`, `${1}`, `$@`, `$*`, `$?`, `$$`, `$0`) is judged as an unknown variable, so `set -- ../e; echo x > "$1"` is refused. A check that names CDPATH, or runs `trap`, `shopt` or `setopt`, is refused, as is a `cd` or `pushd` with more than one operand or a directory-stack index (`pushd +1`, zsh's `cd -2` and `cd old new`), a `popd` with an operand, and `dirs` with one; each moves a later `cd` somewhere the write inspector does not follow.
- The CDPATH refusal applies where the variable is set (an assignment, a name operand such as `export`, `printf -v` or zsh's `set -A`, or a `${NAME:=…}`/`${NAME::=…}` expansion) and also sees quoted or escaped spellings (`CD''PATH`, `CD\PATH`), and a coder check is refused when it sets `DIRSTACK`, `BASHOPTS`, `SHELLOPTS` or a shell option that changes how `cd` works (`autocd`, `cdable_vars`, `chase_links`, `pushd_minus` and the like, through `set -o`, `bash -O`, `zsh -o` or zsh's `options` array), when it gives `export`, `declare`, `env`, `read` or `printf -v` a variable name built at run time, or when it runs `pushd -n`. `set -e`, `set -u`, `set -x` and `set -o pipefail` are unaffected. A check that only mentions one of these words, such as `cargo test cdpath` or `grep -r physical .`, still runs. A `cd` operand list now ends options at the first operand, so `cd sub -L` counts two operands and is refused.

- Fixed coder checks being allowed to write outside the worktree by putting a redirection before the destination. `cp a 2>/dev/null ../out`, `copy a 2>nul C:\out`, a redirection before the command itself (`2>/dev/null cp a ../out`, `2>/dev/null cd ..`), an input redirection or here-doc after the destination, `2>&1`, `&>`, `>|`, and a command substitution among the operands (`cp a $(true) ../out`) no longer hide the destination from the write check. The check also now reads past carriers (`env`, `command`, `timeout`, `nice`, `nohup`, `sudo`, `exec`, `builtin`, `cmd /c`), treats `cp`/`mv`/`install -t DIR`, `--target-directory`, or any abbreviation GNU accepts such as `--target` as the destination, ignores a comment's words, refuses any `case` word inside a command substitution, even as an argument, and likewise a comment or here-doc inside one or a substitution that is never closed, and checks bash's `>&file` as a write target. A quoted `>` (`grep '>' /etc/hosts`) is no longer taken for a redirection.
- The destructive-command check now finds its command past a leading redirection and inside a `( … )` subshell, and no longer treats a redirection target (`2> /dev/null rm -rf build`) as a path being removed. In governed host mode, the shell read check now sees a read past a leading redirection (`2>/dev/null cat ../x`) or after a process substitution (`cat <(true) ../x`).
- Coder checks now refuse, with a message naming the construct, what the write check cannot inspect: `eval`, an alias definition or `shopt -s expand_aliases`, a shell function definition, and a write verb behind `xargs` or `find -exec`/`-execdir`/`-ok`/`-okdir`. Read-only forms such as `xargs grep` and `find -exec cat` still run. A write target that is exactly `$(mktemp)` or `$(mktemp -d)` is now admitted under the check's scratch directory, by the same rule that already admits `cd "$(mktemp -d)"`, only in the top-level check shell; a nested `sh -c` does not get the scratch-root shim, so its mktemp writes are denied.

- macOS no longer asks to let CarHost "update or delete other applications" during a web search. The Chrome that CAR's browser tools launch used to start Google's updater as its own child process 19 seconds in, and macOS attributed the updater's rewrite of the Chrome app bundle to CarHost. Every CAR-launched Chrome on macOS (the browser drawer, an agent's browser, the visible sign-in window, `browser.run`, `car browse`, and the FFI browser sessions) now starts with `--disable-updater-scheduler`. This stops Chrome's scheduled updater only: opening Chrome's About / update page (`chrome://settings/help`) in a CAR-launched Chrome still runs an update check and is not covered. Chrome keeps updating through Google's background updater, which Chrome installs and repairs when you open it yourself. The switch is Google's, so it covers Google Chrome only. Microsoft Edge (which CAR launches when `msedge` is on `PATH`, or when no Chrome or Chromium is in `/Applications`) has its own updater switch, which CAR has not been able to verify, so CAR passes Edge no switch of its own and Edge is not covered. Neither is a browser other than Google Chrome named by the `CHROME` environment variable (Brave, Vivaldi, Arc). (#2146)

- Pull-request CI is faster. The heavy Linux jobs (`test`, `lint`, `capability-manifest`, `shared-process-test`, and the main-only `generated-files-main`) run on the 8-core runner. The CLI is built once per run, in `capability-manifest`; the `test` job no longer rebuilds it to repeat the same manifest check. Build caches are saved only from `main`, which is the only place a pull request can restore them from, so pull requests no longer spend minutes uploading caches nobody else can read. `scripts/build-capabilities.sh` now leaves the two tracked capability files untouched when their content is unchanged, so the next build does not recompile the crates that include them. `scripts/check-capability-roles.sh` accepts `--car <binary>` to check a prebuilt CLI.

- CI runs the full test suites once, in the merge queue, instead of on every pull request and again in the queue. Windows breaks are caught in the queue before they land instead of after merge. Pull requests run only the fast gates.

- Release CI: every write to the car-releases mirror (mirror upload, Windows catch-up, registry markers, promote-publish winget/docs/markers, the Windows-installer promotion) now uses a GitHub App token minted in that step by actions/create-github-app-token and scoped to contents:write on Parslee-ai/car-releases, instead of a person's PAT. the mirror waits are capped at 55 minutes so it stays inside the token's one-hour life.

- A context-bound `classify` or `generate` (one carrying a work context, such as a daemon session's) no longer sends the request to a configured preferred model that the context's own credential left unusable. When that context's credential fails, a preferred Parslee classifier or generation model is skipped and the request routes as if none were set, the way it already routed without a preference. When the context can serve the preferred model, and for a request without a work context, the preference still applies. `classify`'s smallest-local-model fallback now picks only among the models that request's routing snapshot lists, so a local model the snapshot left out (one not enabled for CAR) is no longer chosen over a downloaded one it does list (car#3155).

- `classify` with no local model downloaded no longer fails when the smallest local model is unavailable to it. A context-bound request whose routing snapshot leaves that model out, and an unbound request after that model was removed from CAR, now pick the smallest model they can use, which is downloaded on first use. A context-bound `generate` that falls back to its default model for the task's complexity likewise picks the first model its snapshot lists, starting from that default's own size and going larger, then smaller, instead of one the snapshot left out (car#3170).

- The Claude 5 generation (Opus 5, Sonnet 5, Fable 5) thinks when a request omits `thinking`, at its default effort, and pays for it out of `max_tokens`. CAR now reserves that default effort's reasoning on top of the caller's answer budget (16384 tokens at the default high; 8192 on Opus 5.5 and Haiku 5.5, which default to medium), the way it already does for OpenAI reasoning models. Before, Sonnet 5 returned empty answers at exactly its answer cap on 9 of 17 harder judged tasks. Claude 4.x and older get no reserve.

- The published CLI reference (`CLI.md`) now documents `car claude`, which shipped in 0.58.0 without a section there.

- `car coder-ab run`: an attempt the replay ledger cannot record (its lock or in-flight marker cannot be written) is no longer run with only a warning. The arm ends as an infrastructure failure that names the ledger, so every attempt that runs has its ledger entry. An attempt whose result the ledger cannot write is now reported as an infrastructure failure too, matching the `infra_failed` entry the next run's recovery writes for it, instead of as the result it reached.
- `car board`: refused conversation messages that come back out of order are restored in the order you sent them, both in the composer and in the text held for it while the composer is closed. The exit summary lists every session's unsent answer, guidance and revision, even when two of them have the same words.
- `car agent new` with piped input: a stop signal (SIGTERM or SIGHUP) that arrives in the same moment as the typed answer, or as a Ctrl-C, now wins, so the session is cancelled instead of the CLI exiting first and leaving it open.
- `car do --json`: when the run started on a substitute model and a later turn fell back again, the top-level `substituted_from` and `substitution_reason` name the startup substitution, the first one, rather than the later fallback. Each turn in `models_served` keeps its own attribution.

- Security: a daemon client can no longer run the built-in file tools (`read_file`, `write_file`, `edit_file`, `list_dir`, `find_files`, `grep_files`) on the daemon host by registering a tool with one of those names and answering the daemon's `tools.execute` call with the error text "unknown tool". Before this fix, any session, including a supervised agent's, could read and write host files this way (Parslee-ai/car#3104). Now a client tool that fails returns that failure, word for word, and nothing else runs. The same applies to an error message returned by an MCP connector server. The built-in file tools run in the daemon only for a session that turned them on through daemon-side setup: `session.bindSubstrate`, `session.bindSandbox`, or `agents.register_basics`. In-process `Runtime` users keep the old behaviour: a Rust tool executor that returns an error starting with "unknown tool" still hands the call to the built-in tools. An executor that implements `execute_classified` itself must now return `ToolFailure::unhandled(..)` to get that hand-off, because `ToolFailure::ordinary(..)` no longer triggers it, whatever its message says.
- Rust API: `car_ir::ToolFailure` gains a private field. Code outside the crate that builds a `ToolFailure` with a struct literal, or destructures one without `..`, no longer compiles; use `ToolFailure::ordinary`, `terminal` or `unhandled`. `PartialEq` now also compares the hidden flag, so `ToolFailure::unhandled(m)` is not equal to `ToolFailure::ordinary(m)`.
- `calculate` runs in the daemon for a session bound with `session.bindSandbox` or `session.bindSubstrate`, the same as after `agents.register_basics`. Both binds register it, but it used to be sent to the client.

- `car coder-ab`: capturing a delivered branch no longer loads every changed file into memory to scan it for approved credential values. A one-line edit to a multi-gigabyte text file reads only the file's first 8000 bytes to confirm it is text, and a binary file, along with the version it replaced, is counted as it streams. Capture memory now grows with the size of the delivered patch, not with the size of the files it touches; a large binary file that is added or rewritten still carries its whole content as a literal hunk in the patch, and that patch is held in memory. What the scan refuses is unchanged.

- `car coder-ab` no longer lets two task ids overwrite each other's evidence when they sanitize to the same name (`team/a`, `team?a`) or differ only in case on a case-folding filesystem (`Fix-Login`, `fix-login`). A per-ledger attempt directory, and a failed re-score's `rescore/<check>/` directory, keep the name as it is only when it uses `a-z 0-9 . _ -`, is at most 200 bytes, does not end in `.`, and is not empty, `.`, `..` or a Windows device name such as `con`. Any other name is sanitized, cut to 200 bytes, and gets `+` and the first 32 hex digits of the raw name's SHA-256 appended (`team_a+65f838a2fbff37d81a09242f44268f11/`, `Fix-Login+6381d1d08427c5392c1e121d880dba23/`). Lowercase ids keep their existing directory names, rows that already store paths into the bare sanitized directory keep resolving, and `scripts/check-replay-ledger-paths.sh` uses the same naming. Two case-fold ids starting at once no longer fail with "File exists" on the attempt marker.

- Coder cache cleanup on macOS and Linux can no longer be redirected by a symlink swapped in after its check: a cache store or finished-session cache root replaced by a link to another directory is refused or left alone, and nothing outside the cache is deleted (#3036).

- Coder session caches are now cleaned up and swept for retention for any `CAR_CODER_STATE_DIR`. They are created at `<parent of the state dir>/coder`, but session cleanup and the shared-cache sweep looked inside the state dir itself. The two are the same place only when the state dir is named `coder` (the default `~/.car/coder`), so an operator with a differently named state dir, such as `/x/state`, accumulated caches at `/x/coder` that nothing removed. Default installs are unchanged, and caches for custom state dirs stay where they already are. Because that folder may not be CAR's alone, deletion is limited to CAR's own names: the sweep removes only entries named like a repository cache key (64 lowercase hex characters), and session cleanup acts only on an id shaped like a session id (`coder-` plus 32 lowercase hex characters). Anything else, including `..`, a path separator or an absolute path in an edited snapshot, is left untouched whatever its age.

- Coder conversations no longer stop at eight. When eight are open, starting or reopening another closes the least recently active one that is not answering or starting a task, instead of refusing; the closed one stays saved and reopens with nothing lost. CarHost, the CLI and `car code` may close any idle conversation this way; an agent may close only its own, and another principal's only after an hour idle, as before. When nothing can be closed the start is refused, and that message no longer names an RPC. In CarHost, Plan, Build, Start task and Pin example now handle a conversation the service has already ended the way Send does: the task offers Resume conversation and says why in plain words, instead of showing the raw "no open discussion" error; Save and close and Stop reply treat it as already closed, and Apply model reopens it with the new model. In `car code`, sending, preparing a task or starting the reviewed task in such a conversation reopens it from its saved history and keeps your message in the composer, so pressing Enter again sends it.

- Coder discussions on Automatic: when the routed reasoning model answers with nothing (no text and no tool call), the discussion asks once more with reasoning turned down (thinking off and a low reasoning budget, with a small output budget raised to fit it), still on Automatic, and shows that answer. A discussion on a model you picked does not get this reduced-reasoning retry, and its requests are unchanged; the runtime's existing thinking-off retry is unchanged. On Automatic, the discussion's own short calls (turning the conversation into a task, and checking that you asked for one) now ask for low reasoning from the start, so a coding route's large default reasoning budget no longer leaves them no room to answer; on a legacy Claude route this means those calls now think briefly, where before the large budget did not fit and they ran without thinking at temperature 0. When a turn still ends with no answer, in a discussion, CarHost Chat, `car do` or the MCP assistant tool, the error now says "Send your message again, or choose a different model." instead of telling you to raise `--max-tokens` or set thinking off, which none of those offer (car#2087).

- Flaky `car-server-core` tests no longer fail when the whole suite runs on a loaded machine. The two `feedback_rpc` tests that commit logins run in their own process. Before, the credential events they published could fill a sibling test's 4-event queue, so the daemon dropped that sibling's connection. The macOS sandbox-profile tests allow `/private/tmp` only under CAR's state folder, so a `CAR_HOME` there passes. The agent-rerun liveness test counts progress notes instead of timing a gap. The coder terminal-guard, adoption and shell-tool tests get per-step limits well above their real duration. Test-only: no runtime behavior changes.

- CarHost Coder: a task whose run has merged, reported, failed or been stopped can be deleted straight away. Before, **Delete task…** stayed disabled for at least 30 minutes after the run ended, and until CAR restarted if no other task was started. A task whose run CAR no longer lists, or whose only run is a saved snapshot nothing is running, can also be deleted. Delete checks Sessions again first, and refuses while Sessions can't be loaded. When **Delete task…** is disabled, the task's menu says why, and a refused delete names what is still going. A run that is verifying, or whose plan was just confirmed, now shows as Running and holds a second Build until it ends; a run that finished without changes shows as Done.
- CarHost Build: a build that ends without changing anything (`reported`) now counts as ended. Before, the next build of the same agent was refused as "already has a build running" for 30 minutes, the chat card kept checking it, and a reconnect could resume it. The chat card now says "This build finished without changes, so nothing was added." The Build tab now has a headline for every build state, and offers Stop from the moment its plan is confirmed until it is ready for approval.
- WebSocket protocol docs: `coder.list` now says what `live` means. `live: true` means the daemon holds the session in memory, including for 30 minutes after it ends, so callers should read whether it is still going from `state`.
- CarHost Build an Agent: a build that is verifying is shown as building and kept up to date, also after reconnecting to it or resuming it. Before, it read as ended. A build whose saved record says it was still running when CAR restarted stops being checked and says "CAR is no longer running this build, so nothing was added." Resuming a build that ended without changes keeps saying so.

- `car code` sessions: an auditor reply that cannot be saved now journals an `audit_reply` event with an empty `evidence_path`, instead of naming a file that was never written.
- `car code` audit findings that repeat the same defect keep the copy that names a `defect_id`, whichever order the auditor listed them in.
- The 1,000-entry limit on a saved agent's scenario copy now counts every entry, folders and links included (even links the copy skips), so a tree of empty folders or links cannot make the copy run without bound.
- Time a check spends waiting for the protected Cargo home lock now comes out of the limit it runs under (its `timeout_secs`, else `max_check_timeout_secs`), for baseline checks and verifier `run_check` alike. Before, a check could wait for the lock and then still get its full timeout to run. Preparing the check's tree does not count. A check that times out under a limit the lock wait cut below its own is booked as starved, not red: `deadline_clamped: true` on a baseline result and `"deadline_clamped": true` in a `run_check` result. One that times out on its full own limit is still a real red.
- Coder cache cleanup on macOS and Linux, including the pruning of older trusted Cargo target generations, now opens each cache directory one path component at a time without following a symlink, so a parent of the cache swapped for a symlink during cleanup fails the cleanup instead of redirecting deletes outside the cache. A cache an operator moved behind a symlink still works: its path is resolved once, before cleanup checks it. Ancestors are opened for search only, so an execute-only parent folder keeps working, and cleaning a builder home whose cache folder was never created is a no-op.

- A coder shell that starts while its session is ending no longer survives the session: a process tree that registers after terminal cleanup has run is killed at once instead of being tracked, and the ended session's `<id>.procs.json` is never written again after cleanup removed it (#2063).

- A Coder conversation left without **Save and close** can be found again. `coder.discuss.list` now lists every saved conversation in `saved`, including ones still open, each with additive `open` and `archived` flags; before, a conversation stayed out of that list until it closed, which could be an hour or more after it was left. CarHost's Coder task list and the terminal board's saved-conversation picker both read `saved`, so a left conversation now shows there and reopens by reattaching. Hiding a conversation is now its own explicit, reversible action: the new `coder.discuss.archive` (NAPI `coderDiscussArchive`, Python `coder_discuss_archive`) archives or unarchives one of your own saved conversations without removing anything, and `coder.discuss.list` leaves archived ones out of `saved` unless called with `include_archived: true`; the NAPI `coderDiscussList()` and Python `coder_discuss_list()` bindings ask for archived rows too, so each row's `archived` flag is there to filter on. CarHost offers **Archive conversation** on a saved conversation or a task (the task keeps its draft, runs and status and is only hidden), with **Show archived** and **Unarchive conversation** to bring it back as it was; the board's saved picker archives with `x` and shows archived rows with `a`. Choosing the board's own conversation in its picker now returns to it instead of closing and reopening it (car#2089).

- Building an agent, and every other coder session, no longer starts a model download nobody asked for (Parslee-ai/car#3718). The independent verifier's turns carried no routing intent, so on a Mac where the router's cold start favored a local model that was never installed, checking a freshly built agent downloaded it (15 GB for gemma-4-26B), then the next uninstalled local model in its fallback chain, while the card read "Trying the agent on test case 1 of 1…" until the 900-second silence window failed the build. Every model call a coder session makes (the build's turns, an agent's spec and test cases, the verifier, and its re-run of the agent's test cases) now routes only to a model that is usable now: a remote model with its credential, or a local model already installed. With none, the session stops at once with an error that names the model it would have used and the command that installs it, for example "gemma-4-26B-A4B-it-QAT-4bit-MLX-vLLM is not installed, and this request never downloads a model. Install it with `car models pull gemma-4-26B-A4B-it-QAT-4bit-MLX-vLLM`, or connect a provider's API key so a model that is ready can be used". A configured `verifier_model` or chosen build model that is not installed is refused the same way rather than downloaded.
- `IntentHint` gains `never_download` (default `false`; accepted by `models.route`, `routeModel`/`route_model` intent JSON and generation requests). Unlike `require_ready`, which is dropped when nothing ready fits, it is hard: every model whose weights CAR would have to fetch first is removed before any routing path (adaptive, a preferred-model override, cold start, fallbacks) sees it, a requested model that is not installed is refused, and with nothing usable left the request fails with "no eligible model remains after required exclusions" before anything is sent, naming the model it would have used and its `car models pull` command. The rule is also enforced where those downloads start: on the inference generate and stream paths, a `never_download` request's model, drafter and tokenizer acquisitions refuse to fetch, the self-heal re-pull of a model whose files fail to load included, and a child process CAR starts for the request with the Hugging Face environment (the mlx-vlm image CLI, which is handed a repo id, and the vllm-mlx server) runs with `HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1`, so it reads only what is cached. A `never_download` request also installs nothing: a CAR-managed vLLM-MLX model whose vllm-mlx runtime is missing or older than 0.6.0 is skipped like one whose files are missing, a request naming it is refused with what fixes it (`uv tool install vllm-mlx`; for an old one, its path and `uv tool install --upgrade vllm-mlx` when uv installed it, the `uv pip install` command for CAR's own runtime, or else upgrading it with the tool that installed it), and CAR's own runtime install refuses inside the request. Routing looks the runtime up at most once per request, off the async workers, and not at all for a model whose server is already running, and reuses the answer for up to 30 seconds (a found runtime only while its interpreter still exists). Starting a server always looks again, as before. "Installed" means what acquisition needs: a model whose drafter is not on disk is not installed, and an MLX model with no `tokenizer.json`, which loads without a download, is. Other downloads, such as the Whisper speech model, are not covered; no coder session reaches them.
- Changes that also reach requests without `never_download`:
  - A managed vLLM-MLX model with a speculative-decoding drafter now counts as ready only when its drafter is on disk too, so the assistant setup message and the on-device last-resort fallback no longer treat it as ready while its drafter would still have to be downloaded.
  - With `HF_ENDPOINT` set, the drafter's file listing now goes to that endpoint, like CAR's other Hugging Face fetches, instead of always to huggingface.co.
  - A shared vllm-mlx server that a `never_download` request started keeps running offline (`HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1`) for later requests to the same model. Its model and drafter are local directories, so it needs nothing from the hub.

- Non-host callers can no longer receive an Agent project when a host creates the same project concurrently with their app request.

- A coder project slug must be one folder name under the projects folder. A slug that is empty, made only of dots, ends in a dot or space, contains `/`, `\`, `:` or a NUL, or is an absolute path is refused before any project file is read or written: `coder.projects.get` and `coder.start`'s `project` answer with an invalid-params error (`-32602`) naming the slug, and a self-heal target whose `project` is such a slug fails when it runs. Before this, a slug such as `../other` or an absolute path resolved to a folder outside the projects folder. Every slug `coder.projects.create` makes still works, as does any project saved under a plain folder name.

- Two daemons asked to continue the same retained coder task at once no longer both reopen its worktree. The process that reopens it holds the old task's lease until its continuation is saved; a concurrent request is refused with "still running or being reopened in another process", and one that arrives after the continuation is saved is refused with "already reopened as <session id>; open that task instead". Reopening a task's saved review now turns its recovery lock into the task's lease without releasing it, and startup adoption of a finished task locks the same lease file, so neither can recover into a worktree another process is taking over. A lease file its holder removed while another process was locking it is no longer taken as held, so two processes can no longer both lock one session through the old and new file. An older daemon sharing the same state root does not take part in these locks (car#2704).

- `car code` now rolls back a coder shell command that spells a contract check with numeric escapes. On macOS and Linux, `$'\x63argo' test` and `$'\143argo' test` were not recognized as `cargo test`, so the command's edits were kept as builder work. `$'…'` words are now decoded the way bash and zsh decode them (hex `\xHH` and `\x{…}`, octal `\NNN`, `\uHHHH`, `\UHHHHHHHH`, `\cX`, and zsh's dropped backslash on an unknown escape), and text printed with those escapes into a shell (`printf '\x63argo …' | sh`) counts as the check. A script path spelled with those escapes resolves the same way. bash's `$"…"` is now read as the quoted string it is, so `$"cargo" test` counts too, and every parameter expansion that can be empty (`$NAME`, `$1`, `$@`, `${…}`, quoted or not) is also read as empty, so `ca${x}rgo test` counts as it does when `x` is unset, while a name set by a plain literal assignment in a run of assignment statements that starts the line, used in the very next statement, reads as that literal (`x=r; ca${x}go test` counts, `x=x; ca${x}rgo test` does not; after a subshell, `&&`, `|`, `local` or `unset` it reads as empty) (car#3194).

- `car code` no longer rolls back a coder shell command's edits because the command only printed or wrote a contract check's words. On macOS and Linux the check's words still count as a replay wherever they appear (inside quotes, `sh -c`, `eval`, command and process substitutions, groups, and behind any wrapper such as `uv run`, `npx` or `timeout`), except as operands of `echo`, `printf`, `:`, `type`, `which` or `command -v` at the top level of a plain pipeline whose output goes nowhere, to a file, or only into data sinks (`cat`, `tee`, `grep`, `wc`, `head`, `tail`, `sort`, `pbcopy`, `less`, `more`, `jq`). A here-document or here-string is data only when every reader in its pipeline is such a sink, so `echo bash scripts/lint.sh > runbook.txt` and a `cat > notes.md <<EOF` body naming the check now keep their edits, while `ssh host <<EOF`, `python3 <<EOF` and unescaped `$( … )` substitutions still count. Printed escapes (`\t`, `\n`, `\0`), assignment and alias values, `$'…'`, `${NAME:-word}` and `${NAME:=word}` are read the way the shell would run them. A command passed as an argument list (`subprocess.run([...])`) or written to a file and run by a later step is not detected. Under `cmd` on Windows these exceptions do not apply yet (car#2691).
- `car coder-ab` re-scores now find an `output_contains` marker printed after the first 64 MiB of a stream. The stored output is still capped, but the marker is looked for in everything the check printed, so such a check no longer reads as a false failure. `$exact:` and `$json:` assertions are graded as assertions, the way the session grades them, instead of as literal text (car#2691).

- `car code` now rolls back a coder shell command that runs a check script behind a wrapper option that takes a value, such as `time -o out.txt ./scripts/lint.sh` or `time -f %E bash ./scripts/lint.sh`. The option's value was read as the program, so the script went unseen and its edits were kept. On macOS and Linux the value options of `time`, `env`, `stdbuf`, `xargs` and `sudo` now follow the macOS man pages and GNU, a script run with `env -C DIR` or `sudo -D DIR` is resolved in that directory, and the command line `env -S` carries (`env -S'…'`, `env -vS'…'`, `--split-string=…`), with the operands after it, is read as a command (car#3210).

- macOS Coder: a task whose reply finished while you were in another section, or had closed the CarHost window, now shows "Reply ready" when you come back to Coder. Before, the task list showed nothing, so nothing said the reply had finished. The mark stays until you open or delete that task, also if CAR's background service restarts in the meantime. Coder sees that a reply is running only when it checks, every few seconds while a reply may be running, so a reply that finishes within a few seconds of leaving Coder may not be marked, and neither is one that finishes before Coder has been opened since CarHost started. A conversation that ends before its reply finishes, because you stopped it, saved and closed it, or the background service restarted, isn't marked. While CarHost can't reach the background service, it checks less and less often, and after ten minutes it stops checking until you open Coder again. A mark you haven't opened isn't kept when CarHost quits. Fixes #2156.

- CarHost Coder: **Stop preparation** now cancels the task being prepared at once, instead of waiting up to 180 seconds for preparation to finish and then discarding the result. CAR's service never tags a task with the app's request key, so the one task Stop can see may be one this Build is queued behind; when `coder.start` then names a different task as this Build's own, Stop cancels that one too, including when the reply arrives after the app's timeout.

- The coder retention sweep now runs on the first boot of a fresh state root. It used to be skipped with "open .../coder/.sweep.lock: No such file or directory" because the `coder/` directory did not exist yet; the sweep now creates it before taking its lock.

- On macOS, CAR Coder no longer lets an untrusted repository's model shell, contract checks, or baseline checks read or write the operator's credential stores (`~/.aws`, `~/.ssh`, `~/.azure`, `~/.codex`, `~/.claude`, `~/.config`, `~/.docker`, `~/.kube`, `~/.gnupg`, `~/.netrc`, `~/.git-credentials`, `~/.npmrc`, `~/.pypirc`, Cargo credentials, and their env-relocated locations), and those children no longer inherit `SSH_AUTH_SOCK`. Previously they could, although the independent verifier's shell was already denied the same list. Git's XDG configuration directory stays readable (never writable) so git keeps working, `git/credentials` stays denied, and `~/.gitconfig` stays readable and never writable; a repository kept inside one of those directories still works. A symlink inside a credential store is denied at its target, wherever that target is (a linked binary stays runnable but not readable). Linux untrusted sessions get none of these denies yet (tracked in #3690), nor do Windows ones. Tools configured under `~/.config` or `~/.docker` (such as Docker CLI plugins) need the repository to be trusted; trusted repositories keep today's access.

- ChatGPT sign-in: when a token refresh can't get the credential lock in time, or its refresh request fails, CAR still falls back to the current token, but now only if that token is unexpired at the moment the wait ends. Before, the check used the time from before the wait, so a request could be handed a token that had expired meanwhile. The waits are also bounded in total and sized to cover one refresh in flight. Anything waiting for the credential lock waits up to 90 seconds: one refresh's 30-second token request plus the secret-store reads and writes around it. A refresh, a forced refresh, or the app's sign-out (its pause and delete together) waits at most 3 minutes for this process's refreshes and the credential lock combined, however many are queued ahead. When that runs out a refresh falls back as above. Otherwise the result is "A ChatGPT token refresh or sign-in is still updating the credential; try again in a minute." when this process's own refreshes are still running, or "Another CAR process is still updating the ChatGPT sign-in…" when the credential lock is still held. For Rust callers, `car_auth::codex::pause_codex_refreshes` now returns a `Result<CodexRefreshPause, String>`; delete through its `delete_credential` so the delete shares the pause's wait.
- Remote MCP connectors: removing a connector now waits for at most one token refresh. Refreshes queued behind it give up instead of each spending up to 30 seconds, so a removal could no longer take minutes. The whole wait is bounded at 60 seconds. If that runs out, `connectors.remove` fails with "connector <slug> is busy (credential refresh or removal) after 60s; try again" (the new `ConnectorError::Busy`) and leaves the connector as it was. Refreshes and a sign-in's token save are bounded by the same wait. A `connectors.remove` no longer waits for a tool call that is still running on the connector's session: the connector leaves the list, the manifest and the tool routes before the call returns, and the session closes in the background. A removal cut off part-way, for example by a closed connection, no longer leaves the manifest listing a connector whose tokens are already deleted. A `connectors.refresh` that is still connecting when the connector is removed no longer puts it back into the list or the manifest.
- CarHost: the ChatGPT sign-out request now waits 240 seconds, longer than the daemon's worst case of 225 seconds (a sign-in finishing its save, a Verify ahead of it, and the 3-minute pause and delete), so the app no longer gives up on a sign-out the daemon is still completing. If the reply is still unclear, the app keeps checking the status until the sign-out lands.

- Delegated Codex runs with image attachments no longer hand Codex an extra image named `-`. `--image` takes multiple values, so the trailing stdin marker was read as one more image path and the model was told "Codex could not read the local image at `-`". CAR now passes `-- -`, so the marker always reaches Codex as the prompt argument. The in-process Codex CLI inference backend passes `-- -` too, so its prompt no longer depends on which flag happens to follow the images.

- ChatGPT sign-in: a token refresh in another CAR process can no longer bring back a sign-out or overwrite a newer sign-in. A refresh now holds a per-user lock (`codex-credential.lock`, shared by every `CAR_HOME` because the stored sign-in is one per-user secret; the interactive sign-in's `codex-login.lock` moved beside it) from reading the stored credential through saving the new one, and `car auth logout --provider openai-codex`, the app's sign-out and every sign-in wait for it. If another process holds that lock for more than 60 seconds, sign-out and sign-in say so ("Another CAR process is still updating the ChatGPT sign-in; try again in a minute.") and a model request keeps using the current token while it is still valid. The save also checks that the stored credential is still the one the refresh started from, and drops its result otherwise. A process that waited for another's refresh now uses that refresh's new token instead of spending the old refresh token again, which OpenAI could reject as reused and leave the account needing sign-in. Remote MCP connectors get the same guard: removing a connector while its OAuth token is refreshing no longer leaves the refreshed tokens behind, and a connector token refresh now gives up after 30 seconds.

- Messages between colleagues on an organization-verified Lattice connection are no longer held for a second approval. Offering or accepting that connection is the agreement to what may process messages on the colleague's CAR. Connections no organization verified (an invite code, a LAN knock) still hold the first message for the owner, as before.

- `compose.yaml` no longer loads a `.env` file, and the README no longer tells container users to create one. Provider keys go in CAR's secret store, which in the container is one owner-only file per key in the `car-state` volume, so they survive `docker compose down` and `up --build` and no longer show in `docker inspect` or `docker compose config`. To enter one, run `docker compose exec car car keys set ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` and paste the value when asked. Upgrade step if you already have a `.env` next to `compose.yaml`: move each key with `car keys set`, then delete the file. Until you do, the keys in it are no longer passed to the daemon (car#3174).

- The Linux server `compose.yaml` now binds the A2A peer port to loopback (`--a2a-bind 127.0.0.1:8731`), so a plain `docker compose up` listens on nothing beyond `127.0.0.1` and no longer announces the server over mDNS. To let CARs on your local network find it again, remove the `--a2a-bind` argument from the service's `command:`. The same file adds an optional `caddy` service, started only with `docker compose --profile public up`, that terminates TLS on port 443 for `CAR_PUBLIC_NAME` and forwards only to CAR's public door on `127.0.0.1:9443` (opened with `CAR_PUBLIC_BIND`). The new guide [Remote access](docs/remote-access.md) covers the door, issuing and revoking outside credentials, installing an outside orchestrator, the Azure Container Apps layout and the ingress measurements still to be made, and a Node client example.

- Linux write confinement now drops the user's supplementary groups before a confined coder command runs, so a check can no longer reach a group-owned daemon socket such as `docker.sock` (`root:docker 0660`) and ask a root daemon to write for it. The command keeps only its primary group (the effective group of the process that starts it, so start the daemon under the user's own primary group, not a privileged one such as `Group=docker`). Dropping the groups needs `newuidmap`/`newgidmap` (the `uidmap` package) and `/etc/subuid` and `/etc/subgid` entries for the user; it uses `unshare --map-auto` on util-linux 2.38 and later and runs the helpers itself on older util-linux such as Ubuntu 22.04's. On a host where the user has supplementary groups and these are missing, confined commands are refused, and the message names the three ways out: install `uidmap`, add the subordinate ID entries, or run the check outside write confinement. A check that needs group-gated access (the `docker` group, a group-writable shared repository) must run outside write confinement. A user with no supplementary groups is unaffected.

- Remote MCP connectors: removing a connector and adding one under the same name straight away no longer risks the new connector being disconnected, or listed but unable to run its tools.
