Cleanup left over from the removed connector (closing its session, unregistering its tools from open sessions, or a `connectors.refresh` of it that was still connecting) now acts only on the removed connector and leaves the new one alone, and the removed connector's keychain secrets are deleted before its name is free, so they can no longer take the new connector's secrets with them. Two adds of the same name at once now get two connectors (the second slug with `_2` appended) instead of the second silently replacing the first. A `connectors.refresh` no longer undoes a `connectors.enable_tools` or `connectors.disable_tools` made while it was connecting, and one whose connector was removed meanwhile now fails with "connector not found: <slug>" instead of answering for a new connector of the same name. Saved connectors are now listed, and their names held, as soon as the daemon reads them, and each one connects on its own: one whose server never answers no longer delays the others and can still be removed. A connector redial no longer uses an OAuth token stored by a newer connector of the same name. A tool call or tool listing that is waiting on one connector's server also no longer holds up calls to other connectors, or adding and removing connectors.

- Remote MCP connectors: a `connectors.refresh` of a removed connector that was still connecting when a new connector took its name no longer reads the new connector's stored header secrets (API keys, bearer tokens) and sends them to the removed connector's server; it fails with "connector not found: <slug>" as it already did for OAuth tokens. Header secrets are now read under the same per-connector lock as OAuth tokens, so connecting a connector with header secrets can wait up to 60 s behind a token refresh, another refresh, or a removal of the same connector. When that wait runs out, the error reads "connector <slug> is busy (credential refresh or removal) after 60s; try again".

- Linux servers (source-checkout path for operators): the repository's `Dockerfile` and `compose.yaml` start CAR from a container image with `docker compose up`; they are not part of the release downloads. The dashboard answers at `http://127.0.0.1:9101/` on the server and `docker compose exec car car ls` lists agents. The container uses the Linux host's network and the daemon binds `127.0.0.1`, for the WebSocket, dashboard and MCP ports, so none of them is reachable from other machines; reach the dashboard through an ssh tunnel of ports 9100 and 9101. Only the A2A peer port 8731 listens on the server's network address, as with `car-server` run directly. Every request except the public agent card must be signed. A CAR you have not trusted can only send rate-limited connection requests (a knock you accept or decline, or a connect handshake). Trusted CARs can exchange messages, and the port never runs actions. The server also announces itself over mDNS; `car lattice knock-settings --accepting false` (run through `docker compose exec car`) stops that and refuses new knocks, and `--a2a-bind 127.0.0.1:8731` also closes the port. Do not publish those ports or put a reverse proxy in front of 9101, because the dashboard hands the daemon's auth token to any request that carries a loopback `Host` header. Provider keys go in a `.env` file next to `compose.yaml` and reach the daemon as environment variables; they never enter the image. The image is CPU-only. The first build takes about 47 minutes on a 4-vCPU machine.

- `registerAgentBasics()` (Node) and `register_agent_basics()` (Python) now work against a daemon. The daemon implements `agents.register_basics`, which registers `read_file`, `write_file`, `edit_file`, `list_dir`, `find_files`, `grep_files` and `calculate` for the calling session and runs them in the daemon, so the README quick start's `calculate` tool registers and executes instead of failing with `-32601` and then "tool 'calculate' is not registered". A supervised agent's token is refused (its file tools would act on the daemon host); it registers its own tools with `tools.register`.

- A stop signal (SIGTERM or SIGINT) that reaches `car-server` at any point during startup now shuts the daemon down cleanly and stops every supervised agent it has already started. The signal handlers used to be registered only when the final shutdown wait was first polled, which is after agents start, the lock is taken and the ports are bound, so an early signal killed the daemon outright and left its agents running with no parent. The on-device inference worker (`--mlx-worker`) is unchanged and still exits on SIGTERM.

- When the daemon ends a chat turn's route without a Stop (the host that started it disconnects, the agent does not acknowledge the turn within 5 s, or a conversational A2A turn runs past its 180 s deadline), it now sends the agent `agent.chat.cancel`. The bundled assistant closes the approval cards that turn was waiting on at once, instead of leaving them parked for up to five minutes (two for a goal check) on a route nobody can answer, and runs no goal check (#2668).
- Stop now ends a bundled-assistant turn at once, even while a model call is in flight: the turn ends as stopped and no further reply text is shown or acted on, even when the reply comes back together with the Stop. Before, the reply finished first. The MCP `assistant_cancel` tool stops a run the same way: it no longer waits for the call in flight, so the tool call that reply would have made does not run (#2668).

- Memory and skills written to a `car-server` daemon survive a restart. The shared graph that the daemon's MCP endpoint and unbound WebSocket sessions use was built empty at every start and never saved; it is now saved to `~/.car/memory/daemon-shared.json` (under `CAR_HOME` when set) after each memory or skill write, over WebSocket or MCP, and again on a clean shutdown, and loaded at startup. Skill writes (`skill.*`, `skills.*`, MCP `skill_ingest`) and `memory.load` are now saved for agent and namespace graphs too, as are MCP writes from a supervised agent's credential. Agent and namespace snapshots now keep the whole typed graph — skills, confidence, tenant and category included — in new `agents/<id>.v2.json` and `memory-namespaces/<ns>.v2.json` files; the flat `<id>.json` files older daemons wrote are read when no `.v2.json` exists and are never changed, so going back to an older daemon shows its older memory instead of wiping it, and a write that older daemon makes is picked up by the next upgrade (the replaced typed file is kept as `.superseded-<unix ms>`). Proactive-memory injections and coder repair learning on the shared graph are saved too. `memory.persist` refuses the daemon's own memory files (including legacy agent and namespace files), and `memory.load` refuses its typed snapshots, plus other graphs' files for a supervised agent; what counts as a daemon file is decided by file identity, so a hard link, a symlink or a case-folded spelling (`daemon-ſhared.json`) cannot reach one, and a new non-ASCII file name directly in `~/.car/memory/` is refused. Saves are atomic and land in order, and a typed snapshot that cannot be decoded is moved aside to `<file>.corrupt-<unix ms>` instead of being overwritten. `memory.load` (NAPI `loadMemory`, PyO3 `load_memory`) reads both the flat `memory.persist` format and the typed shape.

- Provider keys: the CAR daemon no longer copies keys into its own environment at startup (provider keys, `car keys` names, AWS, GitHub and search keys, `HF_TOKEN`, and any name ending in `_API_KEY`, `_TOKEN`, `_SECRET` or `_PASSWORD`). Keys are resolved when they are needed (shell environment, then the secret store), so the daemon's model calls, its own Claude Code and Codex runs (including network-isolated ones), voice, search and the coder auditor keep working, and a stored key does not reach the daemon's child processes unless that child needs it: the daemon's `gh`, `git push` and `hf download` child processes are handed a `GH_TOKEN`, `GITHUB_TOKEN` or `HF_TOKEN` from the store when they are spawned. `car doctor` lists each provider key with its source.

- `car-server` now gives its tokio worker and blocking threads a 32 MiB stack instead of Rust's 2 MiB default. In a debug build, `workflow.run` (about 3 MiB) and A2A `SendMessage` (about 3.8 MiB) overflowed the default and aborted the whole daemon, so every connected agent lost its connection and the caller got no response. Both now answer. Released daemons were not affected: measured on macOS arm64, a release build serves both calls on 512 KiB. The stack is reserved address space that is committed only as it is used.

- The macOS CLI tarball (`car-darwin-arm64.tar.gz`) no longer embeds the CarHost zip and the `.pkg`, about 325 MB that every CLI install downloaded. Both stay published as their own release assets. The tarball's documented contents in DISTRIBUTION.md now name `car-host` and the MLX `mlx.metallib` it does carry.

- **Removed: the dashboard's `GET /auth-token` route.** car-server no longer serves the daemon token over HTTP on any port. `GET /auth-token`, which handed the daemon token to any page or local program that looked like the dashboard, and every other `/auth-token` path now answer exactly as an unknown path does on each CAR listener (`404` on the dashboard port), whatever the method, headers or sign-in state. The dashboard's sign-in routes moved off that path with it, so their old names are removed too: `POST /auth-token` is now `POST /sign-in`, and `POST /auth-token/challenge` is now `POST /sign-in/challenge`; the request and reply bodies are unchanged. What a client uses instead: a browser opens the one-time link `car ui` prints and signs in (see `docs/websocket-protocol/ui.md`); the dashboard page no longer falls back to fetching the token and asks for a sign-in link instead. A local program reads the token from its `0600` file (`$CAR_HOME/ai.parslee.car/auth-token`, or the per-platform path), or uses the `car-runtime` npm/PyPI packages, which read it for you. On Windows that file is DPAPI-encrypted, so a raw WebSocket client there sets `CAR_AUTH_TOKEN` (the streaming cookbook shows how to decrypt it as the same user). Front doors step 6c, car#3208.

- The local dashboard's run history now loads: a session with the daemon token can read (list, replay, subscribe) all run history. Known limitation: any process on this machine can obtain that token, and supervised agents hold it too, so an agent can read other agents' runs. Accepted until per-person login replaces the shared token (#3008).

- `car ui` sign-in codes: when more than eight unredeemed codes exist, the daemon now keeps the most recently minted ones, including codes minted in the same millisecond, and a mint's own trim never drops the code it is about to hand out (two daemons sharing one `CAR_HOME` mint independently, so one can still replace a code the other just minted). Before, codes minted in the same millisecond were ranked by a hash, so the link `car ui` was about to print could be the one dropped. Each code record now stores its place in the mint order; a record saved by an older build has none and is replaced first among codes that expire with it.
- A sign-in code dropped over the eight-code limit can no longer sign in when its file cannot be deleted. The daemon now marks it used instead, as it already did at startup and sign-out. If it can neither delete nor mark the code, it logs a warning, and that code still works until it expires or the next sign-out, so more than eight codes can work in that case.

- The dashboard can now sign in without the daemon token. `car ui` asks the running daemon for a one-time sign-in link over its host-authenticated connection (new host-only method `ui.sign_in`), so the link names the dashboard address the daemon actually serves (`http://127.0.0.1:<dashboard port>/#login=<code>`, good for 10 minutes and one use) and a daemon refuses a code recorded for any other dashboard address. With no daemon answering, `car ui` mints nothing and says to start CAR. It prints the link, and on macOS and Windows opens it (on Linux it only prints it, because any user there can read a browser opener's command line). The page then keeps a browser key that cannot be exported and signs in again by itself after a daemon restart. `car ui --sign-out` (or the new host-only `ui.sign_out` method), or the dashboard port moving, ends every signed-in browser session and deletes every browser key. A page that holds a key and is refused, or was closed with `4005`, shows that it is signed out. The dashboard port gains `POST /sign-in` (code or signed challenge in, single-use 30-second ticket out), `POST /sign-in/challenge`, and its own `/ws`; these answer only a request from the dashboard's own origin (an `Origin` equal to the request's own `scheme://Host`, or, with no `Origin`, `Sec-Fetch-Site: same-origin` or `none`). Every response from the dashboard port now sets `Content-Security-Policy: worker-src 'none'`. No route returns the daemon or host token.
- Wire addition: `session.auth { browser_ticket }`. It is accepted only on the dashboard port's `/ws`, as the connection's first credential, and makes a dashboard session: its own principal, never the host or the operator, bound to no agent, and limited to a list of methods generated from the dashboard page (anything else is refused with `-32003` `not_on_dashboard_list`; binary frames are dropped unread). New refusal reasons: `browser_ticket_off_dashboard`, `browser_ticket_invalid`, `browser_ticket_after_auth`, `dashboard_session_bound`. A signed-out dashboard session is closed with WebSocket close code `4005`. No protocol version change; no binding presents a browser ticket.
- `agents.install` replies no longer include the agent's token, and `runs.get_trace` / `runs.subscribe` replies pass through the log redactor, so a key that reached a trace is shown as `[redacted]` when it has the shape of a provider key or token, or the daemon knows its value: from its environment, from a stored value this daemon process resolved since it started, or as a daemon, host or agent token. The redactor does not sweep the keychain, so after a daemon restart a stored key it has not resolved again, in a shape it does not recognise, is not scrubbed; scrubbing at write is tracked in car#3645.
- The dashboard page connects to `/ws` on its own port. The `?ws=` query parameter and the old guess of the WebSocket port (`dashboard port - 1`) are gone.

- `cargo deny` no longer fails on the three October 2026 `zip` extraction advisories (RUSTSEC-2026-0367, -0368 and -0369). The vulnerable `zip` 7.2.0 is pulled in only by `candle-core`, which reads and writes `.npz` weight files but never extracts an archive. CAR's own reader of untrusted Office files is already on the fixed `zip` 9.0.3.

- A proposal with an unmeetable dependency is now refused whole, before any action runs (car#3060). An unmeetable dependency is an action needing a key that is not in state at submission and that nothing ordered before it in the executor's order can write: no declared writer that can itself run, and no `tool_call`. That includes a key nothing in the proposal declares writing. A dependency cycle among declared effects is the usual case, and such a proposal cannot complete through its declared effects and ordering. Previously the per-action check rejected only the blocked actions, and the rest of the proposal still ran. A `tool_call` that finishes before the blocked action keeps it advisory, because its handler can set the key without declaring it (`state.set` from a `tools.execute` handler, FFI `state_set` from a callback, car-multi shared state). That includes the issue's own `[tool, a1, a2]` shape with the default `failure_behavior`, whose first level runs sequentially. In that case the blocked actions are still refused one at a time while the others run. The residual is timing the gate cannot see, and those plans are refused: a `tool_call` in the same concurrently-run level, a streaming or long-running `tool_call` ordered earlier whose handler happens to finish in time, or another client's `state.set`. `car_verify::VerifyIssue::new` is added so callers outside `car-verify` can build a finding.

- A gpt-oss checkpoint added from Hugging Face no longer claims tool calling when CAR serves it through vllm-mlx, matching the built-in `vllm-mlx/gpt-oss-20b:mxfp4` row. vllm-mlx 0.6.0 never returns gpt-oss's tool calls, so a derived schema that claimed them drew tool turns from the router it could only fail. The same checkpoint on another runtime, and every other family on vllm-mlx, keeps tool use.

- A disconnected headless `coder.start` session that another client was watching when its reconnect window expired is now cancelled within one `silence_window_secs` window of the last watcher leaving; a subscription left behind by a connection that has already closed no longer counts as a watcher. Before, a single watcher present at the deadline stopped the expiry timer for good, so the session and its disposable worktree stayed alive with no owner after everyone disconnected. `silence_window_secs = 0` still leaves the wait unbounded.

- A development CarHost (no bundled `car-server` beside it) no longer runs `car-server` straight from a Cargo target. It picks the newest release build among `$CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, `~/.cache/cargo-target` and the checkout's `car-rs/target`, refreshes it into `~/.local/share/car-dev/bin` (or `CAR_DEV_BIN`) with `scripts/install-dev-binaries.sh` off the main thread, and spawns the copy, so targets can be swept or rebuilt while the daemon runs and a relaunch picks up the new build. A build of `car-server` alone still gives a daemon. A refresh that fails, runs past 90 seconds, installs a partial set or keeps a newer copy is shown in the menu-bar menu.
- On Windows, a development `car-tray.exe` likewise copies the newest `car-server.exe` (with `car.exe` and `car-mcp.exe` when present) from `$CARGO_TARGET_DIR` or `car-rs\target` into `%LOCALAPPDATA%\car-dev\bin` and spawns the copy, so a running daemon no longer blocks the next `cargo build` of car-server. The copy is serialized by a file lock, never downgrades a newer copy of any binary, keeps the existing copy when the running one can't be replaced, and reports a development build it couldn't copy as the daemon start error instead of falling back to `PATH`. Kept or partial refreshes are logged (the tray's menu shows them only on macOS).
- `scripts/install-dev-binaries.sh` now serializes installs with a kernel lock (a killed install never blocks the next; it exits 3 after 60 seconds behind a live one), copies every binary before replacing any, leaves an unchanged binary untouched, and keeps any installed binary newer than its source (installing the rest of the set) unless `--force` is given.

- `vllm-mlx/devstral-small-2-24b:4bit` has a car-judged score again: 0.358 on the 17-task judged suite, with every task completed. Its earlier run lost 4 tasks to 300 s timeouts and was withheld. The re-run used the unarmed-deadline and `vllm-mlx --timeout` fixes, so the router can rank devstral against the other local models. `vllm-mlx/gemma-4-31b-it:4bit` was re-run the same way and is still withheld: 2 of 17 tasks ran past the benchmark's own speed-scaled task limit (484 s at about 15 tok/s), which is the model being slow, not the cut-off bug.

- Ending an old LAN or invite-code Lattice connection now reaches the other CAR even when its LAN address no longer answers: the notice goes through the Parslee relay on the verified connection the two CARs also share, so the other side closes it too instead of keeping it open.

- The README's Claude Code plugin section now describes the worktree pool: the plugin's worktree hooks, opting a repo in with `.car/worktree-pool.toml`, and that a repo without it gets ordinary Claude Code worktrees. The README and the agent authoring guide now say that an agent run with Bun on Linux needs Bun 1.3.12 or newer, because older Bun starts with an empty `process.env` inside CAR's per-agent folder wall.

- `car doctor` no longer lists state the current version writes under "Not written by this version of CAR". It now recognizes the agent handshake record (`agent-handshakes.json`), the agent start records (`agent-starts/`), the dashboard sign-ins (`ui-sign-ins/`), the outside-credential store (`credentials/`), standing chat goals (`chat-goals.json`), the concierge's resource-policy marker (`model-resource-policy.concierge.json`), the signed catalog's withdrawn and revoked records (`catalog-retained.json`, `catalog-revoked.json`, `catalog-revoked-models.json`), a run's proposal records (`proposal-finalization/`, `proposal-execution/`, `proposal-retry-rollbacks/`, `proposal-id-claims/`, `proposal-completed/`), the fleet worker's settings and worktrees (`fleet-worker.json`, `fleet-worker/`), workflow dedup seen-sets (`workflow-dedup/`), the coder's repository trust list that `car coder trust add` and `remove` save (`coder-trust.toml`), and what CarHost keeps there (`host-multiplayer-stages.json`, `host-state.json.corrupt`, and `CarHost/` when `CAR_HOME` is set). It also recognizes files CAR reads there but never writes: your own `config.toml`, `heal.toml`, `policies/`, and `car dream`'s default `memory.json`. A second test in `car-cli` reads every crate's source for names joined onto the state root and fails when doctor does not know one (for its kind, file or directory, where the source shows it plainly), so most new writers fail CI until doctor recognizes them. It does not see every way code can reach the state root (car#3719).

- `car doctor` no longer calls live state "possibly from an older install". It recognizes everything the current version writes in the state root: the iMessage inbound watermark (`messages-inbound-watermark.json`), the `onnxruntime` build cache, and `catalog-status.json`, `identity.json`, `work-contexts.json`, `coder.toml`, `concierge-auto-since`, `host-state.json`, `model-management/`, `lattice/`, `peer-certs/`, `work-contexts/`, `tools/`, the supervisor's `agent-log-scrubs/`, the worker directories `workers/`, `bundles/`, `labels/`, `agent-refusals/` and `label-secret`, the spend meter's `spend/`, declarative-agent `agent-runs/`, and the relocated token directory `ai.parslee.car/`. Whatever it still does not recognize is listed as "Not written by this version of CAR" and left in place, with no advice to remove it. A new test in `car-cli` runs the real writers into a temporary state root and fails when doctor reports any of their output (car-releases#86, car-releases#90).
- `car doctor --repair` now clears a model directory an interrupted `car models pull` left behind (links into the HuggingFace cache, no resolvable weights) and lists it under Repairs, so the verdict no longer tells you to re-pull models it never purged. It takes the model's cross-process lock first, removes the directory's manifest files and then its links one by one, never recursively, refuses any file a pull does not write (a `weights.npz`, a `pytorch_model.bin`, someone's notes), never deletes through a link that leads outside the HuggingFace cache, never touches a directory CAR holds an install receipt for or a pull's in-progress staging directory, and never deletes from the shared cache. The remedy for an incomplete install now names commands that work: `car doctor --repair`, then, if you still want the model, `car models pull <id>`. When a curated `car models upgrades` replacement for the model is already installed, the install is reported as removable rather than something to re-pull (car-releases#87, car-releases#90).
- `car models pull` replaces an interrupted install of the same model instead of refusing it as a "pre-existing unreceipted artifact"; any other unreceipted artifact at that path is still refused (car-releases#87).
- `models.remove` takes `force: true` (`car models remove --force`, NAPI `removeModel(modelId, force?)`, Python `remove_model(model_id, *, force=False)`) to remove an artifact CAR holds no install receipt for at the model's own managed path: a link is unlinked without being followed, and an interrupted install is cleared as above. An unreceipted directory of real files is still refused and named for `models.adopt`. Forced removal drains the model and holds its activity lease for the whole deletion, in the same order as a receipt-backed removal, and writes no tombstone (car-releases#87).
- `car doctor`'s partial-download report adds how long ago each `.sync.part` was last written, and `car doctor --repair` now removes the partials that are provably abandoned and CAR's: in a repo CAR fetches whole, no writer holding the lock, untouched for a day, re-checked just before each unlink (the rule concierge maintenance already used). Every other partial is reported as possibly still being written or not CAR's and left in place, with no command to delete it by hand (car-releases#87, car-releases#90).

- `car doctor --repair` no longer deletes files outside `$CAR_HOME` or the HuggingFace hub through a symlink. Journal discovery skips a symlinked `journals/`, `work-contexts/`, work-context entry or context `journals/` directory instead of following it, so the empty `.jsonl` files behind one are left alone. The journal hash-chain check skips the same links and a symlinked journal file. Purging a corrupt model file removes the file a link resolves to only when that file is a blob inside the HuggingFace hub; any other link loses only the link itself. A model directory linked to your own storage is still repaired there: only its own corrupt files are removed.

- The agent draft card in chat now offers the same clock choice as Change schedule: this Mac's time (the default) or a fixed time zone picked from a list. The choice carries into the agent it builds, so a schedule drafted in chat can keep one zone's clock from the start. A change to an existing agent drafted in chat starts from the zone that agent's schedule keeps. Rebuilding an agent from Build an Agent also keeps the fixed zone its schedule has, where before it went back to the Mac's clock.
- The draft card asks CAR how it reads the Schedule field and says so under it. Text CAR can't read as a schedule, such as a sentence about scheduling later, is flagged in orange; an agent built with it runs only when you ask. If you choose Build it before CAR has read the field, the card reads it first: text CAR can't read stops that Build it with the flag showing and nothing built, and choosing Build it a second time builds it as it is. If CAR can't be asked, or hasn't answered within 5 seconds, Build it goes on as it did before.
- The draft card no longer warns that your answer mentions email when the answer rules Mail out ("do not read Mail", "no Mail", "never email anyone"). It still warns when Mail is off and the job or what the agent may use asks for email; for a change to an existing agent that leaves Mail as it is, Mail is off when that agent has it off. Build an Agent's own Mail warning reads answers the same way.

- Durable scheduled command tasks now record their runs and keep their output (car-releases#88, #102). The OS job that `scheduleTask` / `tasks.schedule` / `car schedule add` installs launches `car schedule run-recorded <id> --digest <hex>` instead of the command itself. The digest pins the command the job was installed with: the recorder reads the task record at each firing, and if the record's command (program, args, env, working folder, timeout or log paths) has changed since, it runs nothing and records a failed run saying so. Changing what a durable task runs means scheduling it again. That recorder writes a start stamp before the command runs (`last_run_at`, `status: "running"`, an in-flight execution) and an exit stamp after (`run_count`, `status`, and a new `exit_code` on the execution), so a healthy schedule no longer reads like one that never fired. It also applies the task's `working_dir`, `env` and `timeout_secs`, which OS-fired runs used to drop, and appends the command's stdout and stderr to new `stdout_path` / `stderr_path` spec fields. Paths must be absolute; the default is `<CAR_HOME>/logs/tasks/<id>.log`. A missing log is created 0600; the default log is rotated to `<id>.log.1` past 10 MiB, and a file you name keeps its own permissions. `car schedule add` takes `--stdout` / `--stderr`. The daemon-timer path writes the same log. Installing a durable task now needs a `car` CLI that has the recorder, found as the current program, beside it, or on `PATH`. A `car` too old for it is passed over, and with no other the install fails rather than installing a job that could never run; this matters from node or python, whose packages ship no `car`. A job with no timeout now stays in the job's process group, so stopping the job stops the command too. On macOS the OS now launches `car` rather than your program, so macOS may attribute privacy permissions (Calendar, Full Disk Access) to `car`; this has not been checked on a job that needs one. If such a job starts failing after the update, grant `car` the permission.
- Existing durable command schedules move onto the recorder at the next daemon boot, or with `car schedule reconcile` / `scheduler.os_reconcile`, whose report gains `reinstalled`. A job whose installed command no longer matches its task record is left as it is and reported in `errors`, and a moved task is measured for lateness from the move, not from its creation date. Reconcile no longer reaps the job of a task file it cannot parse.
- `listScheduledTasks` / `tasks.list` / `car schedule list` now return every record in `~/.car/tasks/`, not only command tasks. Each row has a `kind` (`command`|`agent`|`dream`|`prompt`, or `unreadable` with `file` and `error` for a task file that cannot be parsed, which used to be dropped silently), the last run (`status`, `last_run_at`, `run_count`, `last_exit_code`) and its log paths. `backend` is now one of `os`, `daemon`, `orphaned` or `none`. A durable task whose OS job is missing reads as `orphaned`, where it used to read `daemon` although the daemon has no cron evaluator, and the daemon timer no longer adopts such a task. `unscheduleTask` adds `orphaned` to its result. The dream loop now stamps the `car-dream` record after every pass. That pass still does not consolidate your memory: the daemon gives the loop an empty memory engine (car#3495). The record says so, with each pass `failed` and marked detached rather than `completed`. The loop also no longer overwrites `~/.car/embeddings.bin` with that empty engine's embeddings every hour. `car doctor` gains a Scheduled tasks check. It reports orphaned tasks, installed jobs from before run recording, enabled tasks well past their cadence and unreadable task files, and it notes the detached dream loop without counting it as a problem.
- The `scheduleTask` spec now accepts the camelCase field names its binding docs used (`intervalSecs`, `workingDir`, `timeoutSecs`). Before, only snake_case was read, and a camelCase `workingDir` was silently ignored.

- Corrections to earlier release notes:
  - v0.58.0 said receipts replayed from earlier turns of a thread now number turns the same way as live `tool_call`/`tool_result` ids, "so the two agree". They agree only for calls made after the thread's most recent compaction. A call from before it keeps the id it was given then, so its replayed receipt can carry a different turn number. Resume after a restart is not affected: it looks up only the latest turn, which always comes after the compaction.
  - v0.57.4 said that under an absolute `core.hooksPath` the pre-push hook hands over to the pushing worktree's own hook. The handoff is in the hook copy git loads, so it happens only when that copy is from car#2557 or later. If the absolute path points into a checkout whose hook is older, that hook still checks its own checkout. Run `git config core.hooksPath scripts/git-hooks` (or `scripts/install-hooks.sh`) in each checkout.
  - v0.56.0 said `verify-release.sh` checks that the published macOS wheel's extension module "will actually load". It does not load or import the module: it parses the module's Mach-O load commands for the string-table alignment macOS 27's dyld enforces, the defect behind the 2026-09-20 and 2026-09-21 wheels. Its pass line now says "structurally loadable (Mach-O load-command check; not imported)".
  - v0.56.0 said the docs stopped deriving an MCP session's peer principal from `MCP-Session-Id` "in all four places". The `car-mcp` README and the `car-peers` API docs still called the sender `mcp:<session-id>`. Both now say `mcp:<peer-id>`, and the README says that `MCP-Session-Id` is a bearer credential that must not be shared.

- TypeScript audit queries and retention policies written as `index.d.ts` documented now take effect. `events.query` accepts `actionId`, `proposalId` and `dataMatches`, and `events.retention` accepts `maxEvents` and `maxAgeSecs`, as aliases of the snake_case names. Before, those keys were ignored, so a camelCase filter returned every event and a camelCase policy kept everything. Unknown keys are still ignored; sending both spellings of one field is now an error naming the duplicate field. The snake_case names Python callers use are unchanged. The `index.d.ts` and `car_runtime.pyi` stubs now show the snake_case names: `eventCostByAgent` returns `{agent, calls, tokens_in, tokens_out, cost_usd}`, as it always did. The stubs and `docs/websocket-protocol/events.md` also say that `events.query`, `events.cost_by_agent`, `events.stats` and `events.chain.verify` read only the calling connection's log (except the `worker_started` / `worker_starts_omitted` events the daemon copies into host and orchestrator sessions), and that the daemon deletes finished per-client journals at startup once they are older than 30 days or beyond the 1,024 newest. The `permissionSetTier` / `permission_set_tier` stub text now says what the daemon does: under a host token every tier change, lowering included, needs the host role.

- On the Everyday resource preset, CAR now automatically recommends local models up to its full 40% memory ceiling, not half of it. On a 64 GB Mac the old 12.8 GB target meant that Qwen3.8-27B, Qwen3.6-35B-A3B and Gemma-4-12B (CAR's best-scoring local models) were never offered by default, even though each loads within the ceiling. The estimate a model is compared against already includes its context, runtime and a transient margin, and loading still checks live memory. Local-focused and Custom are unchanged.

- Saved agents: when a step that changes something didn't go through and the agent ends its turn anyway (for example, it tried to edit a file without reading it first, then said "Heartbeat appended."), CAR now tells it once which step didn't go through, in the tool's own words, and asks it to do it now or say why it can't. If it then makes the change, the run succeeds. If it still doesn't, the run fails as before, naming the step. This applies to scheduled runs, Run now, Retry, runs started from a chat, routed runs and test cases, and it fits inside the run's turn limit. A run that was stopped is never asked.
- A run that failed because a change didn't go through, or because its goal check never passed, no longer shows the agent's last answer as its Result. Work, the agent page's Latest work and the Scheduled run history show that answer under "What the agent said" instead, and test case results list it the same way. Work items and run history saved before this update are shown the same way. `declagents.invoke` returns that answer as `agent_said` with `output` empty, `declagents.runs` lists it as `agent_said`, and `declagents.run_scenarios` adds it to each failed case.

- A request that falls back from its first model to another now sends the fallback an output limit worked out for that model from the caller's own request: its output cap when the caller left `max_tokens` at the default, automatic thinking only where the fallback supports it, and room for reasoning only where the fallback reasons on every call. Before, every fallback was sent the first model's limit and thinking budget, so a reasoning fallback behind a model that does not reason could spend the whole limit thinking and return an empty answer, and a fallback with a smaller output cap could be sent a limit its provider rejects. A streamed request's remote fallbacks now get their own output cap, and its local fallbacks the caller's `max_tokens`, instead of the first model's.

- CarHost: a report submitted with "Report a problem" now goes to your Parslee organization when you are signed in, even if nothing in that app session had loaded your account yet, and even when you're offline. Before, those reports were saved for the anonymous lane, which never sends, and stayed on the Mac. `feedback.submit` with `lane: "authenticated"` now binds the stored sign-in's organization when the daemon has not loaded its Parslee session, and after `auth.switch_org`, or the next `auth.status` after a re-sign-in, the daemon's session follows the new organization instead of keeping the first one, so a report submitted after an org switch is no longer refused as a mismatch. If CAR can't confirm the sign-in at all, the report is still saved on the Mac (on the anonymous lane) instead of the submit failing, and `feedback.submit` returns `lane` and `sign_in_unconfirmed` so the confirmation can say why. When a report can't be sent, the confirmation now says it was not sent and makes Export the main action. Export on that confirmation now saves the report you submitted (with diagnostics collected again when no preview was used); before, it saved an empty report.

- The Apple system model (`apple/foundation:default`) now receives the whole conversation. A request with `messages` used to be answered from `prompt` alone, so `car do`, which sends an empty `prompt` beside its `messages`, got an answer with no context. The conversation is now rendered into the model's prompt, oldest turn first. Its system messages lead the instructions, tool calls and results are included, and an external tool result is fenced as data, as it is for a remote model. On macOS 27, images inside `messages` now reach the model on non-streaming requests without tools or a JSON schema; before, they were refused. That does not cover `car do`, which always sends tools, so its image turns still route to a vision model. A history too long for the model's context window is not truncated. An overflow is now a context-window refusal (`UnsupportedMode`, with the token counts in the log) instead of a model failure, so it no longer counts against the model's circuit breaker or health record. An unpinned non-streamed request moves to the next model, a stream ends with the refusal, and a request pinned with `strict_model` gets the refusal where it used to be answered without its history. A pinned `car do` turn on this model still cannot be answered: its tool catalog alone exceeds the 8,192-token window on macOS 27 (measured on turn 1: 10,262 tokens before this change, 11,625 with the system prompt it now receives). The history fix applies to requests that fit. The overflow is recognized in each form the framework reports it: on macOS 27, the typed error and the untyped error a tool-bearing session raises (both measured); on macOS 26, `GenerationError.exceededContextWindowSize`. The mlx-vlm route (`mlx-vlm/qwen3-vl-2b:bf16`) now also receives the rendered conversation and its images. A request without `messages` reaches it unchanged.

- A fresh install no longer writes the deprecated `agents.json`, so the next boot no longer warns "loading agents from legacy agents.json". Agents are registered only in the current `agents/<id>/manifest.toml` layout. An install that already has an `agents.json` keeps it up to date and keeps the warning; because writing is keyed on the file existing, downgrading to an older CAR (which recreates `agents.json`) and upgrading again returns that install to legacy mode. `car doctor`, `car info` and feedback bundles read agents from both layouts, so they still list agents on a fresh install.
- If saving an agent fails on a fresh install, the daemon no longer keeps the agent in memory (or keeps an edit) that is not on disk, and removing an agent still deletes its folder under `~/.car/agents/` even when another agent's file cannot be rewritten.
- CarHost Settings -> Agents now points at `~/.car/agents/` (Reveal in Finder) instead of `~/.car/agents.json`, and no longer creates a stub `agents.json`.

- The concierge no longer recommends GLM-4.7-Flash's 4-bit build. On simple prompts it repeats itself until it runs out of tokens ("+ 5 - 5 + 5 - 5 …"), at every sampling temperature and on both vllm-mlx 0.4.1 and 0.6.0, and it scored 0.081 on CAR's judged suite. An installed copy still works and stays listed, marked deprecated. The 8-bit build (`vllm-mlx/glm-4.7-flash:8bit`) answered the same prompts correctly and is cataloged in its place. It needs about 37 GB, more than the default local-model allocation on a 64 GB Mac (40%), so on that machine it is offered only under the `local_focused` profile.

- `agents.chat` goal turns continue the session's conversation. A goal turn on a session that already had turns used to start from a fresh transcript after waiting for the earlier turn and then store it over that turn's exchange, so the next turn lost the earlier history and could reuse its call ids. It now loads the session's thread the way an ordinary turn does (the stored thread, else the durable checkpoint, else a fresh one). The goal's completion check sits with the conversation's system prompt for that goal turn only: a later goal turn replaces it, and an ordinary turn after a goal turn no longer carries it (car#2794).

- `openai/gpt-6.1-sol:latest` carries a car-judged score (1.0, one run, measured 2026-10-26 with `thinking: auto`) and a measured answer rate, like the other native frontier rows. It was the last OpenAI frontier row ranked without one.

- `vllm-mlx/gpt-oss-20b:mxfp4`'s car-judged score now rests on two clean runs, 0.501 (spread 0.029), the second taken under the tool-less task prompt. Its two tool-less tasks that used to stop for a phantom python tool now reason to the output limit instead; both still score 0 there.

- The catalog no longer claims tool calling for `vllm-mlx/gpt-oss-20b:mxfp4`, so the router stops sending tool-using agent turns to it. On vllm-mlx 0.6.0 gpt-oss cannot emit a tool call at all: with CAR's `--tool-call-parser auto`, the streamed reply carries the model's intent to call ("We need to call the get_weather function…") as reasoning and no `tool_calls`, and the non-streamed one returns the arguments as plain text (`{"city":"Paris"}`) with no function name. `--tool-call-parser harmony` with `openai-harmony` installed does the same. The row keeps generate, code, reasoning and summarize.

- `params.reasoning_effort` now reaches gpt-oss on vllm-mlx, as its chat template's `reasoning_effort` ("Reasoning: low" in the harmony system message). It was dropped there like on every local model, which left gpt-oss with no thinking control at all: its template ignores `enable_thinking`. The effect is large: on a hard probability task with a 4000-token limit, the default (medium) and `high` reasoned to the limit and returned no answer, while `low` finished in 2835 tokens with one. Only the explicit field is sent; a level derived from `budget_tokens` is not, so an automatic thinking budget cannot push gpt-oss to `high`. `xhigh` is sent as `high`.

- gpt-oss served through vllm-mlx no longer returns its reasoning as its answer. vllm-mlx 0.6.0's non-streaming path flattens gpt-oss's harmony channels before its reasoning parser runs, so a turn cut off at `max_tokens` inside the analysis channel came back with that analysis as the answer ("We need to count spanning trees…"), and a finished turn lost its reasoning entirely. Its streaming path keeps the two apart, so CAR now sends gpt-oss's non-streaming requests as a stream and folds the result back: the reasoning is thinking, the answer is only the final channel, and a turn that never reached an answer is empty and handled as one (CAR's thinking recovery). Every other family keeps the plain request.
- A gpt-oss turn cut off at `max_tokens` is no longer retried with thinking off, and CAR never sends gpt-oss `enable_thinking=false`. Its harmony template has no thinking switch, so the retry decoded the same reasoning a second time, and on vllm-mlx the switch only turned the server's reasoning parser off, which returned the analysis channel as a "recovered" answer.
- `vllm-mlx/gpt-oss-20b:mxfp4`'s car-judged score is re-measured with the fix: 0.516 (1 run). The three earlier runs (0.531–0.693) are withdrawn because their judges graded leaked analysis-channel text as the answer. Turns that hit the output limit inside the analysis channel now score as what they produced, nothing. Two hard tasks also come back empty because the suite's system prompt tells the model to use tools it does not have, and gpt-oss starts writing code for its built-in python tool; that is a harness issue, not counted here as fixed.

- The `car-judged` benchmark the router ranks models by is now a 17-task suite with harder math, tracing and debugging problems, because the old one saturated: half the catalog scored 1.0 and the router could not tell a frontier model from a mid-tier one. The scored models in the built-in catalog were re-measured against it on one machine with one judge; Gemma 4 31B and GLM-4.7-Flash 8-bit could not be re-measured in time, so their old scores are removed rather than left on the old scale. Cloud models now spread from 0.49 to 1.0 and the best local model scores about 0.66. A model whose run did not complete (more than one timed-out or errored task) has its old score withdrawn instead of kept. The four 6,000-token tasks allow 600 seconds instead of 300, so a model that thinks by default can finish them; no other model came near the old limit. The `tau-bench-airline` scores, measured once in June for nine models and never refreshed, are removed, and `openai/o4-mini` is marked deprecated because OpenAI's API now answers 404 for it. Because real scores now sit lower, the size estimate `car models recommend` uses for an unmeasured local model is scaled to the best measured local score, so "most capable" no longer picks an unmeasured 31B over every measured model.

- `car-self-harness` now splits its tasks three ways, following *Harness Learning* (arXiv 2609.35738). The current harness's failures on the **feedback** set become the evidence the proposer sees. Candidates are compared on a separate **scoring** set the proposer never saw: a candidate is kept only if it improves there without getting worse on the feedback set. A third **eval** set is measured every round and for the final harness and decides nothing. Before, the loop mined and ranked on the same tasks, so an edit that fixed exactly the failures it was shown looked like an improvement. `--held-in-fraction` is replaced by `--feedback-fraction` (default 0.4) and `--scoring-fraction` (default 0.3), and the report's `held_in_task_ids`/`held_out_task_ids` by `split` and `final_eval`.
- `car-self-harness` records every proposal that never became a candidate, with the reason (`model_error`, `parse_failed`, `apply_failed`, `noop`, `duplicate`), in each round's `dropped` list, and flags a candidate that passes no scoring task as `zero_score`. The summary prints failed edits and zero-score candidates per round and the run's share of unusable proposals. Before, dropped proposals disappeared without a count.
- `car-self-harness` re-runs the round's best candidate and the current harness on the scoring set before merging, and merges only if the candidate still passes more tasks; the report records the re-run as `confirmation`. Each candidate's evaluation names the scoring tasks it gained and lost and the feedback tasks it lost. A candidate that did not improve the scoring set is rejected without running the feedback set. The run refuses to start when the task set is too small to give the feedback, scoring and eval sets a task each.
- `car-self-harness` proposals are SEARCH/REPLACE edits against the current system prompt instead of a rewritten prompt. Each SEARCH text must occur exactly once; an empty one appends. A reply that does not parse or whose SEARCH text does not match is sent back with the error, up to three attempts, and otherwise recorded as `parse_failed` or `apply_failed`. Each candidate keeps its `replacements` beside the prompt they produce.
- When two `car-self-harness` candidates improve the scoring set by the same amount, the one whose runs errored or timed out less is nominated.
- The `car-self-harness` proposer now sees, for each failed example, the task text, the agent's final answer, its turn and tool-call counts, and the grader's explanation for each unmet criterion. It also sees the run's earlier candidates and whether each was merged, did not improve results, or did not hold up on a re-run (never scoring-set numbers). Bench `TaskScore` gains an optional `trace` excerpt (task, answer, turns, tool calls).
- `car-self-harness --independent N` runs a baseline arm instead of the loop: N independent edits of the seed prompt from one execution report, each measured on the scoring and eval sets and never merged. It reports the seed's eval score, the mean candidate's, the share above the seed, the candidate the loop's own selection (gate, nomination, confirmation re-run) would keep, and the oracle best on eval (labelled as such). It refuses to run when the seed's feedback report has nothing actionable to propose from. With `--out`, the report is tagged `"arm": "sequential"` or `"arm": "independent"`.
- `car-self-harness` records every proposer call (exact prompt, raw reply, how far it got, the candidate it became) with a reward following Harness Learning's Eq. 6 and 8, and `--dataset <path>` writes them as JSONL rows for training a proposer elsewhere. A no-op reply is scored as the current harness and a duplicate as the candidate it copies, and each row carries its own scoring pass rate and the current harness's, so a quality filter applies to a row alone. `--proposer-model <id>` runs the proposer on a different model from the one under test; `SelfHarnessConfig.model`, which was documented as a proposer override and read by nothing, is replaced by `proposer_model`.
- Skill evolution now says why an attempt produced nothing. `MemgineEngine::evolve_skills_attempt` returns the skills with an `EvolveOutcome` (`proposed`/`ingested` counts, `parse_failed`, `model_error`, `nothing_to_evolve_from`), and `parse_skills` has a `try_parse_skills` sibling that keeps a reply that is not a skill array apart from an empty answer. The `evolution.run` Skills step and the daemon cadence's Skills step append the counts to their summary, and count a domain as evolved only when a skill was admitted to trial.
- Fixed: the dream pass (`consolidate`) and the daemon's evolution cadence never evolved a skill. Both call skill evolution with no failure traces, meaning "evolve from the existing skills", and evolution returned early on an empty trace list without asking the model. With no traces it now builds the prompt from the domain's recorded outcomes and each skill's success and failure counts, which is what elected the domain in the first place, and it makes no model call when the domain has no recorded outcomes, or none since its last attempt, so a reply that admits nothing is not paid for again on every dream pass or cadence tick. The dream pass now counts a domain as evolved only when a skill was admitted.
- Fixed: `car-harness-opt`'s random restarts all climbed from the seed config. The perturbed start point for each later restart was computed and thrown away. Each later restart now climbs from a perturbed seed that actually differs from it, scored and recorded as that restart's step 0, and that evaluation comes out of the restart's share of the budget, so a search spends at most `budget` evaluations, the seed's included.

- The self-healing repair loop can cover every project in every organization you belong to. A new `[[org]]` section in `heal.toml` names a GitHub organization or user, or `*` for your `gh` login and every organization it belongs to. Each sweep keeps that owner's unarchived repositories your login can push to and that you have checked out under `roots`. Checkouts are matched by `origin` remote, never by directory name; a main checkout is preferred over a linked worktree; symlinks, hidden directories and `~/Library` are not searched; and nothing is cloned. Each kept repository's labelled issues are worked like a `[[target]]`'s. A sweep now works at most three items across all targets, and the next sweep starts just past the last target it reached. `heal.status` reports the configured `orgs` and what the last sweep `discovered`. `heal.run` with `item` accepts a discovered repository, matched case-insensitively. The self-heal detector's hand-off sees discovered repositories too.

- The self-healing repair loop hands work it cannot do unattended to a person instead of retrying it on local models. The cadence skips any item carrying `human_label` (`needs-human` by default). When local models fail an item twice, or it hits a failure no retry changes, the loop does four things. It labels the item. It assigns the `escalate_to` GitHub logins from `heal.toml`, so GitHub notifies those teammates in whatever organization the repository belongs to. It comments the reason. And it raises a CarHost approval card. Approving the card runs that item with the attended configuration (frontier models) on the next tick, charged to the operator. `car heal run --item owner/repo#N`, `heal.run` with `item`, `healRun(chatSessionId, item)` and `heal_run(item=...)` run one item that way at any time, setting aside its backoff, exhaustion and label. An item is handed over once (recorded in the claim ledger) with one comment. The approval card is a high-risk gate row, so a supervised agent cannot approve the frontier run it starts, and it is dismissed only after the run it approved has happened. A change that passed review but could not be delivered is escalated without offering a frontier run. `heal.run` refuses a malformed `item` instead of sweeping every target.

- Self-healing escalations reach people over Lattice. A new `notify` list in `heal.toml` names Lattice addresses (`<node>@<car>`): your other devices, or nodes a consented connection shares, so a teammate's CAR hears when an item needs a person. The daemon sends as `car-self-heal`, now a reserved id no agent may take. It sends in the work context of the item's repository: your context for the organization its checkout declares in `.car/config.toml`, or personal. An organization's notice therefore reaches only that organization's nodes. Each notice passes every stage an agent's `agents.message` does: the channel guard, the context rule, policy, the audit, and the hold on a first message to another owner's CAR. `agents.message` and the daemon now share one pipeline, which takes the sender's context as stated rather than reading it from the sender's name.

- The self-healing repair loop's unattended sweeps (the cadence) now run on local models only, so it never spends money with no one watching. The engine is `native`, because an outside coding CLI bills its own provider outside CAR's meter. The coder and every review seat are pinned to installed local models, and the sweep runs under a new local-only spend scope (`SpendScope::local_only`), which refuses any remote dispatch at admission with `remote_in_local_only_scope`. Name the models under a new `[unattended]` section of `heal.toml`, or leave it out and CAR picks them from installed local models: only downloaded text models with at least 32,768 tokens of context are considered; the largest that writes code and calls tools codes, and the largest model of each other vendor reviews, up to three seats. A local seat's independence is counted by lineage (who trained it), so Qwen variants are one seat. Under the local-only scope, routing considers on-device models only and the verification auditor picks a local model from another vendor, so a run never reaches a remote model it would then be refused. The refusal also holds where no spend gate is installed. The self-heal detector's own auto-fix now runs `car code-task --local-only`, a new flag that restricts that process to local models, and the detector hands work to the repair loop only while its unattended cadence is running. `heal.status` reports the result as `unattended` (`{coder_model, review_models, source, refusal}`). The top-level `review_models`, `coder_model` and `engine` now apply only to a sweep a person starts with `heal.run`, which may use frontier models. Targets alone enable the loop; before this, the cadence ran on the attended configuration, by default an outside coding CLI and a frontier panel, with its spend uncapped. Named models meet the same bar as picked ones (a coder that writes code and calls tools, text seats with room for a diff), a reviewer from the coder's own vendor is seated last, and a remote verifier or non-`auto` auditor named in `~/.car/coder.toml` refuses the cadence up front instead of failing every item at verification.

- Unattended self-healing picks local models that can actually run and finish. It skips models served by vLLM-MLX while that runtime is missing or outdated, and models larger than the Local Models RAM allocation, and refuses a named one of either with the reason. It ranks candidates by CAR's measured quality before size, and requires a coder that generates at least 50 tokens per second when its speed is measured. Outcome enumeration runs one model at a time under a local-only scope, so local models are not loaded side by side.
- A contract check whose nested single quotes split its argument (`python3 -c 'print('x')'`) is refused when the contract is validated, not reported as a failing check. A `python -m <module>` check whose interpreter lacks the module is reported at baseline as unable to run, naming the interpreter, instead of as a bug the coder must fix.

- Redaction: long hex values and keys cut into short segments no longer pass as plain identifiers and survive into coder ledgers, evidence artifacts or feedback bundles. A run of 40 or more characters stays readable only when it looks like an identifier. These are now redacted: a run spelled entirely in hex digits however it is grouped, a run where segments carrying a digit outnumber letter-only ones (`k3jx9qm_p2v8wzr_…`), and a run with an 8+ character segment that is all hex digits (including letter-only hex such as `deadbeefcafefacebead`) or mixes letters and digits. Descriptive names such as `conversation_task_list_isolation_and_restart` and `test_utf8_base64_decode_handles_padding_ok_x` stay readable.

- The Claude Code plugin's policy hook (`car policy-check-hook`) and the `car-mcp` `policy_check` tool now run CAR's built-in egress guard on Claude Code's `Bash` and `WebFetch` calls. Before, the guard only looked at tools literally named `shell`, `http` or `webfetch`, so it never ran in a plugin session. **This turns on new denies in every plugin session, with no rule written:** a `Bash` command or `WebFetch` URL that runs `nc` or `netcat` is denied — as the command, as an argument (quoted or not) of any program not on the guard's naming list, such as `sudo nc`, `watch nc`, `docker run alpine nc -z db 5432` or `kubectl exec pod -- nc -z host 80`, or in script text a shell runs, including what `echo` or `printf` pipe into a shell (`echo 'nc …' | sh`) and `bash <(echo 'nc …')`. So is any text containing `/dev/tcp/`, which matches as a plain substring, so a `grep` or a doc line naming it counts too; and so is one of `| bash`, `| sh`, `curl -X POST`, `wget --post` or `base64 -d` combined with a host off the guard's allowlist; the allowlist includes the installer hosts `sh.rustup.rs`, `bun.sh`, `astral.sh`, `ollama.com` and `claude.ai`, so their `curl … | sh` installers warn rather than deny, while the same pipe from a host off the allowlist is denied, and an upload pattern (`curl -X POST`, `wget --post`) to one of them warns rather than denies, the same as one to `github.com`; that rule, like `/dev/tcp/`, reads the raw text, quoted strings and here-documents included, so a command that only writes those strings is denied too. Each deny names the guard and the pattern (`egress: egress inspector blocked: suspicious pattern 'nc'`). `nc` passes as an argument of a program on the naming list — programs that only name, look up, install, print, or inspect and manage files and processes (`man`, `which`, `brew`, `cargo`, `make`, `grep`, `rg`, `echo`, `git`, `ls`, `cat`, `cp`, `rm`, `pkill`, `killall` and the rest the README lists) — except their runner forms (`npm exec`, `pnpm dlx`, `yarn exec`, `git bisect run`, `rg --pre`, `vim -c`, `make --eval`, `cargo --config`; `npx` is never exempt); inside a quoted string holding a space for another program; and in a here-document no shell reads, such as a commit message written with `$(cat <<'EOF' … EOF)` (an unquoted one's `$(…)` and backticks are still followed). The guard is a heuristic: other transports, an upload to an unlisted host without one of those patterns, and `nc` inside a multi-word quoted argument of a non-shell program (`ssh -o ProxyCommand='nc %h %p'`, `tmux new 'nc …'`), another interpreter (`python3 -c`), a script read from a file (`bash script.sh`, `cat f | sh`, `echo … > f; sh f`), a command built at run time (`$N`, `bash -c "$(echo nc …)"`, brace expansion), a locale string (`$"nc"`), an option or assignment value (`-o ProxyCommand=nc`, `LESSOPEN='|nc …' less`), a pager or editor running it some other way (`less +'!nc'`, `nano --speller=…`), the arguments `cargo run --` passes to the user's own program, or a Makefile target that runs it are not denied, and an unlisted host on its own only warns. Every URL in the text must be allowlisted; loopback (`localhost`, `*.localhost`, `127.0.0.0/8`, `::1`) counts as allowlisted, but `nc`, `netcat` and `/dev/tcp/` are denied even toward loopback. Operator rules still match the host's own tool names exactly (`deny_tool = ["WebFetch"]` matches what it matched before). The response gains `inspected_as`, the CAR tool a call was inspected as. CAR's own `http_request`, `browser`, `browse_navigate`, `browser_await_signin`, `web_fetch`, `run_command` and `pty_start` are inspected too, and a tool with no mapping that carries a `command` or `url` string is inspected but its guardrail findings only warn.
- `car_policy::EgressInspector` (library behaviour): `nc` and `netcat` now match where they run, reading the line with shell quoting, comments, substitutions and here-documents, instead of as the substring `nc `. A command that merely contains the letters (`rsync`, `async fn`, `cargo test -p car-sync`) or names the program (`git commit -m "fix nc"`, `man nc`, `pkill nc`, `brew install netcat`) is no longer denied; an `nc` argument of any program off the naming list (quoted or not, unless the quoted text holds whitespace), script text a shell runs, input a shell would reject as unterminated that names it, and nesting deeper than 8 are denied. `| bash` and `| sh` match only when the command after the pipe runs that shell (also through `sudo` or `xargs`), so `| sha256sum` is no longer read as `| sh`. The host allowlist matches on a domain-label boundary for every URL in the text, so a lookalike such as `evilgithub.com` is no longer treated as `github.com` and an allowlisted URL no longer excuses an unlisted one beside it; an `@` after `?` or `#` is not userinfo, and a host counts as allowlisted only if it does under both readings of a `\` before `@` (curl reads `https://github.com\@evil.com/` as `evil.com`). Loopback hosts count as allowlisted. `EgressInspector::default()` adds `sh.rustup.rs`, `bun.sh`, `astral.sh`, `ollama.com` and `claude.ai` (and their subdomains) to its allowlist, for every caller of the default, so a pipe-to-shell installer from those hosts warns instead of being denied. A deny reason now names `'nc'` rather than `'nc '`. Its contract is otherwise unchanged: it still inspects only `shell`, `http` and `webfetch`.

- Host approvals no longer accumulate rows nobody can clear, and a resolve that did not land now says so (Parslee-ai/car-releases#89). `host.resolve_approval` on a row that is no longer pending (another decision won, or the daemon closed it as `agent_gone` when the agent's session dropped) is refused with `-32003` and `error.data.reason: "already_resolved"`, carrying the resolution that stands and the row. Before, it returned success with the unchanged row, so a client that checked only for an error believed its deny had landed. The first resolution still stands either way. A resolve of a row whose owning session is not subscribed is now `-32003` with `error.data.reason: "owner_not_connected"`; the message text is unchanged. The new `host.dismiss_approval { approval_id }` removes a resolved row: an agent may remove its own agent's rows, and a host app or the operator any; a pending row is refused (`approval_pending`), and so is a Lattice review mirror whose decision has not reached the Lattice ledger yet (`lattice_mirror`). `host.approvals` now drops resolved rows older than 24 hours when it is read, not only when an approval is created, so an idle queue is pruned too; pending rows still age out only when an approval is created. `host.request_approval` adds two fields to its result: `subscribed`, whether the requesting session is subscribed to host events, and `answerable`, whether a host's decision can take effect. A session-owned row from an unsubscribed session is `answerable: false`, since it can never receive a host's answer, and the daemon logs a warning. A `system_level` row from an unsubscribed session can be decided, but the requester will not receive `approval.resolved` and has to poll `host.approvals` for the outcome. CarHost on macOS, the iOS and Android apps, the Windows tray, `car-host` and the bundled web host UI read the new refusal reason (an `already_resolved` refusal whose standing resolution is the one sent counts as landed), and keep comparing the returned row when connected to an older daemon. CarHost shows a row whose agent is not connected as "The agent that asked for this isn't connected, so your answer can't reach it" instead of the raw daemon error.

- **Host authority fails closed (front doors PR 6d, car#3208).** A session is the host only when it proved the host token. Before, a daemon with no host token installed (`--no-auth`, a failed host-token write at startup, or an embedder that never called `ServerState::install_host_token`) let every authenticated session act as the host. Now nobody is the host there, and every host-management method refuses every session with `this operation requires the host-management role` plus the notice and its cause. The same holds for acting on another agent (`agents.start`/`stop`/`restart` and the rest of the own-agent-or-host methods), `workers.spend` without an orchestrator binding, the host-only credential event stream, and an agent's Coder run. A bound agent still acts on itself, and an unbound connection still acts on a person's Coder run.
  - **Who breaks.** A deploy script that runs `car install`/`uninstall`/`start`/`stop`/`restart -y`, or `car agent new --yes`, against a daemon with no host token; a cloud or headless CAR started with `--no-auth`; a Rust embedder that never installs a host token; on Linux, any supervised agent other than CAR's own assistant under `--no-auth`, which is now not started (status `errored`, with a `status_reason` naming `--no-auth`); an unbound client of a no-host-token daemon that read the daemon token from `mobile.runtime`; every scheduled declarative agent on a no-host-token daemon, whose runs are now refused. A refused scheduled run does not stop silently: its time is recorded `failed` with the host-role reason (shown in `declagents.runs`), the daemon logs it, and it emits the host event `agent.scheduled_run_refused`.
  - **Migration.** Run `car doctor`: its `Daemon auth:` line says whether the running daemon has a host token and why not. If the host-token write failed, fix what it names (usually the permissions of `~/.car`) and restart the daemon. Drop `--no-auth`. In a deploy script run by the server's owner, replace `-y` with `--as-host` on `car install`, `uninstall`, `start`, `stop` and `restart`: it presents CAR's local host key without asking, needs the host-token file, and is refused in a process started as a supervised agent. That refusal reads environment variables the agent controls: on Linux the agent's wall keeps the host-token file from it, but on the Mac an unwalled agent can read that file and present the key (car#3706). Plain `-y` never presents the key. An embedder calls `ServerState::install_host_token`.
  - **Not changed yet.** `host.resolve_approval` still admits an operator session holding the daemon token, because the phone apps, CarHost and the SDK resolve approvals with that token today. It moves to the host role in a follow-up (car#3701).

- The host-permission probe tests no longer fail on a loaded machine (#3370). The success and error cases each use their own probe key, so the error case can no longer read the success case's finished answer. The overlapping-caller test waits until the second caller has joined instead of sleeping, and gives the first caller a long enough wait. The stuck-probe test times each call on its own clock and checks the re-probe with a different answer, so it fails when the probe is never cleared. The runtime behaves as before.

- On a machine whose certificate store holds certificates but none that can be read, these paths now fail with an error message instead of panicking: OpenAI Codex device sign-in, `car install <namespace>/<name>` registry downloads, Parslee capability discovery, M365 document generation, the OpenRouter connect flow (which now ends as rejected instead of staying pending), and remote AgentDNS discovery (which is skipped with a warning). `car_connectors::discovery::RemoteRoot::new` now returns a `Result` for this reason. An empty certificate store was never affected.

- `http_request` follows a redirect only on the origin (scheme, host and port) of the URL that was approved. A redirect to another origin is no longer followed: the tool returns that 3xx response with a new `location` field holding its absolute destination, so a POST and its body are never re-sent to a host the approval did not name. To go there the model makes a new `http_request`, which asks for approval naming the new host. Remote MCP connectors (`connectors.add`) follow redirects only on the origin they were added with, and re-send a call, auth headers included, to a 307 or 308 that only upgrades it from `http` to `https` on the same host; any other cross-origin redirect is a connection error naming that origin, instead of re-sending the call and the connector's auth headers there.

- A supervised agent can be a person's **human delegate** on the Lattice without the operator's host credential (car-releases#109). The operator grants it with `delegate_for: ["human:<name>"]` on the agent's spec (`agents.upsert`, host-only; an install keeps the grant already recorded for the id and never takes one from a publisher's manifest; personal agents only). Over its own agent connection the delegate is notified of that person's requests and answers (`lattice.human_request` / `lattice.human_answered` notifications), may read that person's requests with `lattice.requests` (only that person's rows), answer them with `lattice.respond` (recorded as `by: "delegate:<agent id>"`, and the asker is told: every answer from a person's node now carries `answered_by` — `host`, `delegate:<agent id>` or `approval` — on the `agent.peer_message` push, in an MCP inbox, from `lattice.wait`, and across a Lattice connection as `carPeerAnsweredBy`; never for a request it asked or forwarded itself, and never a `review_request`, which is an approval and stays the operator's: `approval_is_host_only`), set that person's status and note with the new `lattice.human.status` (capabilities, display name and project stay as the operator declared them), forward a question it received to that person with `agents.message { to: "human:<name>", on_behalf_of: <id of the delivered message> }` (CAR records the real asker, connection, body and kind from its own delivery record — the delegate's `body` is kept apart as `forward_note`, and it cannot turn a question into a review — charges the 10-pending cap to the asker, stamps `forwarded_by` and `forwarded_from` on the request and the approval row of a forwarded review, and delivers the answer to the asker directly with `in_reply_to` naming the asker's own message, so the asker waits on the id it already has; an answer to another owner's CAR crosses from the delegate's node, which that CAR asked), and read `multiplayer.list` / `multiplayer.get` (git then runs with a minimal environment, no system config, no file or `ext::` transport, and the repository's program-running keys overridden). Consent stays the operator's: `acknowledge_processing`, `agents.message.pending` / `.approve`, `lattice.connect.*`, `lattice.connections`, human registration and approval resolution are refused to a delegate, and an `agents.upsert` that changes `delegate_for` is refused to anything but the host client, even on a daemon without a host token. A worker never carries a grant, whatever its bundle declares. Removing the entry revokes it on the agent's next call, including over a connection already open. `lattice.nodes` lists each of this CAR's people with `delegates`.
- **Breaking (bindings):** `HostClient.latticeRequests` / `HostClient.latticeRespond` (Python `HostClient.lattice_requests` / `HostClient.lattice_respond`) were removed from the npm and PyPI packages. `lattice.requests` and `lattice.respond` moved from the host-only method set to the agent role so a person's delegate can call them, and the generated wrappers follow the role table: they are now `CarRuntime.latticeRequests` / `latticeRespond` (`lattice_requests` / `lattice_respond`), which send the caller's own credential — right for a delegate, refused (`not_a_delegate`) for any other process. A host-side operator process that used the `HostClient` wrapper calls `runtime.daemonCallHostManagement("lattice.requests", paramsJson)` (Python `daemon_call_host_management`) instead; the CLI already does. The wire protocol is unchanged. `multiplayer.list` / `multiplayer.get` are role `agent` (a delegate's read) instead of `operator_only`.
- A refused call on a person's Lattice node now has its own error code, `-32020`, with `error.data.reason`: `processing_notice_pending` (the answer waits for the operator to agree to the cross-owner processing notice; `data.connection` names the connection), `consent_is_host_only`, `approval_is_host_only`, `not_a_delegate`, `not_delegate_for_human`, `self_answer`, `forward_not_found` or `forward_not_a_question`. The message text of the processing-notice refusal is unchanged.
- `agent.peer_message` pushes and `lattice.requests` rows carry provenance an agent can tier trust on without the host-only `lattice.connections`: `connection`, `verified_by` (`fingerprint`, `tofu`, `org:<org>` or `cert:<org>:<account>`), `verified_org`, `verified_account` and `same_org` (whether the certifying org is one this CAR's owner belongs to). The protocol reference now says what an `unacknowledged` delivery means (written, not redelivered, may still be handled; acknowledge first and work after), which lattice fields agents depend on, and how a second attach with the same agent credential supersedes the first connection. No wire-protocol version change: every field is additive.

- The `human_delegate_ws` test suite now raises its compiler recursion limit, as the other suites that drive the WebSocket dispatcher already do, so it builds on Linux and Windows and under the release profile.

- A streamed reply whose caller has disconnected by the time the stream settles is no longer recorded as a success on the API key; it only frees the key's slot. CAR cannot see whether a disconnected caller read the final event, so two edges remain: a caller that read it and disconnected before the stream settled is released rather than counted, and a caller that stays connected without reading and disconnects only afterwards is still counted as a success. A successful HTTP response whose body CAR cannot parse is recorded as a failure on the key, with no tokens, matching the error the caller receives.
- A Codex CLI model is not listed as available while CAR's first `codex login status` check for it is still running, so a signed-out Codex no longer appears usable for the first few seconds after start. A check that fails or times out still leaves an installed Codex available, as before.
- On-device Foundation Models structured output now reports a constraint it cannot enforce as a schema degradation, instead of dropping it silently: numeric bounds (`minimum`, `maximum`, `exclusiveMinimum`, `exclusiveMaximum`, `multipleOf`), string constraints (`pattern`, `minLength`, `maxLength`, `format`) and array constraints (`minItems`, `maxItems`, `uniqueItems`).
- A multi-turn conversation sent to an on-device model, and the coder discussion transcript the coder distills into an intent, indent each turn's text, so text inside a turn that looks like a speaker label cannot pass for a turn of its own.

- Two `car-inference` test fixes. On an Apple Silicon Mac, the model-maintenance tests no longer depend on which models the machine has cached: they point the Hugging Face hub at their own temporary cache, and their fixtures carry the tokenizer and weight files a real snapshot has. The test-environment lock's 30-second backstop now fails only when the lock stops changing hands, so a long but moving queue of tests under `cargo test` no longer trips it. Its message now names a stuck holder instead of blaming lock order for a long wait. Test-only: no runtime behavior changes.

- `agents.install` (and `car install`, and the in-process `agents_install` binding) no longer replaces an agent already installed under the manifest's id without a word. It refuses, changing nothing, and names the installed agent and its version: "agent <id> is already installed (version <v>); nothing was changed. To replace it with version <w> on purpose, install it as an upgrade: `car install --upgrade`, or `upgrade: true` on agents.install". The same version is always refused as "agent <id> <v>: this version is already installed", with the way to install it again (`car uninstall <id>` first); versions compare by semver when both parse, so `v1.0.0` is `1.0.0`. A different version, an older one included (a rollback is an explicit upgrade), replaces the installed agent only with the new `car install --upgrade` flag or a top-level `upgrade: true` in the `agents.install` params, and then replaces it as before. An install never switches an id between a supervised agent and a `pure_data` / `health_url`-only bundle; that is refused with "Remove it first". An agent enrolled by `agents.upsert`, or a `pure_data` bundle tracked on disk only, holds its id the same way, and of two installs of a new id at the same moment exactly one is installed.
