An install id is validated before any path is built from it, so an id like `x/../notes` is refused instead of overwriting another agent's manifest. `agents.remove` of a `pure_data` or `health_url`-only agent now deletes its `~/.car/agents/<id>/` folder and reports `removed: true`; before, it removed nothing. The installed version is now recorded as `AgentSpec.version` (the manifest's `agent.version`), and an `agents.upsert` that names no version keeps it, as it keeps the token. `agents.upsert` still adds or replaces (car#3264).

- CarHost's Model Library and model setup no longer offer "Use in CAR Chat" for an installed model this Mac cannot run (for example downloaded GGUF weights on Apple Silicon, where CAR runs local text only through MLX). The daemon already marks such a row `platform_compatible: false`, and its installed weights never run here: CAR either refuses the row or quietly runs a different model, its MLX twin. Installed models CAR can run keep the action.

- On a cloud CAR, an agent can no longer see or act on other agents (#3257). A session bound to a supervised agent now sees only its own row in `declagents.list`, `declagents.get`, `registry.list` and `host.agents`, and only its own paused workflow runs in `workflow.list_paused` and `workflow.resume` (a refused resume is logged and leaves the run for its owner). `fleet.composite`, `fleet.inventory`, `tasks.list` and `scheduler.os_list` are refused to such a session with a plain reason because they have no "own rows". On Linux it is also refused `tasks.schedule`, `connectors.add_stdio` and `browser.run`, which make the daemon start a program outside the agent's wall. The Mac does not change for those three, and host, operator and CLI sessions are unchanged everywhere. Paused workflow checkpoints and `workflow.list_paused` rows gain an optional `owner` field.

- An agent now sees only its own records. A supervised agent that binds a memory namespace gets its own private graph (`agent:<id>:namespace:<ns>`), so two agents that pick the same namespace name no longer share facts, and `agents.list` returns an agent-bound session only that agent's own row instead of every agent's spec, `env` values and log paths. An agent-bound session's `memory.load` and `memory.persist` touch only the file of its own bound graph, and `agents.health` returns only its own row. The `registry.*` methods and `meeting.list` / `meeting.get` refuse a caller-named `registry_path` / `root` from an agent-bound session. Operator, host and `car` CLI sessions still see every row and bind the bare namespace name as before. Facts an agent stored under a bare namespace name before this change stay there for operator and host sessions; the agent starts empty under its own key. Step 4 of CAR in the Cloud agent isolation (`docs/proposals/cloud-agent-isolation.md`).

- On Linux, a supervised agent can now call only the daemon methods its own role and a short reviewed list allow (#3257). Every other method is refused with `<method> is not available to a supervised agent` and the refusal is logged, including the A2A methods (`SendMessage`, `ListTasks`, ...) and `coder.start`, `foreman.run`, `vision.ocr`, `transcribe`, `synthesize`, `models.register`, `declagents.invoke` and `scheduler.os_uninstall`, which ran outside the agent's folder wall and could read or write other agents' files. `agents.invoke_external`, `agents.chat`, `evolution.run` and `sync.*` (outside a work context) are refused too, host-role methods are refused whether or not a host token exists, a work context no longer exempts a session, and the built-in assistant is recognised by its daemon-registered binary, not its id. The Mac, host, operator and CLI sessions, and the built-in assistant are unchanged. A walled agent also cannot resolve approvals (`host.resolve_approval`), cannot call the operator's connector tools through `proposal.submit`, and can update or delete only the A2UI surfaces it created.

- Release lab: a promotion now opens its stable's bookkeeping pull request on its own. The lab's checkout is a worktree of a mirror clone, which has no `origin/main`, so v0.58.0's bookkeeping died after `DONE` and main kept the previous version and every consumed fragment. The step's `bookkeeping.log` is now published with the run record and printed by `car-release log`.

- Release lab: the lab retries opening the stable bookkeeping pull request itself (five attempts, doubling backoff) instead of leaving a person to run `bookkeeping-pr.sh`.

- Release lab: when enabling auto-merge on the stable bookkeeping pull request fails, the retry now requests auto-merge again instead of reporting success because the pull request exists. An attempt counts as done only when the pull request has merged, has auto-merge enabled, or is in the merge queue; otherwise it fails, and `bookkeeping.log` names the pull request and the `gh pr merge` command that queues it by hand. When the release branch was pushed but opening its pull request failed, the retry opens and queues the pull request from the pushed commit (after checking it is exactly the bookkeeping commit) instead of failing every retry on a rejected push. Only pull requests from the repository's own release branch count (a fork's pull request from a same-named branch is ignored), and a merged one is preferred over an open one, and an open one over a closed one. A pull request closed without merging now fails the attempt unless `main` already records the stable.

- Release lab: nightly catch-up now shows up everywhere once it lands. CI records each `catch-up-<lane>.txt` marker on the `car-releases` mirror as well as the primary, so `verify-release.sh --scope full` no longer fails on `asset count mismatch` after every lane succeeded. `--scope mac` reports each lane as `DONE catch-up attached`, `PEND catch-up pending`, or `FAIL catch-up failed` instead of always saying pending. A finished nightly's run record now gets its catch-up states written back and republished, both on `car-release status` and every ten minutes from the notices drain.

- Release lab: the catch-up sync now reads every nightly in its window. It used to look at only the ten newest runs of any kind, so after a busy stretch of updates and promotions a recent nightly's run record could stay `pending` until it aged out. When `car-release status` and the notices drain sync the same nightly at once, neither overwrites the newer catch-up states the other wrote: a sync whose read is overtaken leaves the record to the other one, and a publish that fails keeps the newer states; both say so on stderr. A failed run-record publish no longer replaces a log link another publish has just written with the `car-release log` fallback. When GitHub does not answer, the sync stops after the first failed read instead of waiting out a timeout for every nightly.

- Release lab: every nightly or promotion that does not finish DONE now reports itself once, with one Teams post and one GitHub issue. This covers a failed step, a failure while planning (before a commit or build id exists, which used to break the notice renderer and stay silent), an interrupted run, and a run found dead by the next command (the 2026-10-17 02:30Z scheduled nightly, which nobody announced). A step name missing from the name table now gets the generic label "an unnamed step" instead of crashing the notifier. Failing registry clones and run-record clones or pushes now log the real error line.

- Release lab: a missing `actions: write` grant on the lab GitHub App now shows as a `WARN ... needs-person` row in `lab-health` instead of a failure, so it no longer blocks `car-release update` or the nightly. Only the `promote_tag` recovery dispatch needs it, and `lab-promote` still stops with a message naming `actions: write` when it is missing.

- Release lab: full `lab-health.sh` now runs a suite listed twice in `scripts/ci/release-lab-tests.d` (or in `CAR_LAB_HEALTH_TESTS`) twice, as the CI runner does. It used to skip the repeat silently, so a suite whose second run fails could pass health and then fail CI.

- Release lab: retrying a nightly that failed after its tag was pushed now finishes it, when retried on the UTC day of its version or the next day. After that the planner decides again, so a version that cannot be resumed never blocks later nightlies. Before this change the retry asked the planner again, the planner answered `already-cut` because today's tag existed, and the nightly stayed half-published until someone deleted the tag by hand. The retry now resumes the recorded plan from its step ledger. It checks that origin's tag still names the recorded commit and Build-Id, puts back the archived macOS files instead of rebuilding them, skips the publishing steps that already finished, and runs the Mac-side verifier again. If the tag has moved to another commit, the retry ends `REFUSED tag-mismatch` and names both commits.
- Release lab: `car-release status` now shows when the Linux and Windows catch-up builds are attached to a nightly. Its `Catch-up:` line reads each lane from the release's assets (`attached`, `failed` or `pending`), separately from the `Mac side:` line. Before this change the line read `pending` forever.

- Release lab: when a promotion's promote-publish job fails, `lab-promote` now dispatches the `promote_tag` recovery itself (and fails naming `actions: write` if the lab App lacks that permission); `lab-health` gains a "GitHub Actions write" row.

- Release lab: `lab-promote` no longer hides a failed GitHub read. During `wait-ci`, a read that fails (the markers, the tag run, the `promote-publish` check, the dispatch listing, a job) was taken as "no answer yet": a failed tag-run read meant no recovery was dispatched, and the operator saw only the marker timeout an hour later. Each failure is now printed as `wait-ci: <read> failed (exit <N>: <last two lines of its error>)`, cleaned and redacted like admission's `remote-failed`. An answer waiting cannot change fails the step naming the read (GitHub's missing-grant 403 at once; HTTP 401 or a run read's 404 when the same read repeats it on the next poll); a 5xx, a rate limit, a network failure or a release read's 404 keeps polling to the deadline, whose timeout names it. A recovery dispatch refused with any 403 other than GitHub's missing-grant answer is no longer blamed on `actions: write`, and one that failed on a server error or a lost connection says it may still have gone through; a failed npm or PyPI read is reported as unreadable rather than as a stale latest; a failed read of whether the stable release exists, or of its assets, stops the upload naming the read. `lab-health` reports a GitHub read as `read refused` only when GitHub's answer says so, and any other failure as `read failed` with its status and message; a failed run listing no longer passes the "GitHub Actions jobs" row as "no run exists" (car#2940).

- Release-lab promotions now run the changelog assembly script from the lab's own checkout instead of the promoted nightly's frozen copy, so a fix to promotion tooling reaches a promotion of an older nightly.

- Release lab promotions now refuse an unpromotable nightly before anything starts. `car-release promote` checks the nightly before taking the run lock or creating a run record, prints one named reason (`REFUSED too-old`, `not-lab-built`, `catch-up-missing`, `run-failed`, `run-unfinished`, and the rest) followed by `nothing was started for <nightly>`, and exits 1; before, the refusal came from inside a started run and the command exited 0 with `no plan was recorded`. During a promotion `car-release status` now names the nightly being promoted, and every finished promotion shows `Promoted from:` and `Verifier: pass|fail|pending`. CI now records `npm-latest-moved.txt` and `pypi-latest-moved.txt` on the `car-releases` stable release as well as the primary, and the lab waits for both before verifying, so the primary and mirror stable releases list the same assets and the stable verifier's equal-count check can pass.

- A release lab promotion is reported `already done` only for the nightly it actually promoted. Before, `car-release promote <nightly>` exited 0 with `already done: <stable> published and verified` whenever any nightly of the same version had finished promoting, so a request for a different nightly reported success without checking or promoting it. That request now goes to admission, which refuses it with `REFUSED already-published: <stable> exists, promoted from <nightly>`, prints `nothing was started for <nightly>`, and exits 1 (car#2830).
- A release lab promotion no longer skips steps that a promotion of a different nightly of the same version completed. Before, a promotion that failed after `relabel` and before `tag` let a later promotion of another nightly of that version skip `preflight`, `fetch` and `relabel`, although that work lived in the first nightly's cache. A promotion now reuses a step only from an earlier run of the same source nightly (car#2830).
- `car-release promote` no longer waits forever when GitHub or the vault stops answering during admission. Each `git ls-remote origin` tag read is limited to 30 seconds and the whole admission to 120 seconds; past either limit the request prints `REFUSED remote-timeout: …` or `REFUSED admission-timeout: …`, `nothing was started for <nightly>`, and exits 1, and the timed-out checks' processes are stopped (car#2830).

- `car-release promote` now names a remote read that fails at once instead of blaming the nightly. A `git ls-remote origin` or GitHub read that fails with a DNS, authentication, transport or server error is refused as `REFUSED remote-failed: <read> failed (exit <N>: <last two error lines>)`, followed by `nothing was started for <nightly>` and exit 1. The error has control characters removed and secrets redacted before it reaches `car-release status` or a notice. Before, a failed nightly tag read was reported as `not-lab-built`, a failed release read as `catch-up-missing`, and a failed stable read as "stable absent", which could admit a promotion the already-published check should have stopped. Only an HTTP 404 from GitHub now counts as absent, and only after admission has confirmed that the token can read the repository, since GitHub answers 404 for a repository it hides too (car#3183).

- Release lab: the schedule command no longer runs code the `car-release` service account can change as root. `setup-lab.sh` now installs a root-owned copy at `/usr/local/car-lab/sbin/car-lab-schedule.sh`, and the runbook runs `sudo /bin/bash /usr/local/car-lab/sbin/car-lab-schedule.sh on|off|show` instead of the copy in the service checkout. As root, `schedule.sh` refuses before doing anything unless its own file and every directory above it, on the invoked and the resolved path, are owned by root with no group or world write bit and no ACL entry granting write, and the refusal names the path. Root also takes every command from the system directories, never the caller's PATH, and reads and writes `schedule.json` and publishes it only as `car-release`. Health warns, without failing, when the copy is missing or stale. Rerun `bash scripts/lab/setup-lab.sh` as the rental administrator to install the copy; until then the old checkout command refuses with the path to use.

- Release lab: a scheduled nightly now runs where a manual one does, in `car-release`'s Background launchd context. The first scheduled tick ran as a `car-release` job in the system domain, where `nohup` cannot adopt a session; its detached runner never started and the run was recorded `INTERRUPTED planning`. The nightly LaunchDaemon now starts as root only to enter that context with `launchctl asuser` and `sudo -u car-release`, sets `AbandonProcessGroup` so the run outlives the scheduler's exit, and has launchd open no file as root. `setup-lab.sh` is now the plist's only writer: `schedule.sh on` used to rewrite it with different bytes, which made every later `car-release update` fail its setup check. The scheduler also refuses to start a run outside that context. `car-release update` cannot deliver this change: an administrator must run `setup-lab.sh` from a setup checkout at this commit, then `schedule.sh off` and `schedule.sh on`.

- Release lab: log excerpts and notices now redact Azure client secrets (the `~`-bearing secret format) and `*_CLIENT_SECRET=` / `*SECRET*=` assignments before they reach a Teams post or GitHub issue, and the notice gate refuses text that still holds one.

- Release lab: setup now installs `cmake`, which the Mac build needs to compile whisper.cpp (the first lab nightly failed at mac-build because it was missing). Setup prints its version, the lab health command reports it, and a release refuses before any build step, naming `cmake` and the `brew install cmake` fix, if it is absent.

- Release lab: when `car-release status` cannot read a nightly's release from GitHub (an outage, a timeout, a refused token), its `Catch-up:` line now ends with `(recorded states; GitHub could not be read)` and stderr says `car-release: could not read <tag> catch-up from GitHub (<gh error>)`. It used to show the states stored in the run record as if they were current, so lanes whose files had landed read as pending. That message and the catch-up sync's own now redact any secret in gh's error before printing it.
- CI promotion: `promote-publish`'s wait for `promotion-assets-complete.txt` no longer treats a release it could not read (a refused mirror token, an outage) as one without the marker. The poll line shows `could not read (<gh error>)`, redacted, and the final error says the release could not be read instead of that the marker is still missing.

- Release lab: `car-release status` no longer takes over a minute. It checked every stable tag ever published (86 GitHub calls, about 33 s) for each nightly, rounded each vault read up to a whole second, and ran them one after another; it now reads only the tag that can belong to the nightly, polls in tenths of a second, and does the vault reads and each nightly's GitHub reads at the same time. A failed `car-release update` now shows the lines of the step that failed instead of an empty "Step log" block. `scripts/lab/check-release-actors.sh` no longer passes a window in which a person uploaded release assets to a mirror release whose `created_at` is its commit date.

- A stable promotion no longer fails because the release lab verified the Windows installer before CI had rebuilt it. The lab's `wait-ci` step now also waits for `build.yml`'s `promote-windows-installer` job and runs `verify` only after it passes. The job that counts is the newest run of it for the stable: a `promote_tag` recovery dispatch counts, and `build.yml` now names such a run `promote vX.Y.Z` so a dispatch for another stable never does. A failed job, or a tag whose `build.yml` lacks the job, fails `wait-ci` at once naming the run and the recovery; a job still running at the deadline fails it with a timeout naming the job. Each GitHub read in the wait is bounded by `CAR_LAB_PROMOTE_GH_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (default 60) and an error counts as still pending (car#2932).

- The car.parslee.ai landing page headline now reads "The runtime a business runs its agents on. On machines it owns."

- car.parslee.ai install section: the full `scoop bucket add` command now wraps inside its box instead of being cut off, and the macOS and Windows download buttons line up on one row on desktop.

- The car.parslee.ai landing page now offers the Mac installer (`CAR-darwin-arm64.pkg`, for Apple Silicon Macs) as a direct download beside the Windows one, and the run-history access note in the WebSocket reference no longer cites an internal tracking id.

- An answer to an organization's message from one of this owner's other devices is now admitted only from that organization and only to the device that sent it. Before, a session in another organization could reuse the message's id to reach an address no listing showed it. `lattice.wait` likewise returns a reply pushed to an attached agent, or a person's answer found by address, only in the collaboration context it was asked from (the same organization under any member account, or the same personal account), so an agent id enrolled again in another context does not read the old one's replies (#2807).

- `lattice.wait` finds a reply pushed to an organization's agent again when the asker waits under another member account of the same organization. Since #2908 it compared the whole work context, account included, so an admitted reply could go unfound and the wait timed out with no error. A reply pushed into another organization or a personal context still stays invisible. Rejoining the Lattice under another member account of the same organization also no longer releases the claims that address holds in the organization's claim table (#2909).

- `mail.messages` with `all_mailboxes: true` now names what it did not read when it reads nothing. A locked Envelope Index used to come back `available: false` with `unresolved` empty; it now lists every mailbox of the selected accounts (`timed_out: true`), still without reading any message through Apple Events. Naming them takes one Apple Events walk of the mailbox names, bounded by 2 s (or `timeout_ms` if lower); if it does not finish, each requested account is listed with `mailbox: null`. When Mail reports it cannot list the accounts' mailboxes, each requested account is listed with `mailbox: null` too. With no `account_ids` in either of those whole-account cases there is no account to name, so `unresolved` stays empty.
- `assistant.identity.set` refuses an `apps` list of more than 32 distinct names, and a `role` or app name longer than 120 characters, the way it already refused too many spellings. Both go into every built-in system prompt, and the list had no limit. `car_identity::AssistantIdentity::with_role` and `with_apps` now return `Result`.
- `car_bench::ensemble_eval::analyze` refuses records it used to call interpretable: an arm whose players had no time (80 minutes against 0 passed the budget check), more than one depth player, two roster entries with one identity, and a finding timed after its player's session ended. Each is a new blocking `Integrity` variant.
- `car_harness_opt::search` no longer exceeds its evaluation budget when `budget` is not larger than `restarts`: a later restart's start is scored only out of a non-zero share. The seed is always scored, so a `budget` of 0 spends one evaluation, as documented on `SearchConfig::budget`.
- Skill evolution from recorded outcomes retries after a failed model call. A rate limit or network error used to count as an attempt, so the domain was not tried again until another outcome arrived.
- The `mcp_smoke` example fails when the unpoliced call returns no result, instead of printing `RESULT: direction 2 OK`.

- `car install`, `car uninstall`, `car start`, `car stop`, `car restart`, `car approvals default|set-tool|reset-tool`, and `car do`'s registration with CarHost (the older-daemon fallback and `car do --serve --governed-host`) now present CAR's local host key only after the person at the terminal answers yes (`... with CAR's permission? [y/N]`, asked on stderr), the rule `car agent new` follows. With the new `-y`/`--yes` flag or with no terminal (stdin and stderr), they never present the key, so a daemon with a host token refuses them and says why; a daemon with no host token still admits them. With `CAR_AGENT_ID` or `CAR_AGENT_TOKEN` set they never present it either. An agent with a shell tool can no longer install, start, stop or remove agents, or change approval policy, through CAR's own CLI by borrowing the host key. The Windows tray dashboard's agent Start / Stop / Restart buttons and its setup wizard's approval preset now go out on the tray's own host-authenticated connection instead of running `car start|stop|restart` and `car approvals default`. When `car do` cannot register Parslee Core through an older daemon (refused, declined, unreachable) it now says so on stderr and continues. The create-car-agent skill tells an agent to ask the user to start, stop or install an agent (CarHost, or their own terminal) rather than run those commands itself. CarHost, CAR's boot-time registration of Parslee Core, `car claude`, `car models`, `car approvals get|evaluate-tool` and the SDKs are unchanged. (#2444)

- On Linux, CAR now locks every agent it starts into its own folder with Landlock, a feature of the Linux kernel that CAR applies itself when it starts the agent: nothing to install, no extra users, no Docker or host setting, with or without root, in the stock container or on a server running `car-server` directly. An agent can read and write only `<CAR_HOME>/agents/<id>/data/` (its working folder, `HOME` and `TMPDIR`), read its own code and `manifest.toml`, and run the system's ordinary programs; CAR's token files, key store, state folder and every other agent's folder are refused by the kernel, and so is everything the agent starts. This needs Linux 6.2 or newer with Landlock enabled (Ubuntu 24.04 and current cloud images qualify; Debian 12 and the original Ubuntu 22.04 kernel do not), so that an agent cannot empty other files by truncating them. Where the kernel cannot fully enforce the wall, the agent does not start: it shows `errored` with the reason "This server's Linux kernel can't confine agents to their own folders", and is not retried. The daemon logs the kernel's Landlock ABI at start, and which extras are active: on Linux 6.12 or newer an agent also cannot signal processes outside its wall or connect to abstract Unix sockets, and on Linux 7.1 or newer it cannot connect to pathname Unix sockets outside its folder (on older kernels a same-user program on a Unix socket, such as tmux or `systemd --user`, could act for an agent; our Docker image has none; keeping such programs off the server is the server owner's job: run CAR as its own system user with nothing else under it, or use the Docker image). Agents also get `/dev/shm` read-write, because Python `multiprocessing` (and likely Chromium) cannot work without it; other agents' named shared-memory objects are visible there. A `cwd` outside the agent's own folder is refused, and no path in an agent's manifest (`args`, `cwd`, `command`) grants access to anything outside its folder and the system paths; a `command` outside its folder and `/usr` (including `/usr/local`), `/nix/store` and the other system paths is refused. The agent ids `parslee-core` and `car-assistant` are reserved for CAR's own assistant: `agents.upsert` and bundle install refuse them. The Mac is unchanged. The built-in assistant's shell on Linux now also hides CAR's state folder (except the folder it was asked to work in), and runs under Landlock alone where the `unshare` sandbox cannot start (the stock container), where it used to be refused, and on Linux 7.1 or newer that fallback also refuses connecting to a Unix socket by path outside its allowed folders (a path session bus included). Still open on Linux, and closed by a separate step: an agent can reach CAR's own dashboard and MCP ports on `127.0.0.1`. See `docs/proposals/cloud-agent-isolation.md` (step 3).

- Security (Linux): files and folders the secret sandbox hides from a command, such as `$CAR_HOME/env` with the operator's provider keys and the daemon's socket and token folders, now stay hidden whatever other folders the sandbox locks down. Before, on Linux, a hidden file or folder inside a folder the sandbox also made read-only, such as `$CAR_HOME` itself, could be read by the sandboxed command. The sandbox now hides files and folders after it has set up every other folder, and starts the command from its working folder as seen after that setup. macOS was not affected. The Linux CI `test` job and the release `test` job now enable unprivileged user namespaces with `scripts/ci-enable-linux-sandbox.sh` and run the secret sandbox's tests with the sandbox on, so those tests run on Linux rather than skip.

- Linux: a sandboxed CarCoder check whose working folder sits inside the CAR state folder, such as a session worktree under `$CAR_HOME/coder/worktrees`, can now write to that worktree and to its own evidence and scratch folders. Before, the Linux sandbox made the whole state folder read-only and ignored these per-check exceptions, which macOS already honored, so such a check could not write its own files. The rest of the state folder stays read-only, folders CAR keeps immutable stay read-only even inside a worktree, and hidden files stay hidden.

- CarCoder on Linux: a path the sandbox policy named, such as a state root or a protected directory, can be deleted between building the policy and the sandbox reaching it, and so can the whole directory that holds it. A mount step whose target is provably gone no longer fails setup (exit 125, "mount point does not exist", which reached users as "Cargo PATH probe returned no closing delimiter"). "Provably gone" means the nearest existing ancestor is a directory that lists cleanly and lacks the next component on the way down. This covers the phase-1 mounts, the phase-2 blocks and the explicit read-only remounts of the policy's read-only paths. The check runs `command ls` with `QUOTING_STYLE=literal LC_ALL=C`, so `ls` output formatting variables and shell functions cannot change the verdict, and a path with a `.` or `..` component is never gone. A path that merely cannot be checked (an I/O error on a network home, an unreadable or non-directory ancestor), a mount that fails with its target present, and the read-only remount of host mounts found in mountinfo (which still fails closed) all still fail setup (#3017).

- Linux: CarCoder's sandboxed commands no longer fail with "car: secret sandbox setup failed" (exit 125) while a Docker container is running on the same machine. Before a command runs, the sandbox makes every mount it can reach read-only. A running container's filesystem sits under Docker's root-only directory, where the sandbox cannot reach it, so making it read-only was refused. The sandbox now skips a mount only when the folder above it cannot be entered; the command inside cannot enter that folder either. Any other mount it cannot find or cannot make read-only still stops the command.
- Linux: the sandbox now reads mount paths with escaped characters correctly. A mount whose name has a space followed by a digit (such as a drive called "Backup 2024" or "Disk 7") or ends with a newline was read under the wrong name. The command then stopped with a setup failure even though the drive was reachable. Such mounts are now found and made read-only like any other.

- Linux: CarCoder's independent verifier now runs isolated on Linux in CAR's default layout, so a headless `car code` run on Linux can deliver. Before, when the verifier's frozen copy and evidence folder sat inside the CAR state folder, which is where CAR puts them, Linux reported verifier isolation as unavailable: `run_check` refused to run and headless delivery was blocked. The Linux sandbox now hides the state folder from the verifier and gives back only those two folders, as macOS already did. Everything else in the state folder stays hidden. The folders above them can be passed through but not listed, and inside a write-confined sandbox the folders given back are writable only where the sandbox already allowed writes. As on macOS, the verifier's shell on Linux also no longer sees the shared `/tmp` and `/var/tmp` folders, and each `run_check` keeps its private state in a short folder of its own under `/tmp`.

- Unattended self-healing's local models no longer refuse each other. Under a local-only spend scope, where there is no remote route to fall back to, a local model that fits the Local Models RAM allocation on its own is no longer refused because an idle local model from an earlier request still holds memory. CAR releases the smallest idle local model that makes room, or several largest first, and only when releasing them can actually make room. A model starting up or serving a request is never released. Everywhere else, a refusal at the allocation still sends the router to its next route, as before. The self-heal review panel also asks its local reviewers one at a time. Before this, every reviewer was refused beside the coder that had just finished, so a fix the local coder made could never pass review.
- A contract check whose own `assert` compares the claimed value (`python3 -c "from greet import greet; assert greet(7) == 'Hello, 7'"`) now counts as asserting it. Before, it was held as asserting nothing, which could leave a contract with no runnable check.

- Local model memory estimates now match what models actually use, measured through vllm-mlx on an M5 Pro. Before, Qwen3.8-27B was estimated at 51 GiB for a 64k-token context that peaks at 24 GiB, so the Everyday preset refused it past about 9k tokens. GLM-4.7-Flash 8-bit was estimated at 45 GiB for a 64k context that peaks at 53 GiB, so CAR admitted loads the machine could not hold. Four changes:
  - CAR now reads a model's `config.json` from wherever it is actually served, including the Hugging Face cache snapshot that `ensure_local` reuses. Before, those rows fell back to a parameter-count guess.
  - Layers that keep no per-token cache (Qwen3.5+ linear attention) are no longer counted. Their fixed state is counted once.
  - Multi-head latent attention models (GLM-4.7-Flash, DeepSeek-V3) are sized by their compressed cache plus the memory needed to expand it while the prompt is processed.
  - Models served with a drafter count the full-vocabulary logits of a 2048-token prompt chunk, which mlx-vlm computes (2.8 GiB on Qwen3.8).
  
  The two rows' `ram_mb` is now their measured resident size. Every estimate is within 6 GiB above the measured peak and never below it.

- The model catalog carries re-measured local and frontier scores (car-judged suite, M5 Pro 64 GB, 2026-10-26). Judged tasks now run with `thinking: auto`, as production requests do; before, the suite asked reasoning models not to think. Best local quality: gpt-oss-20b 0.971 at 77.6 tok/s, Qwen3.6-35B-A3B 0.893 at 92.3 tok/s, Qwen3.8-27B 0.887 at 33.9 tok/s (with its drafter). Every native frontier row except the two Gemini rows now has a car-judged score and a measured answer rate, so `prefer_quality` ranks frontier models against each other on one benchmark instead of on each vendor's own (Sonnet 4.6 and the GPT-5.6 rows score 1.0, Opus 4.8 0.986, which is the suite's ceiling, not a tie in ability).
- The Gemma-4 rows serve Google's QAT 4-bit conversions; they pointed at full-precision repos whose listed sizes were about 4x too small. New rows: Gemma-4-31B, gpt-oss-20b, Devstral-Small-2-24B and Qwen3.5 4B/2B/0.8B. The 2B and 0.8B score 0.18 and 0.05 on the judged suite and suit only trivial tasks.

- A saved agent can now keep everything it reads on this Mac. The Agent Builder's privacy step has a "Keep everything this agent reads on this Mac" switch (off by default, including for every starter), and a chat draft can carry it as a `local_only` yes/no answer that needs the person's own words. It is stored as `builder_draft.local_only` (default `false`, omitted when off, so existing agents keep their JSON, fingerprint and behaviour); the runtime reads that field, never the free-text privacy answer, and an edit started without answers keeps the agent's stored ones. For such an agent, every model call that sees what it reads uses a model on this Mac: writing and repairing its spec during the build (the repair quotes its test cases' output), its test cases, `declagents.run_scenarios`, and every run. An unaddressed call routes with every model not on this Mac strictly excluded, so a cloud model that would score higher is never picked. A build is refused before any model call, with a "configuration" failure that names the keep-on-this-Mac setting and says to install or pick a model on this Mac or turn the setting off, when the model chosen to build it or the agent's saved model isn't on this Mac, or when no model on this Mac can build it; a run stops with an error naming the setting in the same cases, or when its saved model on this Mac isn't available (Parslee never stands in). The build's final check calls no verifier or auditor model, so a Mail agent's real Mail no longer reaches a model elsewhere during the build: the agent is saved as not independently checked ("It keeps everything on this Mac, so no AI model outside it reviewed the build."), never as passed, the session's `verification` receipt carries `kept_on_this_mac: true`, and CarHost shows that reason instead of saying no second AI model was available. "On this Mac" is a model CAR runs on-device (MLX, Candle GGUF, a CAR-managed vLLM-MLX, Apple's on-device model); an Ollama or external vLLM-MLX endpoint does not count, since it may be on another machine.
- `IntentHint` gains `on_device_only` (default `false`; accepted by `models.route`, `routeModel`/`route_model` intent JSON and generation requests). It is a hard constraint: the request is routed only with models CAR runs on this machine, checked against the registry it is routed with when it is sent (so a model added to the catalog later is never picked), a requested model elsewhere is refused, and with none left it fails with "no eligible model remains after required exclusions" before anything is sent. A local-only agent sets it on every model call.

- The model catalog scores five more local models on the 17-task `car-judged` suite: `vllm-mlx/glm-4.7-flash:8bit` 0.402 (its earlier run errored on every task) and `apple/foundation:default` 0.282, both new, plus fresh runs of `mlx/qwen3-30b-a3b:4bit` (0.470 over two runs), `mlx/qwen3-8b:4bit` (0.439) and `mlx/qwen3-4b:4bit` (0.370 over two runs). `vllm-mlx/gemma-4-31b-it:4bit` and `vllm-mlx/devstral-small-2-24b:4bit` stay unscored: 6 and 4 of their 17 tasks hit a 300 s request timeout, fixed separately. `vllm-mlx/gpt-oss-20b:mxfp4` keeps 77.6 tok/s; its recorded calibration (42.2) was taken under load.

- Lattice: a daemon started with a loopback `--a2a-bind` (`127.0.0.1`, `::1`, `localhost`) no longer announces itself over mDNS, because nothing else on the local network can reach it, and logs that once at startup. A daemon on the same machine pairs with it through `a2a.peers.add <url>`: `lattice.knock` now falls back to that registry, by label, slug or URL, when no discovered CAR matches, and holds the target to the same address rule a receiver applies to a knocker's address (a LAN address, or loopback only from a CAR on loopback). Bind a LAN address to be discoverable on the local network. A loopback-URL record that another host advertises (an older CAR) is no longer listed, since knocking it would reach the knocker's own machine. A loopback CAR that knocks a peer on the LAN is told its A2A URL isn't on the local network, instead of the knock being refused silently over there. `a2a.peers.add` and `a2a.peers.remove` are now operator-only: a session bound to a supervised agent is refused, since the registry names what the host knocks on. Every knock target, discovered or registered, must meet that address rule before it is dialled. `a2a.peers.add` also accepts the documented `allowUntrusted` spelling; `allow_untrusted` keeps working.

- Workers start on macOS. `workers.start`, and the install and start of an orchestrator (a manifest with `[[workers]]`), are admitted on a Mac where CAR's file wall applies, and refused with `fence_unavailable` where it does not; Windows still refuses. On a Mac the wall is the same plan the Linux wall enforces with Landlock, applied with Seatbelt (`/usr/bin/sandbox-exec`): a worker's `workers/<handle>/home/` is read-only, `home/data/` is its only writable folder, its bundle root is read-only, and the system paths a Mac program needs are readable (program prefixes such as `/usr` and `/opt/homebrew` by their program parts only, so a Homebrew service's `var/` data and `etc/` configuration are not); CAR's token files, its key store, the user's home, other agents' and workers' folders and the rest of `CAR_HOME` are refused. System services are closed as well: every Mach service is refused except the five a command-line program needs (user and group names, certificate trust, proxy and DNS configuration, the temp folder path), so Spotlight, the Keychain, the pasteboard, the window server, screen capture and preferences are unreachable; Unix sockets outside its own `data/`, hard links and clones outside it (a readable file cannot be given a second name there), signals outside its own sandbox, the process table through all three of its doors (`process-info`, the `kern.proc` sysctls that `ps` reads, and `proc_listpids`), Apple Events, launchd jobs, Launch Services opens, preference reads and writes through `cfprefsd`, IOKit drivers and registry properties (the serial number), the system keychain files under `/Library/Keychains` and the system logs under `/Library/Logs` are refused, and a worker gets no POSIX shared memory or named semaphore. **System V IPC is open and must be assumed to be a data channel between labels:** Seatbelt has no rule that refuses creation, so `shmget`, `semget` and `msgget` succeed for every binary and leave system-wide objects the worker cannot remove; attaching to or opening another label's existing object was refused for every binary measured (Apple's and Homebrew's `python3`, a C program), and a binary nobody measured must be assumed a channel (Linux's Landlock does not mediate System V IPC either). What else stays open or limited, measured on macOS 26: TCP and UDP, `sysctl` reads other than `kern.proc.*`, the hardware UUID (`gethostuuid`), signal 0 to any process of the user, the rest of `/Library`, the five services, `mktemp` and `mktemp -t NAME` with no path (they use the user's shared temp folder and fail; `mktemp "$TMPDIR/x.XXXXXX"`, `mktemp -d "$TMPDIR/d.XXXXXX"` and `mktemp -p "$TMPDIR"` work, as do Python's `tempfile` and node's `os.tmpdir()`), and bun's `os.tmpdir()`, which reports `/tmp`, where its writes fail. The start-up self-test covers the file rules, the service deny and the clone rule; every other rule is covered by the test suite only. The orchestrator that starts workers is walled the same way and, like every walled agent on Linux, is held to the daemon's walled-agent default-deny and never handed the daemon token; a walled Mac agent left running by an earlier daemon is never adopted at boot but stopped and started afresh, walled. Every other Mac agent is unchanged. At daemon start a self-test runs a probe under the real rendered profile and checks that it cannot read a token-shaped file outside its folder, another worker's data, or the Spotlight service, cannot clone its readable bundle file into its own folder, cannot write its own read-only folder or bundle root, and can read its bundle root and write its own `data/`; workers are admitted only after it passed, and a daemon that itself runs inside a Seatbelt sandbox (which macOS cannot nest) refuses workers with that reason. The verdict is the one Linux gets, file wall only: there is still no network fence, and every surface showing a worker keeps saying so. A Mac has no per-agent init, so a walled worker's tree is ended by process group. The design's decision 20 is amended accordingly (`docs/proposals/orchestrator-workers.md`).

- `mail.messages` with `include_body` on macOS now spends ONE 10 s body budget on an INBOX read from Mail's Envelope Index, covering every account's body lookups from the index together: each account's lookups get only what the earlier accounts left (rows for an account read through Apple Events get their bodies in that read, outside this budget), where before every account started a fresh 10 s, so four slow accounts could hold one call for about 40 s. The budget still cannot interrupt work already running, so a call can take the budget plus at most one lookup or one account/mailbox resolution already running. A Mail.app row whose body was asked for but not returned now carries `body_unavailable`, a sentence saying why (the budget was spent, Mail found no message with that id, Mail could not read it or did not answer, or the message at that id has a different Message-ID) and, where it helps, to fetch it with `mail.message_body`; before, such a row showed only `body: null`, the same as a row whose body was never requested. This covers Apple Events rows whose `content()` fails, too. The field is absent when the body came back or was not asked for.

- `mail.messages` rows carry `cc`, the CC addresses in header order, beside `recipients` (still the To line only, so existing callers keep their meaning). CC recipients were dropped entirely before, a regression against reading Mail.app through `osascript` (Parslee-ai/car-releases#92). Mail.app, the Envelope Index and Microsoft Graph all fill it.
- `mail.messages` reads a filed mailbox (not only INBOX) from Mail's Envelope Index when Full Disk Access allows, so `limit` bounds the work: a 10-row read of a 117,000-message All Mail no longer snapshots the whole folder through Apple Events and outlasts the 15 s bound (Parslee-ai/car-releases#93). Without the index, or for a mailbox the index has no row for, the Apple Events path is unchanged.
- `mail.messages` takes `all_mailboxes: true` to read every mailbox of the selected accounts in one call, merged newest first inside the backend and deduped by Message-ID within each account (the same message in two accounts stays one row per account). Mailboxes it could not read in time are listed in a new `unresolved: [{account_id, mailbox, reason, timed_out}]` rather than left silently out. Passing both `mailbox` and `all_mailboxes` is an invalid-params error. On Microsoft Graph it reads `/me/messages`.
- `mail.messages` takes `timeout_ms` (1,000–120,000; default 15,000) to bound each Mail.app read. `car mail messages` gains `--all-mailboxes` and `--timeout-ms`.
- A Mail.app (or any JXA-backed) call that does not finish in time and used to fail with error code `backend` now fails with `timeout` (Rust: the new `IntegrationError::Timeout` variant), so callers can skip one slow mailbox without parsing the message; calls that already reported a timeout as `available: false` (most `notes.*` and `reminders.*` reads, Microsoft Graph requests) are unchanged. Once the app has answered earlier in the same process, the message no longer tells the caller to check automation permission, which that answer makes unlikely.
- The daemon runs every `mail.*` method on a blocking thread, so a long Mail.app read no longer occupies a Tokio worker.

- `car mail send` (and `mail.send`, the assistant's `mail_draft`/`mail_send` tools, and the Node/Python `mail_send` bindings) on the Mail.app backend now drafts or sends from the account named by `account_id` even when it is not Mail's default account. Mail ignores a sender set after the message is created, so a draft for any other account used to fail with `sender_override_failed`; the sender is now set when the message is created and the draft lands in that account's own Drafts. When Mail will not use that address the call still refuses — nothing is drafted or sent from another account — and the reason says what to change in Mail's account settings; `draft_only` for an account Mail reports turned off, or for which Mail lists Drafts mailboxes for other accounts but none for it, refuses with `drafts_unavailable` before anything is made (when Mail cannot say, the draft goes ahead), and with an `account_id`, a Mail too slow to reach the save within 8 s gives up without saving. A display name with commas or quotes is quoted in the sender. Mail can leave an autosaved copy of the message in Drafts: usually empty, or with the message if Mail moved the sender while it was being filled in (beside a saved draft, or after a refusal); CAR doesn't remove it. The `mailSend` / `mail_send` / `mail.send` docs now describe the real result shape.

- `mail.inbox` no longer reports an Exchange (Microsoft 365) account's inbox as empty in Mac Mail when it reads through Apple Events — when Mail's Envelope Index cannot be read, or is readable but has no INBOX row for that account. The overview now finds the inbox the same way `mail.messages` does, so an inbox Mail names `Inbox` gets the same total, unread count and newest subject that search sees, and it reads the counts without asking Mail for every message.
- `mail.inbox` never reports an inbox it could not read as `total: 0`. A selected account whose INBOX cannot be found or read is left out of `summaries` and listed in a new top-level `unresolved: [{account_id, reason}]`: a missing inbox carries the reason `mail.messages` gives, and a failed read carries Mail's error. `unresolved` is omitted when every account read, so the reply's shape is unchanged. When no selected account reads, `mail.inbox` returns `available: false` naming each account, and an `account_ids` filter that matches no account returns `available: false`, as `mail.mailboxes` and `mail.messages` do. The assistant's `mail_inbox` tool tells the model that an `unresolved` account's counts are unknown, not zero.

- On macOS, `mail.inbox` reading through Apple Events no longer reports an inbox as empty or fully read when Mail answers with no value or an incomplete one instead of an error. A missing message count, or (when Mail gives no unread count) a missing read-status reply, a message whose read status is missing, or a read-status reply that does not cover every message, now lists that account in `unresolved` with the reason `INBOX message count unreadable` or `INBOX unread count unreadable`, instead of returning a summary with `total: 0` or `unread: 0`. A newest message with no subject now gives `most_recent_subject: null` instead of the text `"null"`.
- On macOS, `mail.messages` reading through Apple Events no longer returns an empty listing when its per-message fallback cannot get the message count from Mail; it returns `available: false` with `Mail message count unreadable; retry the listing`. On that fallback a missing subject or sender is `null` instead of the text `"null"`.

- `mail.messages` on macOS reads INBOX rows from Mail's read-only Envelope Index (the store `mail.mailboxes` already reads counts from) instead of asking Mail.app for the whole INBOX through Apple Events. A Gmail INBOX label inside a large All Mail took 17 s warm and about 5 minutes cold, past the 15 s bound, and ignored `since`. The index query reads INBOX membership through Mail's own indexes and fetches details for at most `limit` rows, with or without `since`, and never asks Mail.app to load the whole mailbox. Gmail's INBOX label members are included and rows Mail has marked deleted are not. `include_body` fetches bodies only for the rows returned, one lookup each, starting no new lookup once a 10 s budget is spent (a single slow lookup can still reach the 15 s bound), and keeping `body: null` for a row Mail cannot find, a row past the budget, or one whose Message-ID does not match. An index that is locked or does not answer within its 2 s bound returns `available: false` with the reason rather than falling back to the slow read; any other index failure (no Full Disk Access, an older schema) keeps the Apple Events path. Every other mailbox keeps that path, with its date predicate, and an account the index has no INBOX row for is still read through Apple Events and merged newest-first. `mail.inbox`, which made the same whole-INBOX read plus one read-status event per message, now takes its counts from the same index rows `mail.mailboxes` reports and its subject from the newest INBOX row, with the same fallbacks.
- A JXA call abandoned at its 15 s bound now says that the scripted app (Mail.app, Messages.app, Notes.app, Reminders.app, Photos.app) may stay busy running the abandoned request, so later calls to it can time out too and callers should wait before retrying. Killing `osascript` does not cancel the Apple Event, and a stalled Mail.app read made every later Mail read time out. `car_automation::applescript` timeouts say the same about the app a script was sending Apple Events to.

- Write-confined coder commands (checks and builder shells) can no longer reach user-session services that would run a process outside the sandbox: the systemd user manager, rootless and desktop Docker and Podman, ssh-agent, gpg-agent, ssh connection masters, tmux, screen, an Emacs server and the X server. On Linux, `/tmp` and `/var/tmp` show only the command's own confined roots; `/run/user/<uid>`, `/run/screen` and the runtime directories `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, `SCREENDIR` and `TMUX_TMPDIR` name are hidden; `~/.docker`, `~/.colima`, `~/.rd`, `~/.lima`, `~/.screen` and the Podman machine directory are hidden; and the directories holding the sockets under `~/.ssh`, gpg's `S.*` sockets and the sockets `SSH_AUTH_SOCK`, `GPG_AGENT_INFO`, `TMUX`, `DOCKER_HOST`, `CONTAINER_HOST`, `DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS` and `VSCODE_IPC_HOOK_CLI` name are hidden (only the socket itself where that directory is HOME, top-level or meets a confined root); the command gets a private devpts instance and fresh `/dev/shm` and `/run/lock`, all re-read on every spawn; a mask that fails to mount refuses the command. A network-approved write-confined check runs under Landlock's abstract-socket scope, which the CAR binary applies inside the sandbox (so users with supplementary groups keep it, and a CAR binary in a read-denied `~/.car/bin` still works). Where the scope cannot apply (Linux before 6.12, CAR embedded without its binaries, or a binary replaced since start) it is refused before it runs, naming the cause: run it on Linux 6.12 or later with the CAR binary, or withdraw the network approval. That includes every check of a `car code` run with a supplied contract, whose checks are network-approved: on Linux before 6.12 each one is refused. On Linux and macOS a write-confined command no longer inherits the daemon's open descriptors (other than its check-run marker) or its controlling terminal. On macOS a write-confined command may connect to Unix sockets only under its confined roots and the system's name-resolution and log sockets, and cannot submit launchd jobs, open apps through Launch Services or send Apple Events; it can still reach loopback TCP services. This deliberately stops `systemctl --user`, rootless and desktop containers, agent-backed ssh and gpg, tmux, screen, X11 clients (including a check's own Xvfb), and on Linux reading other files in `/tmp`, ssh keys in a hidden `~/.ssh` and the gpg keyring, and on macOS `open`, Apple Events and launchd jobs inside write-confined commands; the A/B re-score and a copied rerun line keep them, and the general assistant's shell keeps agent-backed ssh and gpg, X11, the rest of `/tmp`, ssh keys, the gpg keyring and on macOS `open` and Apple Events (its own masks are listed in the `car do` assistant shell entry).

- **Breaking:** the daemon's MCP endpoint (`http://127.0.0.1:9102/mcp`) now requires a credential for anything that reads or changes daemon memory or starts an assistant run: the `memory_*` and `skill_*` tools, `policy_check`, `resources/list`, `resources/read`, `prompts/get`, `completion/complete`, and `assistant_start`/`assistant_poll`/`assistant_cancel`. Until now any local process could read and write the daemon's memory and start personal assistant runs there with no credential (car#3185). If you point Claude Desktop, Cursor, Codex or a script at 9102, add `Authorization: Bearer <contents of the auth-token file>` to its requests. The file is `~/Library/Application Support/ai.parslee.car/auth-token` on macOS, `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/ai.parslee.car/auth-token` (else `~/.config/ai.parslee.car/auth-token`) on Linux, or `$CAR_HOME/ai.parslee.car/auth-token` when `CAR_HOME` is set; it changes on every daemon launch. Without it those requests get HTTP `401` with that same instruction in the body, and nothing runs. If you launch external CLIs through `car-external-agents` `InvokeOptions` (or `agents.invoke_external`) against an endpoint you hold a token for, pass that token as `mcp_bearer`; it is sent as the CLI's `Authorization: Bearer`. The peer and Lattice tools (the plugin's `car-local-peers` entry), the plan checks (`verify`, `simulate`, `equivalent`, `optimize`) and the handshake still need no credential, and an uncredentialed caller is no longer shown the tools it cannot call. A daemon started with `--no-auth` keeps the old behavior, and so does Windows.
- CLIs the daemon spawns against its own MCP endpoint (`agents.invoke_external`, coder sessions and their Foreman farm-outs) now get a run credential minted for that run and revoked when it ends, presented as `X-Car-Agent-Id` plus `Authorization: Bearer`. A CLI started by the operator (a host session, the `car` CLI, CarHost, an operator's coder run) works on the daemon's shared memory as before. A CLI started by a supervised agent works on that agent's own memory — its own graph, or the namespace or organization graph its connection is bound to — and cannot start an assistant run. The daemon token is never written into a spawned CLI's MCP config or command line, and a CLI launched for a supervised agent no longer inherits `CAR_AUTH_TOKEN`, `CAR_AUTH_TOKEN_FILE` or `CAR_HOST_TOKEN` from the daemon's environment; Codex receives the run token in the `CAR_MCP_RUN_TOKEN` environment variable rather than on its command line. A supervised agent can also present its own unscoped `CAR_AGENT_TOKEN` with `X-Car-Agent-Id` to reach its own memory there.
- The MCP endpoint now also refuses a `Host` header naming anything other than `localhost` or an IP literal (`403 {"error":"host not allowed"}`), which stops a DNS-rebinding page from reaching it under another name.
- A peer message to the built-in assistant over the daemon's MCP endpoint now needs a credential too, since the assistant runs a turn on it; without one the tool call fails with the remedy and nothing is sent. It is sent as whom the credential names: the operator for the daemon token or an operator-started run, the agent for a supervised agent's token or a run it started. Messages to other peers are unchanged. The CAR plugin's `car-local-peers` entry now runs the new `car-mcp --local-peers`, a stdio forwarder to the endpoint that both Claude Code and Codex can launch: it presents a CAR-spawned CLI's run credential (never the operator's token), a supervised agent's own identity, or the daemon token read on every request. A CLI the daemon launches for a supervised agent (`agents.invoke_external`, a coder run it started and its Foreman workers, its own `foreman.run`) carries `CAR_AGENT_ID` even when nothing was minted for it (not yet across the fleet: a subtask a supervised agent farms to an enrolled peer (foreman.run distributed, or an agent-owned distributed coder session) runs on the peer under the peer's operator credentials; the dispatch carries no agent attribution), so the forwarder inside presents no operator token; the entry declares its variables in `env_vars` because Codex passes a stdio server only what it declares.
- A coder sandbox built outside the daemon — `car coder ab` — now denies the ports of the daemon the CLI talks to (from `CAR_DAEMON_URL` and `CAR_MCP_BIND`, defaults 9100–9102). Before, its sandbox could fetch the daemon's auth token from the dashboard's `/auth-token` route, which front doors 6c has since removed.

- Test reliability: `maintenance_raises_an_unchosen_memory_policy_on_evidence` picked its "too big for Everyday" model with a default checkpoint geometry while the concierge measured fit with the served one, so on a machine whose Hugging Face cache held some of the catalog rows it failed about 4 runs in 10, depending on which row it found first. It now asks the engine for the same geometry. No runtime behavior changes.

- `memory.query` (WebSocket) and `query_facts` (Node, Python, Swift) no longer report `score: 0` for a fact that matches the query but ranked below the seeded matches and was reached through a graph edge; every returned row's `score` is its own match strength, and only a row matching no query term scores 0. `memory_query` (MCP) now reports a match reached through an edge at its own match strength as `activation`, not the higher spread value, so it no longer pushes a better match out of the top `k`.

- `memory_query` (MCP) no longer reports `activation: 1.0` for every direct match. A matched entry now starts at how well it matches the query (0–1, the IDF-weighted share of query terms it contains), so an exact match scores above a one-word overlap and a client can threshold on `activation`; entries reached through graph edges still score below the match they came from. `memory.query` (WebSocket) and `query_facts` (Node, Python, Swift) now weight each match by the same strength when seeding PageRank, so a full match ranks and scores above a weak one; their `confidence` remains a share of PageRank mass within one response, and each row gains a `score` field: the hit's own match strength (0–1, 0 for a hit that matches no query term), which callers can threshold on. Both now consider at least `k` direct matches instead of a fixed five, so a request for more than five results can return matches no graph edge leads to.

- Six tests that failed in the merge queue on unrelated changes are now deterministic under load. No product behavior changes. `hf_cache` token tests ride out Linux's transient `Text file busy` when executing the fake program they just wrote. The late-cost "never answers" test waits for the retried lookup in real time before it pauses the clock. The scheduled-run forgery test gives its second agent the same schedule instead of one built a minute later. The stalled-generator deadline test no longer bounds the run at 200 ms of wall clock. The review-restore tests let the event drain's in-flight session writes finish before asserting that nothing else holds the session. The idle-builder-shell liveness test reads the session clock after the sandbox's setup has run, not before.

- Local MLX and vLLM-MLX models whose repository keeps its chat template in a separate `chat_template.jinja` now chat. CAR downloaded the config, tokenizer and weights by name and never that file, so these models installed and loaded, then failed every request: vLLM-MLX answered HTTP 500 with "tokenizer.chat_template is not set". GLM-4.7-Flash, gemma-4-26B-A4B and Qwen3.5-9B were affected. CAR now fetches the file when neither it nor a `chat_template` entry in `tokenizer_config.json` is present, and an existing install picks it up the next time the model loads, with no re-download.

- `mobile.runtime` now leaves the `token` key out of its reply when the caller lacks host authority, instead of returning `"token": null`, matching the documented `token?` shape.

- `mobile.runtime` returns the per-launch daemon auth token only to a host-authority session. A session bound to a supervised agent, or an unbound daemon-token client on a daemon that has a host token, now gets the runtime name and URL without `token`, so a lesser credential can no longer read the daemon token through it.

- A multiplayer submit or merge check no longer leaves its builder Cargo home behind. Each contract run seeded a private Cargo home and builder state under `~/.cache/car-coder/<item>-submit-<pid>-<n>` (or `-check-`) and nothing removed it, so each run could leave several gigabytes on disk (one measured at 8.8 GB). The run's executor now owns that directory and removes it when the run ends, whether the checks passed or failed. At startup the daemon also removes the ones a crash left behind: a contract-run directory whose process is gone, and a coder session's directory when its snapshot is finished and no process holds its lease. A directory for a running submit, a live session, or a session this daemon has no snapshot for is left alone. Snapshot retention now also removes the builder Cargo home of each session it collects, and keeps the snapshot instead when that home cannot be reached. Coder sessions keep their builder Cargo home across turns as before and remove it when the session finishes.

- Multiplayer work items now show whether each stage built on the work before it. `multiplayer.get` and `multiplayer.merge_check` return `insights` per stage, computed from git and never stored: how many of the previous stage's added lines this stage kept, and which files it changed that an already-locked check names in its command. `car mp check` prints them for the reviewer. They never change the merge verdict.
- The multiplayer record is now schema **v3**. A managed stage records `signals.checks_red_during_stage`: how many contract checks its own journal saw fail during the stage. `multiplayer.submit_stage` (`car mp submit --model`) records the model the work was done with, as the submitter's own statement, limited to a model-identifier shape. **Compatibility:** a teammate on an older CAR cannot read an item once a v3 CAR has published a stage to it, and is told to update CAR.
- `multiplayer.publish` now places a stage on the commit `coder.approve_merge` recorded, and refuses when the `car/coder/<id>` branch has been re-pointed since approval. Before, a branch replaced by a different single commit on the same base was published as the reviewed work.

- **Node (`car-runtime`): non-blocking `…Async` variants of the OS integration methods** (car-releases#97). The integration methods on `CarRuntime` — `mail*`, `calendar*`, `contacts*`, `messages*`, `notes*`, `reminders*`, `photosAlbums`, `bookmarksList`, `filesLocations`, `keychainStatus`, `accountsList`/`accountsOpen`, `permissionStatus`/`permissionRequest`/`permissionExplain` and `health*` — run on the JS thread and block the event loop for the whole call (`mailInbox` measured ~10 s with no timer, I/O or request handling in between). Each now has an `…Async` sibling (`mailInboxAsync`, `calendarEventsAsync`, …) that takes the same arguments and resolves to the same JSON, running the call on a background thread so the event loop keeps serving and several calls run concurrently. The synchronous methods are unchanged; their `index.d.ts` docs now say they block and name the `…Async` replacement. In Python the same methods keep their signatures and now release the GIL for the call's duration, so other Python threads keep running.

- CarHost Chat: an approval card now starts with one plain sentence saying what approving lets the agent do. Parslee Core's own tools get fixed words ("Approving lets the agent run a command."), and an AppleScript tool running JavaScript for Automation says so. Any other agent's tool is named in quotes in plain ASCII ("Approving lets this agent use its tool “…”."). For Parslee Core, the card says where the action works when the request proves it: the folder a command runs in, the folder of a file being written, the folder a file listing looks in, the folder a file search looks in (named as the default when the search gives none), or the website a request goes to. Paths are spelled exactly. The website line is left off when the address could be read two ways or names a numeric host. For Parslee Core's command, script, web, search and file tools, the block shows the one value the tool runs: shell's command, the AppleScript or PowerShell script, a web search's query, a web request's address, a file tool's path, or a file search's pattern. A web request's method, headers and body, a file's new contents, and the details of mail and calendar requests are not shown, the same as before. Other agents' approvals show the target their request names. The value appears once, in a block whose Copy button copies it exactly as sent, and every character of it is visible: tabs, trailing spaces, long runs of spaces, empty lines, control and direction characters, and every invisible or blank-looking code point show as markers. A long block, counted in code points, starts as a short preview that says how much is hidden ("Show all 14 lines"), and the chat card's approving buttons stay off until you've shown it all. They also stay off, with "This request didn't include what it would run.", when a Parslee Core request leaves out a value its tool requires. Deny and Don't allow work at any time. A web search card now offers "Approve once" beside the remembered choices, and it approves only that search. The chat's banner and rail row use the same words as the card ("A chat needs you: approve a request to run a command"). Clicking the rail row or the work panel's "approvals waiting" line scrolls to the card and outlines it, the same way a banner click does.

- Release lab: the 7 AM nightly alarm reads the run records with a short-lived read-only token minted from the lab's GitHub App, instead of a personal token secret that was never created.

- Release lab: the 7 AM nightly alarm now judges only the newest nightly published in the last 24 hours and dated today (UTC), and stays silent only when the lab's run record for that tag and Build-Id shows the run finished (outcome done, Mac-side check passed) and every CI catch-up lane reported. An uploaded nightly whose appcast entry or final check failed, that is still running, or whose run record cannot be read now raises the alarm with its own message naming the step. A catch-up lane is now judged by the jobs that put its files on the releases: a Linux attach or mirror failure is reported as Linux, a pip or npm marker step that failed is reported as that lane, and lanes whose own jobs never ran get one `blocked by <job>` notice and a `catch-up-<lane>-blocked.txt` marker instead of a failure notice each. `catch-up-linux.txt` is written only after the mirror copy, and a re-run that lands a lane removes its earlier failed or blocked marker, so status and promotion read it as attached.

- Nightly release notes now end with a "What changed since <previous nightly>" section: one line per change merged since the previous nightly, with its PR number, so you can try each one after installing. It says "No user-visible changes since <previous nightly>." when nothing changed, says so when there is no earlier nightly to compare with, and says "Change list unavailable" if the list can't be built, without stopping the nightly. To see the list for any nightly without building anything, run `bash scripts/lib/nightly-changes.sh <nightly tag> [<commit>]` in a full clone.

- car-bench no longer tells the model on a tool-less task to "complete the task using the tools available to you". gpt-oss answered that instruction by writing code for its built-in python tool and stopping to wait for a result that never came, so tool-less hard tasks came back empty after 137 and 396 tokens and were scored as the model's failure. A task that offers no tools now runs under "You are a helpful AI assistant. No tools are available for this task: answer directly." (`GraderConfig::toolless_system_prompt`); a task with tools keeps the old prompt. Measured on the two affected gpt-oss tasks, gpt-oss now reasons toward an answer instead of stopping; both still hit the output limit, so its score does not change.

- When Parslee Core writes a numbered or bulleted list into a note in the Notes app, its `run_applescript` tool description now tells it that a note body is HTML, that list items go back to back (`<ol><li>…</li><li>…</li></ol>`), and that a `<br>` between items becomes an empty list item. It also says that an `<li><br></li>` in a note read back is an empty item, not content (car#2125).

- Reading a note back no longer needs an approval. The assistant gets two read-only Notes tools, `notes_find` and `notes_read`, at the same tier as the Mail and Calendar reads: `notes_read` returns a note's HTML exactly as Notes.app stores it (an empty list item reads as `<li><br></li>`), so checking a note written with `run_applescript` (still full access) needs no second AppleScript. The same read is `car notes read <id>`, the daemon method `notes.read`, and `notesRead` / `notes_read` in the Node and Python bindings; it is macOS only and answers `available: false` elsewhere. JXA reads whose result is larger than about 64 KB (a long note or mail body) no longer time out: the runner now drains osascript's output while it waits, and a cut mail or note body no longer ends on half an emoji. The assistant's permission checks for Mail, Calendar and Notes now wait at most 2 seconds: when an app does not answer in that time (Notes or Mail stuck on an earlier Apple Event), its assistant tools are left out of that run instead of the run hanging, and the run says which app it was ("Notes isn't answering, so its tools are left out of this run. Quit and reopen Notes, then try again.") rather than dropping the tools silently (car#2125).

- When an agent adds a row to a spreadsheet or fills a Word template, CAR now copies the file's permissions onto the saved copy on macOS and Linux (on Windows none are copied, as before). The saved file used to come back at mode 0600 with its Finder tags gone. On macOS CAR copies the file's access list (entries it inherited from its folder included), tries to copy its extended attributes such as Finder tags (the result isn't checked, so a failed copy can leave some missing), and then copies its group, mode, owner and flags. On Linux it copies the file's POSIX access list before the mode, or, when the file has none, removes the list the new copy inherited from its folder's default list. Where a filesystem reports it has no access lists, the save goes ahead and the new copy keeps what its folder gave it. If CAR can't give the new copy the file's group, the group's mode bits are cleared; on Linux the access list's mask is cleared too, so its named entries lose their access, and on macOS an access-list entry can still grant access. On macOS only the mode is then copied, so the owner and Finder flags such as hidden are not (if copying them fails, only the mode is set and the owner and flags are not guaranteed to be copied). A new file gets what its folder gives it, as before, and on Unix 0666 less the umask (0644 under the usual umask). The new content is written while the new copy is at mode 0600, and the new copy takes the original's access list and then its permissions just before it is renamed into place. Saves to FAT and exFAT drives work as before, and so do saves where the file's dates can't be set. CAR refuses the save and leaves the file unchanged, with a message saying so, when the file is a link (on Unix the open does not follow one; on Windows the path is checked first), is locked, can't be read by CAR, has been replaced by something else when CAR checks just before the rename, or has an access list CAR can't copy. A refused save tries to remove its temporary file, including when the original's access list denies deleting it: on macOS it first sets the temporary file to mode 0600 and then empties its access list, and leaves the list in place if the mode can't be set. Before, filling a template over an existing document CAR couldn't read replaced it.

- Saved agents' Office tools, Studio media tools, the image and speech tools, and browser screen recordings no longer write outside the agent's working folder when a folder on the way is swapped for a link after the check. On macOS and Linux the working folder is opened without following a link and then each folder below it the same way, one name at a time; the temp file, the check of the target, the rename, and any new folders all happen inside that opened folder, so the swap is refused with "… changed while it was being saved, so nothing was saved. Try again." Studio downloads, generated images and speech, and screen recordings now save through the same writer, so they are written atomically and keep an existing file's permissions (and, on macOS, its Finder tags) as Office saves do; a link at the output path that leads outside the working folder is refused. Generated images and speech and the recording's ffmpeg encode are made in a private temporary folder and streamed in, and a recording's frames and concat manifest are kept in a private temporary folder instead of a `.car-recording-*` folder inside the working folder. Their errors now say "working folder" throughout. A folder agent's `write_file` and `edit_file` now save through the same writer: the file is replaced atomically, keeps its permissions (and, on macOS, its Finder tags), a link inside the folder is written at the file it names, a file the person can't write stays unwritten, and a folder swapped for a link after the check is refused with "changed while it was being saved". Studio downloads and generated media written to a link inside the working folder land at the file it names. A recording's frames are removed whenever the recording is encoded or given up. Windows keeps the path-based save. `car_browser::recorder::RecordingHandle::dir()` returns the folder a recording's frames are written to.

- On macOS and Linux, a save by a saved agent's Office tools, Studio media tools, the image and speech tools, browser screen recordings, or a folder agent's `write_file` and `edit_file` is now refused when the folder it goes into is moved out of the agent's working folder after it was opened, rather than landing in the moved folder outside. Before the temp file is made and again just before the rename, the opened folder is checked to still be inside the working folder by walking up from it; a folder moved out (renamed out, replaced by a folder or a link, or under a parent swapped for a link) is refused with "… changed while it was being saved, so nothing was saved. Try again.", and its temp file is removed (a daemon killed mid-copy can leave one behind). A move in the instant between that check and the rename, or one made during the check itself by moving the folder out while its parent is moved into the working folder, is not caught; winning either needs rename rights over the moved folder's parent and over a folder outside on the same filesystem. New folders are refused the same way. A folder renamed or moved within the working folder still gets the save.
- On macOS and Linux those tools now pin the agent's working folder when the run starts. A save after the working folder was renamed away and replaced by another folder, or by a link, is refused with "The working folder … was replaced while the agent was running, so nothing was saved. Start the run again." instead of going into the replacement; the next run uses the folder as it is then.

- Claude 2, Claude Instant and Claude 4 ids written without a dashed point release (such as `claude-2.1`, `claude-instant-1.2`, `claude-sonnet-4` and `claude-opus-4.1`) no longer get 16384 extra output tokens added to a request that asks for no thinking; they keep the caller's `max_tokens`. CAR now reads the Claude version from the model id instead of matching a few name patterns. The same reading fixes two more mistakes. Those ids, and dated Claude 4.0 ids such as `claude-sonnet-4-20250514`, now keep the caller's `temperature` instead of dropping it. The Claude 4.0 and 4.1 ones among them now get the `budget_tokens` thinking request they accept instead of the adaptive one, which they reject. A dotted Claude 5.5 id such as `claude-opus-5.5` now gets the same 8192-token reserve as `claude-opus-5-5`.

- Mac app setup only suggests the email first task when CAR may control Mail itself. It used to check the general app-automation permission, which macOS grants one app at a time, so the email task could be suggested while CAR still could not use Mail.
