When Mail is not allowed, not yet asked, or closed (macOS can't say then), setup suggests the next thing you chose, or a safe planning task.

- CAR now has one spend meter, `car_registry::spend::SpendMeter`, the first step of spend caps (`docs/proposals/spend-caps.md`, build PR 1). It counts model-call spend by key (an agent, a worker, a label, the operator, CAR's own work, a declarative agent or an outside credential) in integer micro-dollars, each call rounded up to the next micro-dollar, over calendar hours, days and months in the server's time zone. Hours are computed from the UTC offset in force at the moment, so a fall-back night's two 1:00 AM hours are two periods and a spring-forward day has 23. Each principal has an append-only, sequence-numbered `calls.jsonl` of admit, settle and correct rows that every key replays, so after a crash every key agrees once the meter recovers it at the next start. A key whose write fails during that recovery stays behind until that principal's next call that charges that key, or the next start, replays it; the ledger row is already correct. A call settled twice counts once, and a later correction never lowers counted spend. The meter runs on its own clock (`spend/clock.json`), which never goes backwards, does not follow a wall clock set back, and after a reboot with an unsynchronized clock holds for at most 10 minutes. The workers meter behind `workers.spend` now records through it: `workers/<handle>/spend.json` and each label's `spend.json` are rewritten in format version 2 on their next call, version-1 files are read unchanged, a worker's root gains `calls.jsonl`, and `workers.spend` returns the same figures, to the cent, with each call rounded up to the micro-dollar. The daemon recovers the meter at start and ticks it once a minute. No cap exists yet, so nothing is refused and no wire shape changes. The server-time-zone rule behind the agent clock line moved to `car_registry::spend::zone`, so the meter and the clock line use the same zone.

- Colleagues in an organization can message each other when nobody is running a session. Each organization this CAR holds a login for has an inbox node, `inbox:<org>`, published on Lattice connections that organization verified (another CAR addresses it as `inbox:<org>@<label>`). A message to it is stored durably on the recipient's CAR (delivery outcome `stored`) until their next session in that org reads it. An org message for a session that has exited, such as a reply, goes to the person's inbox instead of being refused or dropped. The new `lattice.inbox` method reads and acknowledges it. A `car claude` session's `peer_inbox` returns stored messages (`stored: true`) together with live ones, and `collaboration_status` reports `durable: true` for an organization-bound session. Limits: 1,000 unread per organization, 200 unread per connection, kept 30 days.

- New `outcomes.record_external_grade` JSON-RPC method (operator role), with `recordExternalGrade` (Node) and `record_external_grade` (Python): record a graded result for a model the daemon did not serve, such as an outside head-to-head, as `{ model_id, task, quality, n?, source, allow_unknown_model? }`. Grades are kept per source, apart from served outcomes, and `models.route` pools them into `reliability` through the same shrinkage toward the catalog prior, at benchmark weight capped at 8 cases per source, 8 per reporter and 16 in all, so one reporter moves reliability at most two thirds of the way to its grade whatever source names it uses. A supervised agent's sources are stored as `agent:<id>/<source>`, so it cannot overwrite an operator's or another agent's. A source not updated for 90 days stops counting and gives up its slot. Each route candidate lists the grades under `external_grades`. Grades survive a daemon restart that applies `benchmark_priors.json`. A model id CAR does not know is refused unless `allow_unknown_model` is set; each supervised agent, and operators together, may report 30 times a minute; a failed write to disk is refused with `persist_failed`. Refusals use the new error code `-32019` with `data.reason` (car-releases#110).
- `outcomes.resolve_pending` no longer drops a report silently. A report that matches no pending trace makes the call fail with `-32019` `trace_not_pending`, naming the dropped trace ids in `data.dropped`; the reports that matched are still recorded and counted in `data.recorded`. On success, `recorded` now counts only what was recorded, not every tuple sent.
- A traced call stays gradeable for 900 seconds after it **ends**. Before, pending traces were swept 300 seconds after the call **started**, so a call longer than that, such as a 372-second generation, was swept while still running and could never be graded, and its completion was lost too. A call still in flight is now swept only after two hours.

- A client on another host can now connect to a daemon's public door with an outside credential: `new CarRuntime({ url, bearer })` in Node, `CarRuntime(url=..., bearer=...)` in Python, `CarDaemonClient.with_bearer(url, bearer)` in Swift/Kotlin and `DaemonClient::with_url_and_bearer` in Rust. The credential goes out as `Authorization: Bearer` on the WebSocket upgrade, only to a `wss://` URL (or `ws://` to a loopback host), and no local credential is ever used in its place. Host-management calls are refused on such a runtime. A refused connection reports the door's error code and message (for example `credential_revoked`), and a connection the daemon closes because the credential was revoked or expired says so. `new CarRuntime()` and `CarRuntime()` are unchanged.

- Setting a paused agent to "only when I ask" now ends the pause. Before, the pause was kept out of sight, so a schedule given to the agent later started paused with nothing saying so. Now that later schedule starts running. An agent saved that way by an earlier release is read as not paused. Pausing a repeating schedule works as before: it stays paused when it is changed to other times, also by a rebuild or an approved build. CarHost's Change schedule sheet no longer says a later schedule will start paused.

- A `car claude` session now notices colleagues' messages without being asked. The CAR plugin runs the new `car peer-inbox-hook` after each prompt and tool call, and Claude is told how many messages are waiting (live ones, and ones stored in the org inbox, refreshed every 20 seconds). When it is about to stop with messages waiting, it is asked once to read and answer them first. The count comes from a file the session's collaboration server keeps (`CAR_COLLABORATION_PENDING`, set by `car claude`), so the hook makes no network call. Outside a `car claude` session it does nothing.

- A Lattice peer can no longer get around the peer-message limits by making up sender names. Every `node@label` sender behind one connection now shares a budget of 40 messages per recipient per minute, alongside the existing 20 per sender.
- The flagship assistant runs at most 4 peer turns at once and holds at most 16 running or waiting. Past that it answers the sender that it is busy instead of queuing the message. It keeps at most 32 peer conversations, least recently used idle one dropped first, and a dropped one takes its task list and turn lock with it. Peer conversations are now kept in memory only, never checkpointed, while their action records stay durable.
- A Lattice share listing that the Parslee relay never accepted within its 24-hour deadline is now mailed again on the next relay pass. Before, it was recorded as sent and the other CAR kept a stale share list.

- `permissionStatus` / `permissions.status` / `car permissions status` now answer for real on macOS for every domain, and say so when they can't tell (car-releases#95). `contacts` is a non-prompting Contacts-framework query (read by the contacts helper, as `calendar` is by the calendar helper) and can also return `limited`; `camera` and `microphone` read `AVCaptureDevice` authorization; three new domains, `photos`, `reminders` and `full_disk_access`, no longer fail with `invalid_domain`. `photos` and `reminders` report the Automation grant for Photos.app and Reminders.app, because the photos and reminders integrations drive those apps over Apple Events; `full_disk_access` opens and closes a file only Full Disk Access can open. A new status, `unsupported`, means the state could not be read without prompting and is distinct from `not_determined` (never asked): `health_read` on macOS, `automation` with no `target_bundle_id`, and any Apple Events check whose target app is not running or returns an unexpected code — all of which used to report `not_determined`. `permissions.request` and `permissions.explain` for `contacts` and `calendar` now report the same status as `permissions.status`. Off macOS the new domains are `not_applicable`. Rust callers of `car_permissions::status` directly get `unsupported` for `contacts` and `calendar` on macOS, where they used to get `not_determined`; `car_permissions::describe` is new, and `car_integrations::contacts::authorization_status` is new. The assistant's Mail/Notes/Calendar tools now say Automation access "could not be read" rather than "is not_determined" when the app is closed. A scheduled or manual run of a Mail-reading agent refused while Mail is closed now says to open Mail and Run now, not to grant access in System Settings. A stuck `photos` or `reminders` check says to quit and reopen Photos or Reminders. `full_disk_access` probes Safari's bookmarks and the Messages database first and opens the system TCC database only when neither exists. `mail` stays `not_applicable` on macOS; check Mail with `automation` and target `com.apple.mail`.

- CAR Settings › General now shows Full Disk Access as **Not granted** when macOS refuses the check, instead of `—`, matching `car permissions status full_disk_access`, which says `denied`. CarHost now checks the same files in the same order as the CLI (Safari's bookmarks, then the Messages database, both under `HOME` and skipped when it is unset, then the system TCC database only when neither exists) and reads the result the same way: opened is granted, refused is not granted, and `—` is left for a check that really cannot tell (none of the files exists, or the open fails for some other reason). It no longer opens the TCC database in your home folder. A Contacts status that this CarHost build does not recognize now shows `—` instead of Granted.
- `car permissions status contacts` and `status calendar`, and the matching `permissions.status` call and bindings, now report `unsupported` ("can't tell") instead of `unknown` when CAR's contacts or calendar helper cannot answer. That happens when the helper, which is built on first use with the Xcode Command Line Tools, cannot be built or started from the calling process. The same applies when the helper answers with a status this version does not recognize. `car permissions explain contacts` (or `calendar`) now says this in plain words: the permission could not be read from this process, macOS keeps the grant per app, CAR's own access is shown in CAR Settings › General, and how to install the developer tools. The assistant's calendar tools now say Calendar access "could not be read" in this case and point to the developer tools the helper is built with, instead of saying the app must be running or telling you to grant CAR Calendar access, which cannot make the helper run. When Mail or Notes is closed, so macOS cannot answer the Automation check, the Mail and Notes tools now say to open the app and retry instead of telling you to grant access that may already be in place.

- `car permissions status|request|explain` and the daemon `permissions.status`, `permissions.request` and `permissions.explain` methods no longer hang on a Mac when the target app is running but not answering Apple Events (Parslee-ai/car#3357). Each check now answers within 2 s; one that has not answered reports the new status `not_responding`, distinct from `denied` and `unknown` because the app's access may already be granted. The CLI prints a `Not responding: …` line on stderr saying to quit and reopen the app and exits 3. `permissionStatus` / `permission_status` in the Node and Python packages report the same value. A check that never returns gives way to one fresh check after 30 s (at most three stuck checks per app; once eight are stuck past their deadline no new check starts until one returns, though a burst of concurrent checks for different apps can briefly exceed eight), so quitting and reopening the app and checking again works without restarting the daemon. A scheduled Mail-reading agent refused because Mail is not responding now says to quit and reopen Mail instead of telling you to allow CAR to control Mail, and the assistant's notice for a Calendar check that did not answer no longer mentions Apple Events. In CarHost, a Mail or System Events that stops answering now shows "Mail isn't responding. Quit and reopen Mail, then check again." instead of an Allow button, and the Mail permission check and Mail access request run off the main thread, so a wedged Mail.app no longer freezes Settings, the Agent Builder, the Command Deck or setup.

- `scripts/push.sh` no longer skips the `--apple` local CI pass on a branch whose changed-file list is longer than the pipe buffer. The container smoke workflow's compose-up step no longer fails when the daemon log does contain the advertising line, and its `env_file` guard can no longer skip itself. `docs/solutions/piped-command-exit-code-masks-failure.md` now covers this case: under `set -o pipefail`, a piped `grep -q` can exit 141 when the line is present.

- Windows: every path CAR canonicalizes is now the plain spelling (`C:\…`), never the verbatim `\\?\C:\…` form. Containment checks that compared a canonical path against a plain root (folder agents' file tools, the coder's worktree checks) refused everything on Windows, and git and bash cannot open a verbatim path at all. Raw `std::fs::canonicalize`, `Path::canonicalize` and `tokio::fs::canonicalize` are now refused by clippy (`car-rs/clippy.toml`); use `car_winenv::canonicalize` or `PlainCanonicalize::plain_canonicalize`.

- The CAR plugin for Claude Code and Codex now reports CAR's release version in its manifests (it said `0.55.0` for three releases), and every stable release updates it with the rest of the release metadata.

- Policy documentation now matches what the daemon accepts and where policies are checked. The README, intro, roadmap and public README name the surfaces that check policies (`car do`, daemon sessions and the bindings, the Claude Code hook) and the ones that do not (a default A2A listener, external CLIs CAR launches) or are unverified (Codex); before, they claimed policies applied to every action without naming any surface. `registerPolicy` / `register_policy` / `policy.register` accept `deny_tool`, `deny_tool_param`, `require_state` and `deny_connector`; the docs and the `.d.ts` / `.pyi` stubs no longer offer `deny_tool_callback` (every binding refuses it) or `max_calls_per_tool` (it never existed; use `ToolSchema.rate_limit` or `rate_limit_tool`). The duplicate `policy.register` entry in the WebSocket reference is gone. `deny_keyword` is documented as a case-sensitive substring match. ASSISTANT.md no longer says the information-flow gate blocks `car do`'s outbound tools by default: `car do` proposes one action at a time, so the gate decides nothing there.
- A `deny_tool_param_matching` denial from a `.car/policies/` or `~/.car/policies/` file now names the policy file the rule came from, home- or project-relative (`~/.car/policies/x.toml`, `.car/policies/x.toml`) and never as an absolute path, so the person refused can find it; the pattern and the matched value still stay out of the reason.
- `car_policy::DenyToolParamMatching` has a new public field, `source: Option<PathBuf>`, set by `load_policy_dir` and never read from TOML. Code that builds the struct with a literal must add `source: None`, and two rules loaded from different files no longer compare equal.
- `session.init` is documented to skip a policy whose rule the daemon does not accept, without an error and without counting it in `policies_registered`; `policy.register` refuses the same rule. The plugin README warns that a rule protecting `~/.claude/` also blocks Claude Code's memory writes.
- The skills docs no longer say `.car/skills/` or a bundle's `skills.jsonl` is loaded: nothing reads either today. The `skill.ingest` examples now pass the required `name` and `code`.

- The Claude Code plugin's `PreToolUse` hook (`car policy-check-hook`) and `car-mcp`'s `policy_check` now apply a repository's `.car/policies/` from any subdirectory. Before, they read `.car/policies/` in the working directory only, and Claude Code's hook `cwd` follows the agent, so once the agent changed into a subdirectory every repo-root rule stopped applying with no output. Both now read every project `.car/policies/` from the working directory up to the repository root, alongside `<CAR_HOME>/policies/`. The walk stops at the worktree's `.git`, never reads CAR's own state root as a project or reads it twice, and outside a repository reads only the working directory's own `.car/policies/`. Every `.car/` on the way is read, not only the nearest, so a subdirectory's `.car/` with no policies does not hide the root's rules; `car do` and the coder still read only the nearest `.car/`. A sub-project's own `.car/policies/` now combines with the repository root's in the hook and `policy_check`; before, a hook fired inside it read only the nested rules. A root policy file that fails to parse now denies calls from every subdirectory, and the refusal names that file.

- `car-server` now binds every port it serves before it starts any agent or scheduled command. The WebSocket port, the Unix socket, the dashboard (`port + 1`), MCP, the public door (`--public-bind`) and A2A are bound in one step. Only after that do `auto_start` agents start and the daemon's command scheduler fire. Before, an `auto_start` agent could bind the dashboard or MCP port first, and the owner's browser or the plugin relay then sent its credentials to that agent.
- A port CAR cannot bind now stops `car-server` with a non-zero exit. The message names the port and how to move it: `--port` for the WebSocket and dashboard pair, `--mcp-bind` or `--mcp-bind disabled` for MCP, `--a2a-bind`, `--public-bind`. Before, a taken dashboard or MCP port was only a warning, and an invalid `--mcp-bind` value turned MCP off.
- **Behavior change: a WebSocket port held by another program now exits non-zero.** Before, `car-server` exited 0 with "another instance is already bound", and spawners took that as a running CAR. A second daemon for the same `CAR_HOME` still exits 0, at the state folder's lock, before it binds anything. A Unix socket that starts answering after the lock is also fatal now, where the daemon used to fall back to TCP.
- Saved stdio connectors (`connectors.json`) now start after every port is bound too, and so does the self-evolution cadence (`evolution_interval_secs`), whose cycles load them. Each one is a program CAR starts as its own user, outside any wall.
- **Behavior change: `--a2a-bind 0.0.0.0:…` (or `[::]`) without `--a2a-public-url` now stops `car-server`** with a message saying that a wildcard address is not one peers can reach and that `--a2a-public-url` is missing. Before, the daemon warned and ran without A2A. An `--a2a-bind` value that is not an address also stops the daemon now.
- On macOS, a listener bound to every interface (`--host 0.0.0.0`, a wildcard `--mcp-bind` or `--public-bind`) logs a warning. A Mac lets another program of the same user bind `127.0.0.1` on that port beside it and take its loopback connections. Every default is a specific address.
- Two bind failures still only warn. A2A on this host's LAN address by default warns, because its callers sign every request. A Unix socket that cannot be created for another reason also warns, and clients use TCP.
- A public door that is configured but refused (`--no-auth`, or no host token) keeps its port held and answers nothing.
- On Linux, `car-server` refuses to start when `CAR_SERVER_SOCKET` or `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` puts its Unix socket in a folder an agent can write, such as `/dev/shm`, an agent's own folder, or `/tmp`. The message names the variable.
- **Behavior change: `a2a.start` without `share_session_runtime: true` now serves no tools** (#3643). Before, the fresh runtime behind that credential-free loopback listener carried the agent-basics file tools (`read_file`, `write_file`, `edit_file`, `list_dir`, `find_files`, `grep_files`, `calculate`). Any local process could use them as CAR's user, including a walled Linux agent outside its wall. The listener still serves its Agent Card (now with no skills), conversational messages and task state. A caller that wants tools served passes `share_session_runtime: true` and serves its own. Embedders: `car_server_core::mcp::serve_mcp_with_peer_sessions` serves MCP on a listener the caller already bound.

- The daemon's audit files are written owner-only (`0600`): `~/.car/external-agents.jsonl`, `peer-messages.jsonl`, `fleet-work.jsonl`, and the default `~/.car/approvals.jsonl`. A file left looser by an older version is tightened in place (at daemon startup, and on each write), never deleted, and a symlink at any of these paths is refused instead of followed; a symlinked `approvals.jsonl` is not loaded, so approvals in the file it points at are not honoured. The first write also makes the state root `0700`, and a state root reached through a symlink (a symlinked `CAR_HOME`) is refused for these files, as it already was for session journals. A failed audit write is logged with its reason instead of being dropped.
- The external-agent audit record now stores `task`, `options` and `result` with common token shapes, credential query parameters and home paths redacted, and keeps the newest 1,024 records from the last 30 days, the same limits as session journals, trimmed at startup and whenever the file passes 1,152 records or 31 days. Records written before this version are not redacted retroactively. Writing it no longer delays the `agents.invoke_external` reply.
- Explicit embedder paths are unchanged: an approval or peer journal set with `ServerStateConfig::with_approval_journal` / `with_peer_audit_journal`, a path passed to `peers::append_peer_audit_at` / `append_peer_audit_at_dir`, and the FFI `ledger_path` are read and written as before. With a symlinked `CAR_HOME` the default approvals journal is refused and the daemon runs on an in-memory ledger (logged), so approvals do not survive a restart.

- A tool failure in one Parslee Core chat no longer turns up in other chats, either as a proactive reminder or in the assistant's `recall`. The proactive-memory pass saved the failure as memory and injected it into every model call of every later chat until the daemon restarted (car#2284). Memory derived from a chat's own runtime events (a failed or retried tool call, an exhausted replan, an unmet goal, a capped or stalled turn) is now kept to that chat. Within the chat it is injected at most once per turn, a goal turn and any delegated sub-task included, instead of on every model call, and `recall` answers only from what was remembered. The chat service runs every chat on one runtime, so the pass now reads only the events the chat produced, not the whole shared log. Native coder sessions, whose repair memory is shared and saved across restarts, keep such memory to the session (once per run), and `infer` keeps it to the connection whose events produced it. The `car do` REPL now runs its turns in one runtime session, so a reminder carries across its turns. Entries saved by a daemon before this change name no session and are no longer injected into any chat, coder session or `infer` call. `memory.maintain` takes an optional `session_id` (default: the calling connection) that keeps the entries it derives to that session, and `memory.intervene`, plus the MCP `memory_intervene` tool, take optional `session_id` and `exclude_fact_ids`; without them `memory.intervene` considers every entry, as before. Other proactive memory, such as remembered facts and saved knowledge, is selected as before.

- CarCoder's cleanup of a finished or orphaned check no longer signals an unrelated process. It finds a run's leftover processes by the marker file they inherited, and compared that file by inode number alone. The number is reused once the marker is deleted, so a process holding any newer file with the same number could be killed. A process now counts only if the file it holds also has the marker's own name.

- Release lab promotions no longer fail at the docs step with `release not found`. That step read the promoted nightly from the private primary repository with the car-releases token, which can only see car-releases; it now reads with the primary's own token and stages the nightly's Linux CLI, since the job runs on Linux. A promotion whose publish job failed can now be finished by dispatching the release workflow from `main` with `promote_tag=vX.Y.Z`, which publishes from the tag's own files. A new required lint check fails any workflow step that points a token at a repository it cannot use.

- Release lab: stable promotion mints a fresh GitHub App token before every step (an hour-old token failed the appcast step after a long crates.io publish), and the release-mirror publish scripts now print why a clone failed instead of exiting silently.

- Release lab: a stable promotion now mints a fresh GitHub App token before the tag step's existence check, not only before tagging, so a relabel that outlives its hour-long token no longer stops promotion with `publication existence check failed for tag`. A mint failure there fails the tag step with a message naming it.

- The promoted-stable verifier's docs-mirror check now compares against what the docs sync actually publishes. It reads the wire schema from `car-rs/crates/car-server-core/` (the `docs/` copy has been untracked since 2026-10-10). It regenerates `CLI.md`, `websocket-protocol-index.md`, `CAPABILITIES.md`, `capabilities.json` and `llms-full.txt` from the promoted nightly's tree, using that release's CLI for the verifying host, and compares each one with the published copy. It also finds the Windows archive it downloads for the winget hash, because `gh release download` names the file after the asset. Release-lab promotions now run the lab checkout's copy of `verify-release.sh` with the nightly tree as data, so a verifier fix reaches a promotion of an older nightly.

- Release lab: a promotion no longer aborts with "TRAILERS[@]: unbound variable" when earlier stable tags carry no Changelog-Fragments trailer; empty arrays are now safe on macOS's bash 3.2 in the promotion changelog, promote tag, nightly plan and Linux-asset steps.

- A stable promotion no longer fails after a good npm publish because npm's `latest` dist-tag takes a few minutes to show it: `promote-publish` now polls `latest` for about 5 minutes (30 polls at 10s) instead of 60s, and its checks bypass npm's metadata cache. The release lab's `wait-ci` reads npm and PyPI `latest` the same way, for about 5 minutes, instead of once. The post-publish `smoke-install` cells retry an npm or PyPI install of the just-published version for about 5 minutes instead of twice back to back (car#2875).

- `car-harness-train` trains a harness proposer on Apple Silicon, in process through CAR's MLX backend. `sft` fine-tunes a LoRA adapter on the `car-self-harness --dataset` rows that Harness Learning's rule admits (the candidate scored at least `max(parent + 0.08, 0.40)` on the scoring set), choosing the training length by suite-disjoint k-fold cross-validation unless `--epochs` gives it. `grpo` continues from an adapter with GRPO against the frozen solver: one-shot replies in groups of 8, rewarded by Eq. 6 and 8, normalized within each group, one update per batch, with `--phases` for successive revision states. `export` fuses an adapter into a checkpoint that serves as an ordinary local model from `~/.car/models`, and with `--probe` reports how much of the adapter's effect the fused copy kept. Other builds refuse to train.
- `car_inference::training` is the trainer behind it: LoRA attach, a weighted completion-only step, a gradient-free loss, adapter save and fused export over new `mlx-swift-lm` entry points. Prompts are rendered and tokenized as serving does, and samples are drawn through the engine's own local decode loop, which now also returns the ids it generated.
- The reward on `car-self-harness` dataset rows uses each candidate's mean grader credit on the scoring set (partial credit included) as its task score, as Harness Learning does, instead of its pass rate. Scoring runs record that credit as `credit`.
- `car-self-harness` proposer calls run with thinking off. A local reasoning model such as Qwen3 otherwise spent the whole 2048-token budget thinking and returned an empty reply. Remote providers ignore the setting.

- **`server.handshake` negotiates a wire-protocol range instead of demanding an exact match** (Parslee-ai/car-releases#104). A client sends its newest version as `protocol_version` plus an optional `protocol_version_min`; the daemon settles on the highest version both ranges contain and returns it as `protocol_version`, beside its own `server_protocol_version_min` / `server_protocol_version_max`. Only a range sharing no version is refused with `-32006`, and the message names both ranges, the daemon's version and which side to update. **No wire-protocol version bump and no compatibility break:** the wire protocol stays v3 (range `3..=3`), the change is additive, and a client that sends only `protocol_version` (older `car-runtime` packages, the native hosts) is read as a one-version range and behaves exactly as before. The compatibility rule is now written down: CAR package minors that share a wire-protocol version connect both ways, a future protocol bump keeps the previous version in range, and raising the minimum is the breaking change. The NAPI and PyO3 bindings add `protocolVersionMin()` / `protocol_version_min()`, and the bundled clients (`car-daemon-client`, the `car` CLI, `car-host`) now send their range.
- **A supervised agent whose handshake is refused, or whose client has drifted from the daemon, is visible outside its stderr log.** The daemon records each agent's last handshake (settled version or refusal, both ranges, both package versions) in `agent-handshakes.json` beside `agents.json`; a worker's goes in its own root (`workers/<handle>/`), never the shared file. `agents.list` rows carry it as `handshake`, `car ls` prints a refusal in red with the side to update (client, daemon, or a capability/params refusal) and skew as a note, a new self-heal detector `agent_protocol_skew.v1` reports a refusal as critical and protocol or package skew as a warning, and `car doctor` lists it under "Agent connections", counting a refused handshake as a problem and marking one recorded by a different daemon build as possibly stale. The agent-reported `client_version` is reduced to version characters before it reaches any terminal, a repeat of the same outcome does not rewrite the record, and a record the daemon cannot read is left alone rather than rewritten with only the newest agent.

- A content block from Google, Vertex, Bedrock or OpenAI Responses is now a typed content refusal (JSON-RPC `-32007`, tagged `type=content_filter, code=<the provider's reason>`) instead of a generic inference failure, as Anthropic's `refusal` already was. Before, a Gemini `SAFETY` stop, a Bedrock filter or a Responses `content_filter` counted against the model's health, so the router demoted a model for declining content. Now no refusal is charged to the model, streamed or not, and a Gemini block that arrives after partial text is a refusal too. A vendor's own filter still lets an auto-routed request fall through to the next candidate, as Apple's on-device guardrail does, because another model is a different ruling. A Bedrock guardrail (`guardrail_intervened`) is the operator's policy and ends the chain. Google's `RECITATION`, `OTHER` and `MALFORMED_FUNCTION_CALL` stay plain failures.

- Contributor scripts (`scripts/ci-local.sh` and the other scripts that source `scripts/lib/python.sh`) now require Python 3.11 or newer and say so in the first seconds. Before, Apple's built-in `python3` 3.9.6 ran `ci-local.sh` for about 25 minutes and then failed on `import tomllib`. The resolver also tries `python3.14` through `python3.11`, so Homebrew Python works with no setup; with none found it prints the version it found, the version it needs and the fix (`brew install python@3.13` or `PYTHON=/path`).

- Docs no longer tell Python users to run `python -m car_runtime.server`, which never worked (the pip wheel ships no `car-server`); they now say to start the daemon with `npx --package=car-runtime car-server --port 9100`, the release tarball, or CAR Host.app (#3093).

- Quitting CarHost no longer orphans a supervised agent that was still stopping. CarHost used to send the daemon SIGKILL 2 s after SIGTERM, while the daemon gives its agents up to 15 s to stop, so an agent still running its shutdown handler was left behind. CarHost now waits up to that budget plus 5 s, and the quit finishes as soon as the daemon exits. The windows, the Dock icon and the menu bar item go away at once, so the wait does not look like a hung quit, and opening CAR again during the wait, as a new launch or a reopen from Finder, Spotlight or the Dock, starts the new instance as soon as the old one has exited. At logout, restart or shutdown, including one that begins during that wait, CarHost waits at most 3 s and does not kill the daemon, which finishes stopping on its own, so CarHost never shows as preventing the logout. Settings > Daemon > Restart daemon waits the same way without holding the app, and starts the new daemon only after the old one has exited, even when pressed twice. Launchd lanes installed with `scripts/car-lane.sh` now set `ExitTimeOut` to 20 s (launchd's default on macOS 27 was 5 s); an installed lane picks it up when you re-run `install-plist`. `install-plist` and `stop` also wait for the old daemon to exit before going on, instead of failing to bootstrap the label while it is still stopping (car#3131).

- CarHost Chat (macOS): clicking a chat that is waiting for you in the sidebar, or a "needs you" notification, now opens it on the approval card that is waiting, outlined for a moment, even when you had scrolled that chat up to an older message. Before, it could open partway up the conversation, on an earlier approval that was already answered.

- Nightly releases are no longer held back by the post-merge daemon-client reachability check. It expected a supervised agent to reach the secret store's write handler and, on Linux, every method that the walled-agent default-deny now refuses. It now expects those agent calls to be refused, and checks that each one is refused by the right gate.

- The daemon-client reachability check passes on macOS again: it now checks the outside-engine refusal of a supervised agent's `foreman.run` and `heal.run`, and the host-chat refusal of `agents.invoke_external`, in the Rust, Node and Python legs.

- `car do`, chat and goal runs no longer accept "verified" when the run's own read-back shows the result is wrong. When the final answer claims verification and the run read its result back after changing it, a model compares that read-back with the request. A contradiction (for example a Notes list read back with empty `<li><br></li>` items) takes `grounded` away from a goal pass, even a deterministic one, and sends a plain run's answer back for correction unless the answer already reports the mismatch; if the answer still claims verification once no correction is left (two spent, or the run's last turn), it is returned with a `[claim check]` note naming the contradiction rather than accepted silently (a reply held to `--response-format` keeps its shape and gets no note). The check never grants a pass, and an inference error, timeout or unparseable verdict accepts the answer as before.
- The judge's model call is one more call on runs that trigger it: harness and benchmark model-call and token counts include it. Its `InferenceMetered` event carries `purpose: "readback_judge"` so a count can leave it out.

- npm `car-runtime`: `import { CarRuntime, executeProposal, verify } from 'car-runtime'` now works in ESM (Node 18+); the package `exports` gained an `index.mjs` entry for the root and `require('car-runtime')` is unchanged. The README quick start now has prerequisites and the platform matrix before the install lines, a complete Node and Python example (a literal proposal, a tool registered in the snippet, a working callback) with the output that proves it worked (`actions_executed: 1`), `npx car-server --port 9100` instead of a bare `car-server`, one copy of the CLI table, and a plain statement that the `car` CLI comes from the platform tarball or the macOS `.pkg`, not from npm or pip. The pip wheel does not contain `car-server` and has no `python -m car_runtime.server`; the Python README no longer says it does. `scripts/check-readme-quickstart.sh` runs the README's Node snippet against the package built from the branch in CI and fails if the promised output is missing.

- Agents that outlive a killed or crashed daemon (SIGKILL, crash, out-of-memory kill) are now taken back under supervision when the daemon restarts, instead of being listed as `stopped` and left unsupervised. `agents.start` on such an agent also re-adopts the surviving process rather than waiting in `backoff` behind it. CAR re-adopts a process only after confirming it is the one it started: the pid and the kernel-recorded process start time must both match, and the start time does not depend on timezone or locale, so a restart under a different `TZ` still recognises it. A pid recycled by an unrelated program is never adopted, signalled or stopped, and its stale record is dropped so the agent starts fresh. If the start time cannot be read at a given moment, CAR treats the process as still the agent's and does not start a second copy. Pid files written by older versions carry no start time, so their survivors are left alone as before. The exit code of an adopted agent is unknown (CAR is not its parent) and is recorded as -1, which `on_failure` agents treat as a failure: an adopted `on_failure` or `always` agent that later disappears is restarted, because an agent set to stay up should stay up; `never` agents stay stopped.

- A request with no thinking budget to a model that reasons on every call (the OpenAI o-series, Codex, and gpt-5.5 and later) now reserves room for that reasoning: `max_tokens` is the caller's answer plus the default (medium) effort's 8192 tokens, capped at the model's output limit. Before, the reasoning spent the answer budget, and GPT-5.6 sol came back empty ("incomplete: max_output_tokens") on two 4000-token tasks.

- Skill evolution no longer re-trials a candidate that already failed the promotion gate once that rejection has been recorded. Since car#1953, promoting a skill recorded its rejected alternatives in the new revision's decision history and also removed them from the session's rejection buffer, which is what blocks a failed candidate's fingerprint and lists failed candidates in the evolution prompt. After any promotion of that skill, the identical failed candidate could be put on trial again. A recorded rejection now stays in the buffer, still blocking and still listed in the prompt, and is still recorded in exactly one revision. The buffer stays capped at 16 per tenant and domain and still evicts its oldest entries first, recorded or not, so recording a rejection never shortens how long it blocks (car#1973).

- The shared redactor now removes three secret shapes it used to keep (#3418). It runs on feedback bundles (`car feedback`, `feedback.*`), the external-agent audit record, coder evidence, verifier and audit input, coder failure reasons, supervised-agent log windows (`agents.tail_log`), coder A/B ledgers, car-bench could-not-run reasons, and the assistant's governance targets and evidence. A PEM private key goes whole, from its BEGIN line through its END line, for any key type (`RSA`, `EC`, `DSA`, `OPENSSH`, `ENCRYPTED`, PKCS#8 `PRIVATE KEY`, PGP armor), including one spanning lines inside a JSON string, when only key material sits between the two lines. A key cut off before its END line goes through its base64 body and no further. A BEGIN marker merely mentioned in a log loses only the marker, plus a one-word line right after a cut-off body. In text, the value of an AWS secret access key named as one goes, in any case, with or without spaces around `=` or `:` (`aws_secret_access_key = …`, `"SecretAccessKey": "…"`), when the value is key-shaped (16+ base64 characters, not letters only), so code that names the key keeps its text. A JSON field of those names loses its string value whatever its shape. Standard base64 runs of 40+ characters with `+`, `/` and `=` padding, which used to survive as shorter pieces, go too. Only the opaque part goes, so the readable path around an id in a URL stays. Base64-alphabet text that reads as words (a long path, a URL path, a `+`-joined query) is kept, as is text with no letters, Ed25519, RSA and ECDSA OpenSSH public keys, and `sha256-`/`sha384-`/`sha512-` integrity hashes. Before this, only the BEGIN line of a private key was removed (PGP armor not at all), and an AWS secret access key only in its uppercase `AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY=` form or when no `+` or `/` split it below 40 characters. Other base64, such as a public key or hash in another form or a base64 blob in a log, now counts as credential-shaped: it is redacted in logs and records. Lattice refuses a message or purpose carrying it or a named AWS value, withholds a node note or disconnect reason carrying it, and leaves a listing row that still carries it out, as it already did for 40+ character runs without `+` or `/`.

- A supervised agent can now write only its own entry in the agent registry (`~/.car/registry/`). Over the daemon, `registry.register`, `registry.heartbeat` and `registry.unregister` from an agent-bound session refuse any `name` other than the agent's own id, and `registry.reap` from one removes at most the agent's own stale entry (on Linux a walled agent cannot call `registry.reap` at all). Before this, an agent could overwrite, remove or reap another agent's entry, although `registry.list` already showed it only its own. Host sessions, the daemon-token operator and the CLI still write and reap any entry.

- Installing or upserting an agent whose agents folder exists but cannot be listed is now refused with a message naming the folder. This applies to the daemon's case check and to `car agents install`'s binary step. Before, an unreadable folder read as "no agents installed", so a case variant of an installed agent's id could reuse its files.
- On a clean shutdown the daemon saves its shared memory after the supervised agents have stopped, not before, so memory an agent changes while shutting down is kept.
- A declarative agent whose id differs from a registered one only in letter case (`Sales` beside `sales`) is refused when saved. Before, both were accepted and shared spend meter files on a case-insensitive file system.
- `max_skills_in_context`, and `find_skill`'s `max_results`, now cap global and domain skills together, ranked by score. Before, every global skill was always included, so every skill added with `skill.ingest` landed in every prompt. On the wire, `skill.find` defaults `max_results` to 1, so a call that names no `max_results` now gets the one best skill, not every global skill plus one.
- The spend ledger syncs its directory on the next append after a failed directory sync, including when the failed append's rows could not be cut back.

- The `car-registry`, `car-inference` and `car-cli` tests now wait for the event they check, up to one 60 s bound per crate (`LOADED_WAIT`), instead of fixed bounds of a few seconds, so a full-suite run on a loaded machine no longer fails tests whose behaviour is fine. A new check, `scripts/check-test-wait-bounds.sh`, run by `scripts/ci-local.sh` and the CI `terminology` job, fails on a short wall-clock bound on a test wait in every crate the check reads (all but those listed in `scripts/lib/test_wait_bounds.py`) unless the statement says why it must stay short (`// short-wait-ok: <why>`). Part of #3763.

- Daemon: two saves of the same supervised agent (`agents.upsert`, a bundle install or upgrade) that overlap no longer leave the daemon's memory and its `agents/<id>/manifest.toml` disagreeing when one save's write fails. Each save now holds the manifest lock from its change in memory through its write and any rollback, so a failed save puts back in memory exactly what it replaced, and a save whose caller disconnects partway still finishes its write or rollback. Before, the failed save's rollback could undo the other save in memory while that save's write stayed on disk, or remove a new agent so the other save wrote nothing and still reported success. On an install that still has the legacy `agents.json`, an edit whose existing `manifest.toml` cannot be replaced is now refused and rolled back, and `agents.json` is left as it was; before, it was reported saved while the next boot, which prefers that `manifest.toml`, loaded the old spec. If the `agents.json` write fails after the `manifest.toml` was written, that manifest is put back too (the previous one, or none for a new agent); if putting it back also fails, the error names the `manifest.toml` the next boot will load. This completes the earlier fix for a failed save on a fresh install, which covered one save at a time.
- Docs: a `declagents.json` that is empty or holds only whitespace reads as an empty registry, like a missing file, and the next save writes over it; it holds no agents, so nothing is lost. The earlier note that a file that cannot be read or parsed is an error, never an empty list, applies to every other file.

- A colleague's message over the Parslee relay reaches a running organization session within a few seconds: while an org-bound session (`car claude`, an org agent or MCP session) is running, the daemon checks the relay every 3 seconds instead of every 25–30.

- Release lab docs: the identity table now covers crates.io (the token owner is the `published_by` actor) and CI, and the releasing-car skill explains the crates.io actor.

- Release lab: `car-release nightly --dry-run` now prints the plan a nightly would run (version, commit, build ID, each skipped commit and why, or "no nightly due") and still builds, tags, publishes and records nothing. A real `car-release nightly` now also prints the skipped-commit lines and a `watch:` line naming the run to follow. The full lab health check now runs every suite in the release-lab runner list, and the Mac-side release verifier's suite is part of that list.

- Release lab: a promotion that fails while waiting for CI, and a resumed nightly that fails while restoring its archived files, now post their Teams notice and open or update their GitHub issue. Before, those two steps (and the promotion fetch step) had no team-facing name, so the notice could not be written and nothing was announced. A new test reads the step list straight from the nightly and promotion runners and fails if any step has no name. Failure excerpts now keep error lines that name a script path (the path is dropped, the message stays), keep red or error-looking lines that scrolled out of the last 40, and always end with the step's exit status, saying plainly when the step logged no error line. A run record can no longer stay at `running` after its run ended: a run killed without cleanup is closed as interrupted by the next lab command, and the scheduled state publish republishes any finished run whose published copy still disagrees.

- Release lab: finish-release.sh and backfill-macos-assets.sh now refuse off the lab, and the laptop refusal switch proves the lab by its root-installed Azure certificate instead of a marker file anyone can create.

- Release lab: `car-release nightly --dry-run` now ends in `release-lab preflight: FAIL` when the planner's PLAN line has a missing or malformed field, naming the first wrong one; it used to print the field and PASS. An absent, empty or whitespace-only field gives `plan: the planner's PLAN line has no <field>`. A malformed one gives `plan: version is not a nightly version (vX.Y.Z-nightly.YYYYMMDD): <version>`, `plan: commit is not a full 40-character commit SHA: <commit>`, or `plan: build_id <build ID> does not name commit <commit> (want YYYYMMDD-<first seven characters of the commit>)`. A real nightly refuses the same line at its plan step through the same check and message. Full `lab-health.sh` now reads the release-lab suite list with the CI runner's own validator, so a list entry CI refuses (an absolute path, more than one line, a directory, or a missing script) fails health with the runner's reason instead of passing.

- Release lab: the Keenan-Mac invariants probe and the baseline capture now scrub evidence with an in-repo scrubber (`scripts/lib/evidence-scrub.sh`) instead of a deleted wheelhouse script, and the nightly-pause check accepts the `=> disabled` form that macOS 26 prints as well as the older `=> true`.

- Release lab: run records no longer store a `promotable` answer. A published nightly record used to say `promotable: true (verified)` while its Linux, Windows, pip and npm catch-up were still pending, disagreeing with `car-release status`. Status now always answers from the same preflight `car-release promote` runs, including for a run that failed before choosing a version, where it used to print the stored answer. The "Not promotable" line on release-failure issues and Teams notices comes from the record's catch-up lanes. Records published before this change may still carry `promotable`; nothing reads it, and every republish (the run's own publish, the catch-up sync and the state publish's repair) drops it.

- Release lab: `car-release status` no longer calls a lab-built nightly "not lab-built" (the forced command now reads the tag from the lab checkout instead of its non-repository home folder), every history row whose run record exists prints its link, a failure issue gets the successful run's record link when a later run succeeds, the run record is published with a fresh GitHub token after every step and once more when the run ends, a failed publish names its cause and no longer erases an existing link, and `scripts/lab/secret-scan.sh` scans every artifact a run produces for secrets.

- Release lab: `car-release status` now mints the lab GitHub token for its own process, so the promotable line can read private tags (it printed "no Build-Id trailer" because it had no credential).

- Added review-only release-lab proposals: a tag ruleset limiting `v*` tag changes to the lab App, and an Azure Key Vault audit-log setting with a query listing who read secrets during a run.

- Release operations: `scripts/car-lane.sh` refuses to `restart`, `stop` or `install-plist` the stable daemon label (`ai.parslee.car.daemon`) without `CAR_LANE_ALLOW_STABLE=1`, as it already did for `install-plist`; `promote` keeps its documented form. `restart` and `prove` count a lane as listening only when the lane's own launchd job holds the port, and `restart` waits for the restarted job's new process, so another program on the port, or the old process still answering, no longer reads as ready. The error names the process holding the port. `scripts/nightly-release.sh` checks every nightly launch from the last 14 days for its tag, not only yesterday's, so a night the Mac was off no longer hides an earlier failed launch, and each failed launch is acknowledged separately.

- The remote-access guide (`docs/remote-access.md`) now records the Azure Container Apps measurements for the public door, made on 2026-10-23 in `eastus`. The ingress passes the WebSocket upgrade, sets `X-Forwarded-Proto`, and kept an idle connection open for the full hour. The dashboard's `/auth-token` route (since removed in front doors 6c) answers `404` from the internet. The reachability check failed: another app in the same environment reaches the door's port 9443 directly, and the environment's internal route carries plain HTTP to it marked as `https`. So the dedicated, VNet-free environment is now a permanent requirement. CAR does not enforce it; Azure access control does, so the roles on that environment are part of the door's boundary. Inside the environment, the `X-Forwarded-Proto` check protects nothing. The guide also corrects things that did not work as written. The `websocat` command now uses `-H=`, and the idle test now counts pings, because the two dates alone prove nothing. `az containerapp create` now uses `--registry-identity system`. The guide now says that no released client can issue credentials yet, that `az containerapp exec` needs an interactive terminal, and that no published npm package has the `bearer` option yet.

- `params.stop` now works on remote models (car-releases#108). It was honored only by the local decoders, so a routed request to Claude, GPT or Gemini returned text that ran through and past the stop sequence. It is now sent natively within each provider's limits — OpenAI chat / Azure / OpenRouter / vLLM-MLX `stop` (the first four; never to OpenAI reasoning models, which reject it), Anthropic `stop_sequences` (whitespace-only sequences such as `"\n"` left off, which Anthropic rejects), Google/Vertex `generationConfig.stopSequences` (the first five) and Bedrock `inferenceConfig.stopSequences` (the first four, whitespace-only left off) — and CAR also cuts the returned text, the streamed deltas and the final streamed text at the first stop sequence on every backend (remote, local, delegated runners, the Codex lanes), which covers the OpenAI Responses API (it has no stop parameter) and every sequence a provider was not sent. A cut does not cancel the upstream request: where no native stop applied, the provider keeps generating, and billing, up to `max_tokens`. When CAR made the cut, `stop_reason` is `"stop_sequence"`, and an answer the stop sequence left empty is returned as is rather than retried as a thinking truncation. Tool-call arguments are never cut; a stop list on a turn that returned tool calls logs a warning.
- New `params.reasoning_effort` (`"low" | "medium" | "high" | "xhigh"`) sets reasoning effort explicitly (car-releases#107). It maps to OpenAI chat `reasoning_effort` and Responses `reasoning.effort` on reasoning models (the o-series receives `"high"` for `"xhigh"`), OpenRouter `reasoning.effort`, the Codex subscription lane and the Codex CLI's `model_reasoning_effort` (both overriding a `:high` model suffix), and Anthropic adaptive thinking with `output_config.effort` (Opus/Sonnet 4.6 receive `"high"` for `"xhigh"`; Claude 4.5 and earlier receive a thinking budget of 2048 / 8192 / 16384 / 32768 tokens). On its own it turns Anthropic thinking on. It overrides the level CAR derives from `budget_tokens` (below 4096 low, below 16384 medium, otherwise high) and is dropped silently on Google, Vertex, Bedrock, the Parslee-managed lanes (`parslee/*`), local models and non-reasoning OpenAI models. There is no `none` or `minimal`, so neither can reach gpt-5.5+ or gpt-6.x, which reject both. `params.thinking` is documented as what it always was: the Qwen3 directive, a no-op on remote models.
- `usage.reasoning_tokens` (optional) reports the hidden-reasoning share of `completion_tokens` when OpenAI returns it (`completion_tokens_details.reasoning_tokens` on chat, `output_tokens_details.reasoning_tokens` on Responses, non-streaming calls), so a caller can confirm an effort setting took effect. It is absent, not zero, where the provider reports no figure.
- Rust API: `ApiRequest` gains `stop` and `reasoning_effort`, `TokenUsage` gains `reasoning_tokens`, `GenerateParams` gains `reasoning_effort`, and `RemoteBackend::generate_with_tools_multi_observed` takes a new `&OutputControls` argument; `RemoteBackend::generate_stream_with_controls` is the streaming entry point that carries them. `infer_tracked` stays the basic positional form by design; `infer_tracked_with_request` is the full path.

- Upgrade note: CAR no longer reads `~/.car/env` (or `$CAR_HOME/env`). Keys and settings live in CAR's secret store: the OS keychain on Mac and Windows, Secret Service on Linux, and on a Linux machine with no Secret Service one owner-only file per key under `~/.car/secrets/` (directory `0700`, files `0600`; a key file with wider permissions is refused, and the error gives the fix `chmod 600 <file>`). `car keys set <provider>` is the one way to store a key, and process environment variables still win for CAR's own calls.
- On the first daemon start after you upgrade, the daemon moves every entry of `~/.car/env` into the secret store, reads each back, and then deletes the file. No backup is kept, because a backup would be the same keys in plain text. If any entry fails to move, nothing is deleted and `car doctor` names the entries that did not move (names only, never values); fix the store problem, then restart the daemon or run `car secrets migrate-from-env`. A `CAR_AUTH_TOKEN` found in the file stays the daemon's token on every start: the move saves it to an owner-only file (`secrets/adopted-auth-token`, mode `0600`, in the same state folder) before deleting the old file, and the daemon reads it from there, never from the store. A token supplied by `--auth-token`, `CAR_AUTH_TOKEN` or `--auth-token-file` (CarHost uses the file) always wins. If the file's value for a key differs from the one already in the store, the store keeps its own value, the other entries still move, and the file stays; the daemon log and `car doctor` name the key (never its value), and `car secrets migrate-from-env --replace` overwrites the store with the file's value if that is the right one.
- Non-key settings that the file used to apply to the daemon environment (`RUST_LOG`, `CAR_NO_AUTH`, `VLLM_MLX_ENDPOINT`, ...) are moved to the store but are no longer applied to the daemon environment. The daemon's startup log, `car secrets migrate-from-env` and `car doctor` each name every such setting and say to set it in the launch environment instead (the launchd plist, or `Environment=` in a systemd unit).
- A `~/.car/env` made by hand after the upgrade is ignored: the daemon logs once that it is ignored, and `car doctor` lists its entry names with the fix `car secrets migrate-from-env`. That command now imports the file into the store and deletes it with no backup (it used to keep an owner-only backup); `--dry-run` prints names and changes nothing, `--replace` overwrites a different store value, and the command still copies provider keys set in the shell environment (`--include` and `--service` apply to that part).
- Key resolution is now process environment, then the secret store. The source column of `car keys list` says `env` or `store` (it used to say `keychain` or `car env`). `car secrets get --check-credential` reads the secret store only.
- `car lattice org join <code> --fingerprint <fp>` writes `PARSLEE_SYNC_ORG_SCOPE` into the secret store instead of a file; restart the daemon afterwards.
- `car purge --keep-secrets` keeps `~/.car/secrets` (the Linux key store) and a leftover `~/.car/env` that has not been moved. The coder sandbox still denies reads of `~/.car/env`, in case a hand-made file exists, and now also denies `~/.car/secrets`. The daemon's child processes (`gh`, `git push`, `hf`, `mflux`, `vllm-mlx`, `mlx-audio`, `gemini`, `mini-swe-agent`) are handed the token they need from the store when they are spawned.

- The source tree no longer carries a stray second copy of the release wire schema at `car-rs/car-rs/crates/car-server-core/`. It was a stale partial output that nothing built or read; the canonical schema in `docs/` and `car-rs/crates/car-server-core/` is unchanged.

- Every text file now checks out with LF line endings on every platform (`* text=auto eol=lf` in `.gitattributes`); `.cmd`/`.bat` keep CRLF. Source-scanning tests, golden vectors and byte-compared fixtures no longer fail on Windows checkouts for content that is identical.
- Windows: the managed-gateway and Codex refused-connection retry tests start their stand-in server only after the refusal is reported, instead of racing a 500ms timer against Windows' slower loopback refusal. The builder-child tests and the CoW Cargo-home seeding tests are marked ignored where CarCoder cannot run them, so they still compile on Windows and show up as ignored rather than vanishing.
- `car board` and `car code` now refuse up front, with "needs a terminal", when standard output is not a terminal. On Windows the console is reachable even with output redirected, so they used to go fullscreen and draw into a file or nowhere until killed.
- Repository scripts that run Python through `scripts/lib/python.sh` now write LF output on Windows too, through a `sitecustomize` that is active only for a native Windows interpreter and still runs the interpreter's own. Python there turned every `\n` on stdout and stderr into `\r\n`, so generated files such as `docs/daemon-outbound.json` read as drifted against their committed LF copies.
- Windows: a saved folder agent's file tools no longer refuse every path. The containment check compared a canonical `\\?\C:\…` path against a plain worktree root, so no path was ever inside it.

- The flagship assistant now refuses an `agent.chat` turn whose `session_id` starts with `peer:`. Those ids belong to peer conversations: a host chat under one shared that peer's thread, was never checkpointed, and could be dropped by the peer conversation cap.

- `models.register` over the daemon no longer lets a supervised agent (or any client that is not the operator) register a model that sends a reserved provider credential — `OPENAI_API_KEY`, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, the AWS keys, the other keys `car keys` stores, or the matching Keychain item — to a host outside that provider. Such a row was sent the real key on every call. The call now fails with an error naming the remedy: store a credential for the gateway under its own name and point `api_key_env` at it, or register from the operator CLI with `car models register`. Re-pointing an existing row's endpoint or credential is checked the same way. The operator (a host-role session, which `car models register`, `car models add` and an unsupervised SDK `registerModel` now present when they hold the local host credential) can still pair any credential with any host, and custom-named credentials still work from any client. The same holds for credentials CAR stores for itself — the ChatGPT subscription token (`CAR_OPENAI_CODEX_OAUTH`), Slack, Microsoft Graph, Fitbit, Tavily, Hugging Face (`HF_TOKEN`) and translation tokens, connector credentials, and any `CAR_` or `PARSLEE_` name — which may not be sent to any registered endpoint by a non-operator. Names are compared without regard to case, since Windows reads `openai_api_key` as `OPENAI_API_KEY`, and a non-ASCII name is treated as reserved.
- A process launched as a supervised agent (`CAR_AGENT_ID` or `CAR_AGENT_TOKEN` set) no longer presents the operator's host credential from the SDKs or `car models register`, even when it can read the host token file: a host-only call from such a process is refused locally with "this command needs the operator (host); a supervised agent can't run it", and a call the daemon admits from an agent acting for itself (`lattice.find`/`join`/`leave`/`nodes`, the Coder run methods, `agents.start`/`stop`/`restart`, `agents.invoke_external`, `models.register`) goes out under its own agent identity. Inside a supervised agent's process this refuses `car lattice` host commands (connect, knock, org and the rest of the host-only set), `car auth status --refresh`, `car approvals default`, and the SDKs' model pull / install / remove / adopt and resource-policy calls, which used to borrow the operator's host token from disk. The `car models pull` / `remove` / `adopt` commands keep their own host-token path. `car models register` from such a process refuses a reserved credential to a foreign host before asking the daemon, naming `CAR_AGENT_ID` / `CAR_AGENT_TOKEN` as the reason. CarHost and the Windows tray drop `CAR_AGENT_ID` and `CAR_AGENT_TOKEN` at launch, so they and everything they start stay the operator even when launched from an agent's process.
- `secret.put` and `secret.delete` over the daemon now refuse a non-operator client on the three slots that choose where CAR sends a credential — `PARSLEE_STUDIO_BASE`, `PARSLEE_SYNC_BACKEND` and `PARSLEE_CAR_SYNC_BASE_URL` — since pointing one elsewhere redirected the Parslee bearer to that host. The CAR app, `car secrets put` and the in-process SDK `secretPut` / `secret_put` are unaffected, as is every other slot.
- `car claude` run inside a supervised agent (`CAR_AGENT_ID` or `CAR_AGENT_TOKEN` set) now refuses before launching Claude Code or enrolling anything, and says to run it from your own terminal: a supervised agent can't enroll a session agent (car#1295).

- Settings > Daemon > Restart daemon no longer starts the new daemon while the old one is still exiting. When the old daemon ignored its stop request and had to be killed, CarHost started the replacement the moment it sent SIGKILL, before the old process had gone, so the new one could find the port still taken. CarHost now waits up to 5 s for the killed daemon to exit before starting the new one. If it is still running after that, CarHost starts no second daemon onto its port: Settings and the menu bar say the new daemon hasn't started, and CarHost starts it as soon as the old one exits. Restarting again, or quitting, stops the old daemon again. A quit whose daemon has to be killed likewise waits up to 5 s longer for it to exit, so the longest quit is now 25 s (the daemon's 15 s budget plus 5 s, plus that 5 s); reopening CAR during the quit waits that long too (car#3144).

- A Coder review recheck (`coder.refresh_review`) whose accepted checks come back red now fails with `failure_kind: "checks_red"` and an error naming each red check (a timeout counts as red, and any checks that could not run are listed after them), instead of the residual `error` kind, so scorers and dashboards treat it as a test failure rather than a runtime fault. A recheck whose failing checks could not run at all (exit 126/127, or no exit code without a timeout) stays `error` and names them as could not run. The `car code` board's approval footer offers `v recheck` only when the daemon reports `review_refresh_available` for that review. The Python stubs' `coder_get` `failure_kind` list now names every kind the daemon reports.

- A new daemon method can no longer become callable by every supervised agent just because its handler forgot a caller guard. The methods whose handlers call none (role `operator`, or `agent` earned only by reading the caller's agent id) are now a reviewed, committed list (`car-server-core/src/reviewed_ungated_methods.txt`), seeded with every such method today, so no role changes. Building `car` fails with "ungated method `x.y` is not in the reviewed ungated list" when a method lands ungated and unlisted, and a dispatch arm like `_ if method.starts_with(..)`, which no role can be derived for, now fails the build too. A test drives every `operator_only` and `owner` method from a bound-agent session to check the refusal.
- `events.clear` and `events.truncate` are refused to a session bound to a supervised agent ("… is not available to a supervised agent: it erases the session event log, which records what the agent did"). Host and operator sessions and the CLI keep both. A bound agent may also no longer set `events.retention` (a policy trims the same log); it can still read the current policy, and only host and operator sessions may set one. The two methods leave the set an agent token's `method_allowlist` may name: a new scope naming either is refused, and an existing agent whose saved scope names one keeps working, with that entry dropped and a warning naming it.
- The daemon's role table now recognizes `refuse_for_bound_agent` as the guard it is, so `fleet.inventory`, `fleet.composite`, `scheduler.os_list` and `tasks.list`, which have always refused a supervised agent, now carry role `operator_only` and leave the set an agent token's `method_allowlist` may name (an existing scope naming one keeps working, with the entry dropped and a warning). A refusal compiled in on only some targets, such as `connectors.add_stdio`'s Linux-only wall check, does not count, so that method stays callable by an agent on the Mac.

- On Apple Silicon, revoking only the `-MLX` variant an agent's `inference.model` name resolved to no longer drops the agent from `concierge.portfolio`, `concierge.status` and the daemon's revocation warning. The pin still runs on the plain row that stays live, and `revoked_pins` lists it against the revoked `-MLX` model, before and after a restart. CAR cannot tell when a pin was made, so a name pin set after its `-MLX` variant was revoked is listed too, for as long as the catalog revokes that variant; pinning the plain row by id clears the entry. Revoking only the plain row lists nothing, since the pin still runs on the `-MLX` variant (car#3153).

- `concierge.portfolio`, `concierge.status` and the daemon's revocation warning no longer drop an agent whose `inference.model` names a revoked signed model in different letter case (or, on Apple Silicon, by the name of its `-MLX` variant), or names one whose name has more than one path part (`acme/foo`). Every revoked signed row's name is now recorded for pins, and the record is matched the way the registry matches a name, so the agent stays in `revoked_pins` after the row leaves the registry and across restarts (car#2203).

- The router no longer picks a model that fails most of a benchmark because it is fast. A calibrated score under half of the benchmark's frontier now maps down to 0 instead of stopping at the measured floor (0.40). Before, measuring Qwen3.5-0.8B at 0.054 on car-judged raised its quality estimate from its 0.30 size proxy to 0.43. Every lane also excludes a candidate whose reliability is under 0.25 while another candidate clears it, and puts it last in the fallback chain. This includes `prefer_fast`, `prefer_local` and background, which skip the quality band. Before, `prefer_fast` sent voice replies to the 0.8B for its 364 tok/s.

- The router now gives every curated `frontier`-tagged remote row without a published benchmark the frontier tier prior (0.85), and every curated `balanced`-tagged one the balanced prior (0.72), whichever provider serves it. Before, only OpenRouter rows got these priors, so native rows such as `openai/gpt-5.6-sol:latest`, the `openai-codex/gpt-5.6-*` subscription rows and `anthropic/claude-opus-5:latest` sat at 0.60. On a `prefer_quality` code route that put them below `openai/gpt-5.4-mini` and a 4-bit Qwen3-4B (car-releases#105). The tag prior is a tier claim made when the row was curated, not a measurement. It stays below the measured ceiling (0.92), so a model with a published benchmark, such as `claude-opus-4-8` with its `car-judged` 1.0, still ranks above it, and graded outcomes move it either way. Auto-discovered (Community) rows still get no tag prior. With a Codex sign-in, a default code route can now go to the Codex subscription rows (`openai/gpt-5.6-sol:high`, `openai-codex/gpt-5.6-*`) where it used to go to a car-judged local model (about 0.75). An unpriced subscription row scores 0.5 on cost against about 0.96 for a priced API row, so when both are available the API row wins the tie.
- The builtin catalog has an `openai/gpt-6.1-sol:latest` row (Responses API, `frontier`), so GPT-6.1 Sol can be routed and pinned (car-releases#106). Its price ($2 input, $0.10 cached input, $2.50 cache write and $10 output per million tokens) and window (1,050,000 tokens including 128,000 output, stored as a 922,000-token input window as for `gpt-5.6-sol`) come from OpenAI's model page. Because it is priced below `gpt-5.6-sol`, it wins cost ties against that row. It still ranks below `claude-opus-4-8` on a `prefer_quality` code route until it is measured on the same car-judged suite (car-releases#111); until then, pin it to use it.
- Model discovery (`CAR_MODEL_DISCOVERY=1`) now derives the `/v1/models` URL from a bare-host endpoint such as `https://api.openai.com`, which is what every curated OpenAI row carries. Before, it found no `/v1` segment to anchor on and returned without making a request, so enabling discovery never discovered anything. Discovery remains opt-in. A discovered row now inherits only its transport (endpoint, credential variable and protocol) from the curated row it is cloned from. Its window is 128,000 tokens, its capabilities are `generate` and `code`, and its only supported parameter is `max_tokens`, so a discovered `gpt-4o` no longer claims a frontier row's 922K window, vision, reasoning or extended thinking.
- The `models.route` reference now explains where `reliability` and `score` come from: the catalog prior ladder, how graded outcomes shrink it toward the observed quality EMA, and the two existing channels for reporting graded outcomes from outside the daemon (`outcomes.resolve_pending` for a pending `trace_id`, within about 300 seconds of the call, and `benchmark_priors.json` at startup). A call that runs longer than about 300 seconds cannot be graded through `outcomes.resolve_pending` at all. There is still no RPC for reporting a graded result for a model the daemon did not serve (car-releases#110).

- The router's minimum-quality gate (added with the re-measured catalog) reads a model's answer quality, not its reliability. Failed calls never decay, so three transient failures before a first success put even a frontier model under the gate in every lane for good. Availability stays with the completion rate and the circuit breaker.

- The model router now honors `prefer_local` and `prefer_fast`. Before, a cold-start rule kept only trusted remote models for interactive requests whenever any cloud key, or a signed-in ChatGPT subscription through the Codex CLI, was available. It applied even when the request said `prefer_local`, so local preference sent work to the cloud, and every voice turn (`prefer_fast`) went to a remote model; with only a ChatGPT subscription, that was a Codex subprocess. The rule now applies only to plain interactive requests. There, a local model whose measured benchmark is within the router's quality band of the best trusted remote (Qwen3.8-27B is level with GPT-5.5) competes with the remotes instead of being removed. Weaker or unmeasured local models still defer.

- Two routing promises are now tested against the shipped catalog on every run instead of only printed by the ignored `routing_scenario_report`: a turn that carries tools goes to a model the catalog says can call them, under every key set and every Thompson draw; and with no cloud key, the quality lane's pick is never worse-measured on car-judged than a local model it ranked behind it. Each fails with its guard removed: without the router's ToolUse filter a tool turn goes to `vllm-mlx/gpt-oss-20b`, and with local benchmark scores ignored the quality lane picks qwen3.6-35b-a3b (0.514) over gemma-4-26b-a4b (0.658).

- The router compares local and remote latency on one scale: the time to a whole answer. A remote row's declared p50 is time to first token, and it used to be compared as-is against a local model's full 200-token answer time. A 600 ms Haiku p50 read four times faster than the roughly 2 s its answers take. A remote with a measured rate (car-bench, end to end) is now scored by that rate. A remote with only a p50 is scored as that p50 plus 200 tokens at 93 tok/s, the median of ten frontier rows measured on 2026-10-26. A local row's measured time to first token now counts too.

- The router no longer scores managed vllm-mlx MoE models at a tenth of their measured speed. A discount meant for hand-estimated speeds on the old Candle backend also applied to vllm-mlx rows: their speed comes from car-bench, but vllm-mlx reports no time-to-first-token, which is what the router uses to recognize a measured speed. Qwen3.6-35B-A3B, measured at 88.9 tok/s, was scored as 8.9 and got no credit for speed. The Candle and in-process MLX discounts are unchanged for the rows they describe.
- A request that names its task (`intent.task`) now decides the router's capability filter by itself. Before, the keyword guess from the prompt was added on top, and it is substring matching: "let me know", "prefix" and any mention of an error read as code. So a `task: chat` turn could require a code-capable model and lose Apple's on-device model and the small local models.
- `prefer_fast` requests (the voice track) no longer get an automatic reasoning budget when the prompt reads as complex.
- A lane default or preferred generation model no longer takes a request it cannot serve. A pinned model without tool support used to receive tool requests (and one without vision, image requests), and the call failed. Those requests now go to the router.

- The router's learned quality now moves only on real grades (accept/edit/reject and external grades). Before, every generation failure counted as a 0.0 quality grade while a success was never graded. A single stray failure cut a model's quality by a third, and successes never restored it, so routing favored whichever model happened to have the fewest failures. Failures count against availability instead: a model's reliability is its answer quality times the share of its calls that complete, `(completed + 1) / (resolved + 1)`, so a model that failed its last three calls and answered none drops to a quarter and loses even a deterministic `prefer_quality` pick. The circuit breaker is unchanged. Profiles saved before this change drop their failure-derived quality evidence on load and fall back to the model's catalog score; availability counters are kept. A task's quality now starts at neutral instead of 0.
- A `benchmark_priors.json` seed (written by `car models benchmark`) no longer overrides a catalog benchmark. The seed's raw scores are on a different scale, and a few trivial cases had lifted small models well above their measured quality.

- **The router's pick follows its scores instead of call volume.** Thompson sampling used to count every completed call as a success, worth the same as the model's whole quality score. So a model with a few hundred completed calls won nearly every draw against a better-scored model it had not tried. Graded quality and observed latency now enter the draw once, through the score, and call history only sets how tightly a model's draws cluster around it. Scores above 1.0, where locality bonuses stack, still rank instead of tying at a clamp. `RoutingConfig::prior_strength` now defaults to 20, the spread of an unobserved model's draw counted in virtual calls.
