- Automatic routing no longer picks a deprecated catalog row while a current one is available. `vllm-mlx/glm-4.7-flash:4bit`, deprecated because it loops on every vllm-mlx version, was still drawn for `prefer_fast` voice replies on a machine that had it installed. A deprecated row stays usable by name, and stays a candidate when nothing else is left.

- `cargo test -p car-inference` passes again on an Apple Silicon Mac whose build did not link the Swift MLX stack (no Metal Toolchain, `CAR_BUILD_SWIFT_LM=0`). The router test that checks a curated frontier model outranks small measured models on a quality code route now compares it against the local Qwen3-4B row only when this build can run that row. Otherwise it checks that the row is not a candidate. It also checks that the registry marks that row runnable exactly when this build can run it.

- Agents: when a run ends because one step didn't go through, its message now says which step failed and what the run had already saved, so a failed step's "Nothing was saved" no longer reads as the whole run's. For example, an agent that added two spreadsheet rows and filled two Word copies, then made one more fill that failed, used to end with "The agent stopped without finishing: Nothing was saved: these placeholders have no value: …" although its files were saved. It now ends with "The agent stopped without finishing: it saved "Transfers.csv", "Priya Raman.docx" and "Daniel Okafor.docx", but filling the Word template "Board Reminder.docx" didn't go through: Nothing was saved: …". The step is named in plain words ("adding a row to …", "writing …", "running a command", "sending a web request"); a tool without a plain name keeps its own. The files named are the ones the agent's file, spreadsheet and Word steps saved in its folder in that run (the first ten, then a count), each once and shown relative to the folder; the failed step's file is shown as the agent gave it, with your home folder as `~` (the failed step's own error is quoted as the tool wrote it). An agent without a folder works in a scratch folder that is deleted when the run ends, so its message names only the step that failed; so does a test run, which works in a copy of the folder. Changes made by a command or another tool aren't listed, since the run has no record of which files they touched. The run is still marked failed, in a chat, in Run now and in a scheduled run.

- `bookmarks.list` (`bookmarksList` / `bookmarks_list`) now reads a stock Safari `Bookmarks.plist` on macOS. It used to convert the file with `plutil -convert json`, which rejects any `<data>` or `<date>` value, and every real Safari file has both, so the call returned `available: false` with "Invalid object in plist for JSON format". The file is now parsed in-process and data and date values are ignored. A bookmark entry with no usable URL is left out and counted in a new `skipped` field instead of failing the whole listing, on both the Safari and Chromium backends (only entries visited before `limit` are counted). A read macOS refuses for lack of Full Disk Access now gives a reason that names Full Disk Access, a file whose own permissions deny reading says that instead, and a file that does not parse gives a third reason. The dashboard's permission panel labels a row "Needs permission" only when its reason is a permission problem; an unparseable or missing bookmarks file shows "Unavailable".

- CarCoder's sandbox and secret-isolation tests now run on Linux CI, and fail when the OS sandbox is missing instead of skipping. Before, about 60 of them returned early on GitHub's Ubuntu 24.04 runners, because AppArmor there restricts unprivileged user namespaces. The test summary still counted them as passed, so no Linux run had checked the `unshare` secret sandbox. The Linux `test`, `shared-process-test` and `coverage` jobs, and the release `test` job, now run `scripts/ci-enable-linux-sandbox.sh` before any test, which sets `kernel.apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_userns=0` on the throwaway runner and checks the wrapper. Each gated test now goes through `require_host_sandbox_for_test` or `require_sandboxed_for_test`, which fail the test when the sandbox is unavailable. The one exception is macOS inside an inherited Seatbelt sandbox, where the OS cannot nest a second profile. Two headless `car code` tests that were `#[ignore]`d for lack of a sandbox run by default again. A new Linux test runs a gated test with `unshare` removed from `PATH` and requires it to fail.

- A benchmark run whose time limits were scaled to the model's measured decode speed is no longer withheld from the catalog for its timeouts. `bench-consolidate.py` set aside any run with more than one timed-out or errored task, a rule written when every model got the same fixed limits and a slow model on a busy machine timed out on the clock alone. Under `decode-scaled-v1` limits each task already gets time in proportion to the model's own speed, so a task that still runs out is the model failing it: it scores zero, as it always did, and no longer counts as a hole in the measurement. Errors (a memory refusal, a server that never answered) and timeouts under fixed or unmeasured limits still do. `vllm-mlx/gemma-4-31b-it:4bit` gets its car-judged score, 0.532 (13 of 17 passed, 2 out of time at 484 s).
- The gemma-4 models served through vllm-mlx were re-measured after a thinking recovery stopped dropping the discarded pass's tokens, which had undercounted their decode rate (26b's task-time rate goes 29.5 → 56.1 tok/s). The car-judged scores fold in a third run: 26b-a4b 0.658, e4b 0.525, 12b 0.447 (its six out-of-time tasks now score under the rule above), e2b 0.334. `vllm-mlx/gemma-4-12b-it` gets a measured speed, 33.4 tok/s, where it had none.

- macOS: the coder and assistant shell sandbox now checks each command's own Seatbelt profile before running it, instead of reusing the verdict from whichever profile was checked first in the process. A profile that cannot start is refused with the sandbox-unavailable reason before anything runs, rather than failing at spawn. The first use of a profile costs one `sandbox-exec /usr/bin/true` probe (measured 8.8 ms median on a ~9.6 KB untrusted-repo profile); a profile that started is remembered until 1024 distinct profiles have started (then the set clears and the next use probes again), and one that failed is probed again on every use.

- The self-heal detector now feeds the repair loop. When the daemon runs from a validated `Parslee-ai/car` checkout and `<CAR_HOME>/heal.toml` enables `car heal` with a `Parslee-ai/car` target, each tick files one active `recurring_tool_failure` or `capability_miss` detection as an issue labelled with the target's opt-in label. The repair loop then fixes it under its review panel and opens a pull request, which CAR never merges. Before this, the two halves were unconnected: the detector wrote only private files and the repair loop saw only hand-labelled issues. An open issue already carrying the detection's marker is adopted instead of duplicated, and nothing is filed to the public releases tracker. Agent-health and metric detections are not filed. `selfheal.detections` reports the handed-over issue as `heal_issue` (`{repo, number, url}`), and `car selfheal list` prints its URL. While the repair loop qualifies it owns every fileable key the detector's own auto-fix (which has no review panel) has not already started, so one key never gets two coders, and `car selfheal fix` refuses a handed-over key. A detection that cannot be filed is skipped and logged instead of blocking the ones behind it.

- Self-heal no longer rewrites every active detection into `~/.car/selfheal/detections.jsonl` on every tick. A detection is appended when first seen and again only when something other than its observation timestamps changes; `car selfheal list` still shows when each was last seen, including after a daemon restart, from the keys each tick now records (`observed_keys` on the `selfheal.run` result). A metric alert whose measured value moves between ticks still counts as changed. One operator ledger had grown to 30 MB holding 27,010 rows for 47 distinct detections. Existing ledgers stop growing but are not compacted, because the ledger is append-only.
- Self-heal's silently-idle detection now counts a write to stderr as activity. It read only stdout's modification time, so every agent that logs to stderr (Python's `logging` default, and `car do --serve`) was reported idle while it was actively logging.

- `memory.load` no longer silently replaces the daemon's SHARED memory graph. A session with no `agent_id`, `memory_namespace` or work context is bound to that graph — which every other unbound session and the MCP endpoint use — so any holder of the daemon token (including a supervised agent that authenticated without its agent id) could wipe everyone's memory on boot. Such a load is now refused, before the file is read, with an error naming the remedies (authenticate with your `agent_id`, or bind a `memory_namespace` via `session.auth`, `CAR_MEMORY_NAMESPACE`, or `setMemoryNamespace` / `set_memory_namespace`), unless the call passes `replace: true`. A deliberate replacement is logged and recorded as a `memory.shared_graph.replaced` host event. **Behavior change for unbound callers:** NAPI `loadMemory(path, replace?)` and PyO3 `load_memory(path, replace=False)` gain the opt-in; agent- and namespace-bound loads are unchanged (car-releases#91).

- `car secrets migrate-from-env` no longer overwrites a key already in the secret store with a different value from your shell's environment unless you pass `--replace`. Without it, the key keeps its stored value, the command prints `<NAME>: not moved (the store already holds a different value; re-run with --replace)` and exits non-zero, the same rule the `~/.car/env` file import already followed. `--dry-run` prints `would not move (...)`, or `would replace` with `--replace`, and previews a key set in both `~/.car/env` and the shell the way the real run treats it. With `--replace`, the shell's value wins over the file's. A key the store could not be read for, or whose write the store refused, is reported as not moved and the remaining keys are still processed.

- Daemon shutdown no longer leaves a supervised agent running when the agent ignores SIGTERM and the shutdown budget runs out during its grace period. Every agent still alive when the stops finish or the budget expires is now sent SIGKILL before the daemon exits, under the same pid identity check a stop uses, and is still reported as a straggler. An `agents.stop`, `agents.restart` or `agents.remove` whose client disconnects during the grace period now still escalates to SIGKILL and finishes: the agent is recorded as stopped, restarted, or removed, instead of being left running with no supervision. An `agents.start` that lands while `agents.remove` is stopping the same agent no longer leaves a child running that nothing can stop. Once shutdown has begun, starting an agent (including the start half of a restart in flight) fails with "the daemon is shutting down" instead of spawning a child that would outlive the daemon's stop (car#3118).

- Sign-in copy now gives one account instruction on macOS, Windows, iOS and Android. Signing in does not create a Parslee account, so new users are told to create one at parslee.ai first and then come back and sign in, and no sign-in control offers account creation any more. Every sign-in button now reads "Sign in with Parslee": the macOS setup step, the macOS chat empty state and sign-in card, the Windows chat sign-in card, and the iOS and Android sign-in action and its status and connection lines (each was "Sign in or create …"). The signed-out setup row (macOS and Windows) no longer says new users create an account "in the same flow". The signed-out account screen on iOS and Android now carries the same sentence instead of "Sign in or create a Parslee account". When the daemon reports a sign-in problem without a message, the notice on all four platforms is now the daemon's own signed-out sentence instead of "Sign in or create an account to continue". The store listings, screenshot captions, landing page, reviewer paths and release evidence copy in `docs/mobile-store-assets/` and `docs/mobile-app-store-release.md` now say "Sign in with Parslee" and that new accounts are created at parslee.ai.

- On macOS, when a chat's browser needs you to sign in (`browser_await_signin`) while the CAR app is connected, or before the agent's browser has launched, CAR now opens a visible Chrome window that isn't flagged as automated, so "Sign in with Google" and other OAuth, SSO and passkey sign-ins are no longer refused as coming from an automated browser (Google used to answer "this browser or app may not be secure"). A browser already launched headless for the CarHost drawer is replaced by that window on the same browser profile, so persistent cookies and site storage carry over, and the page you were on reopens in it. Once the sign-in window opens, that agent keeps browsing in it until its process restarts, and `navigator.webdriver` is hidden only in that window; if you quit the window, the next browse opens a new one. If the CAR app has disconnected from a headless browser, the sign-in still ends at once telling you to open the CAR app. Ordinary browsing still launches as before, and `CAR_BROWSER_HEADLESS` set to a truthy value still keeps every browser windowless.
- `car_browser::chromium::LaunchOptions` has a new public `automation` field (`true` matches the previous behavior), so Rust code that builds it with a struct literal must add the field or use `..Default::default()`.

- Coder: `silence_window_secs` (`coder.toml`) and `car code-task --silence-window-secs` now raise a value from 1 to 29 up to a 30 second floor and log a warning, instead of killing checks before a sandboxed child has produced its first byte (start-up takes up to about 5 s under load) and reporting them as silenced. `0` still turns the window off; 30 and above are unchanged. (#3083)

- CarCoder: a builder shell or check that prints nothing no longer has its silence window extended by the CPU its own start-up uses (a login shell reading its profile, the sandbox wrapper setting up). Process-tree CPU counts as work only when it reaches 1% of one core over the whole silence window, the same bar the watchdog applies when the window expires. Sustained work still keeps a command alive; a burst of a few tens of milliseconds no longer moves the session clock (#2897).

- A daemon with no host token installed now says so, and why. That covers `--no-auth`, a failed host-token write at startup, and an embedder that builds the server state without one. `capabilities.list` and every successful non-consumer `session.auth` result gain `single_principal_mode: "host role unavailable: no host token is installed, so host-management methods refuse; cause: <cause>"`, `car doctor` prints a `Daemon auth:` line, the cause and the remedy for the running daemon (read from a new `<CAR_HOME>/doctor/daemon-auth-mode.json` record the daemon writes on every start), and `car-server` logs the notice once at warn on start. Clients test for the key; the cause text varies. A scoped inference consumer's pinned receipt never carries it. With a host token installed, none of these appears and both results are unchanged. The wire schema's `capabilities.list` result gains the optional field. What such a daemon refuses is in the "host authority fails closed" entry.

- Slow models can finish long answers. A request with no caller deadline used to be cut at 300 s per phase whatever the model, so a model decoding at 13–17 tok/s failed every answer longer than about 4,000 tokens and was then retried twice into the same cut. The unarmed bound is now 300 s or twice what the model's catalog decode speed needs to emit `max_tokens`, whichever is longer; a model with no measured speed keeps 300 s, and an armed `infer.deadline` still replaces it. The timeout error names the bound that was applied. Separately, CAR now starts `vllm-mlx` servers with `--timeout 3600`: the server's own 300 s default returned `504 Gateway Timeout` before CAR's client gave up, so raising the client bound alone did not help `vllm-mlx/*` models. On the judged benchmark this cut 6 of 17 tasks for `vllm-mlx/gemma-4-31b-it:4bit` and 4 of 17 for `vllm-mlx/devstral-small-2-24b:4bit`.

- `anthropic/claude-sonnet-4-5` now has a catalog row at Anthropic's list price ($3 input, $15 output, $0.30 cache read, $3.75 cache write per MTok; 200K context, 64K max output), so calls to it are priced instead of showing up as unpriced spend (#3539).

- Spend caps, build PR 4 (`docs/proposals/spend-caps.md`, "The refusal"): an agent's bundle now declares caps and CAR enforces them. `[spend] caps = [ { usd = 2, per = "hour" }, { usd = 10, per = "month" } ]` (any combination of hour, day and month, calendar periods in the server's zone) needs `[runtime] bundle_format_version = 3`, now the newest format CAR accepts, and `car_min_version = "0.59.0"` or newer, so an older CAR refuses the bundle instead of installing it with the caps dropped (a nightly, which reports a pre-release version such as `0.59.0-nightly.20261024`, is now judged as its release version by `car_min_version`); a bad cap is refused at `agents.install` (and at `car export` once the bundle unit builds it) with a message naming the field, and nothing is installed. CAR caps that agent's spend on model calls made through CAR: before each such call leaves CAR it is admitted against the caps, and at a cap it is refused with the new JSON-RPC error `-32018`, message `budget reached until 3:00 PM EDT` (or `… until 12:00 AM EST on Dec 1`), `error.data.reason: "budget_reached"` with the cap, the spend so far, `resets_at` and `zone`; a capped agent pinning a remote model the price list cannot price before dispatch is refused `price_unknown` (`price unknown for <model>; this agent has a spend cap`, or, for a priced model whose call cannot be estimated, `the cost of this call to <model> cannot be estimated before it is sent; this agent has a spend cap`; a router-chosen unpriced candidate is skipped instead, on `infer` and `infer_stream` alike, and leaves no refusal record, since only the refusal the caller receives is recorded); a model with no list price whose provider reports each call's cost (OpenRouter) may be used by a capped agent one call at a time, charged the provider's figure when the call finishes, and a second such call while the first runs, or while the provider's late figure for it is still pending (at most 11 minutes; the figure is looked up however the first call ended: a success, an error, a cancelled or broken stream, a candidate the router fell back from), is refused `unpriced_call_in_flight`; a call whose provider never reports a figure stays at $0 against the cap and is counted in `unpriced_calls` (an accepted gap: a cap cannot stop spending on a model whose provider never reports the cost, and `unpriced_calls` is how an owner sees it); a capped agent's call that ends after it was sent but before its answer was read (a stream that ends before its final usage chunk, a call the caller abandons or whose deadline elapses, a request that times out or is reset after it was sent, a 200 whose body could not be read, on every remote protocol including Bedrock) is charged its admission estimate, output ceiling included, so output already generated is not under-counted; such a request is no longer sent again, so it is never billed twice (only a connection that never opened is retried); and a capped agent whose spend the meter cannot record is refused `spend_unrecordable` (`spend could not be recorded`), with nothing charged and nothing left in flight for the call that did not run, while an uncapped agent's call runs and its ledger row is kept, so the call is recorded as it really ended. A principal at budget when the daemon restarts still gets its `spend.changed` clearing event, and a meter that cannot be read never publishes a false clearing. A late provider figure that prices an unpriced call takes it off `unpriced_calls` in `spend.status`'s periods as well as in the day history, so the two agree. The same code, message and data arrive on every path: the WebSocket error of `infer`, `infer_stream` (before the stream opens) or whichever method made the call; `SpendRefusedError` in the Node package (`name`, `code`, `data`, `reason`, `cap`, `resetsAt`, `zone`) and in Python (`car_runtime.SpendRefusedError`, a `DaemonRpcError`); over MCP a run document with `status: "spend_refused"` and `refusal`; and in CAR's own assistant loop a turn that ends with the refusal text and makes no further model call. Nothing is sent to the provider, nothing is spent, and the agent is not stopped, restarted or paused: its next call after the period resets goes through by itself. A refusal is recorded as a `model_call_refused` event in the session journal and the agent's call ledger, as a `{ record: "model_call_refused" }` step of a run that continued (the run is not marked failed; `car … trace` prints `Model call refused: <message> — <model> — <at>`), and on the agent's `refused_calls` counts. New `spend.status { agent }` (role `operator` in the generated table, callable by every supervised agent for itself; `agent` required for host and operator sessions, an agent-bound session reads only itself) returns the agent's three periods with spent, left, calls, unpriced and refused counts, its caps as declared, `at_budget` (derived from the meter and the current caps on every read, so it holds across a daemon restart and clears the moment a new install raises the cap), `caps_from_version`, `cap_history` and the meter's health; `car spend <agent> [--json]` prints it (with no agent inside a supervised agent's environment, the agent's own). `agents.list` rows gain `spend_caps`, `budget` (`uncapped | under | at_budget`) and `at_budget`; `car ls` notes "at budget until <time>". `spend.costs` rows now report `capped: true` and `at_budget` for capped agents, and its result's `at_budget` lists every principal at a cap. `heal.status` gains `spend_meter` (`{ healthy, last_error, clock, zone, zone_source }`). `events.query` and `events.cost_by_agent` take an optional `agent` and then read that principal's call ledger daemon-wide (its `inference_metered` and `model_call_refused` rows), a host or operator naming anyone, an agent only itself. A new `spend.changed` host event is published when a principal enters "at budget" and when the minute tick clears it after the reset. Caps change only by installing a new version: `agents.upsert` refuses a non-empty `spend_caps` field (an absent, null or empty one, as an `agents.list` row carries, is accepted and the installed caps kept), each change is appended to the agent's cap history, and a cap someone hand-edits into an installed manifest that fails validation registers the agent as `errored`, `auto_start` off, with `status_reason` "not started: <message>" rather than letting it run uncapped — the edited manifest is left on disk whatever else CAR persists, and a plain upsert of that agent is refused, so the bad cap never becomes no cap at the next restart; CAR's copy of a capped manifest keeps a `car_min_version` the bundle declared above `"0.59.0"`. A CLI process launched as a supervised agent (`CAR_AGENT_TOKEN` set) never runs a remote model in process: a strict remote pin, which runs in process for anyone else, goes to the daemon, and with the daemon down a remote model is refused with "this agent's model calls go through CAR's daemon, which is not reachable", so a capped agent shelling out to `car infer` is counted; a local model runs as before, daemon or not, capped or not. Prices: ElevenLabs Scribe (speech to text) is priced at $0.22 per hour of audio; ElevenLabs text to speech (`eleven-flash-v2.5`) and the `parslee/*` models (`parslee/fast`, `parslee/reasoning`, `parslee/advisor`) are unpriced, so a capped agent's call to them is refused `price_unknown` until the price list has them, while an uncapped agent's goes through with its cost unknown (as before, a stream that reports no usage settles on its input tokens only, never on the output ceiling its estimate held). Only model calls made through CAR are counted or refused: a call that goes around CAR (`claude -p`, an agent's own provider key, in-process FFI, `agents.invoke_external`) is neither counted nor stopped, and local models count $0. A capped orchestrator's workers are not counted against its cap until workers PR 5 adds the orchestrator's key to every worker call. No wire version bump: the method, error code, row fields, record and event are additive.

- Spend caps, build PR 3 (`docs/proposals/spend-caps.md`, "Who pays"): every model call the daemon makes now runs under a spend scope naming who pays. The WebSocket dispatcher sets it once per request from the session's bindings (`agent:<id>` for a session bound by `session.auth`, `worker:<handle>` plus its labels' keys for a worker, `credential:<id>` for an outside credential, the inference consumer's parent with cause `helper_agent`, else `operator`); the MCP endpoint sets it from its caller; a declarative agent's `agents.chat` turn and scheduled runs pay as `declarative:<id>`; CAR's own background work (self-heal, the evolution cadence, model maintenance, the concierge check) pays as `car:<purpose>`; a peer message to CAR's assistant is delivered under the sender's scope. The daemon installs `car_inference::spend::SpendGate` over the one spend meter (`car_server_core::MeterGate`, `install_spend_gate`) with a scope required, so a remote model call that reaches the engine with no scope is refused as a CAR bug instead of running uncounted. Nobody is capped yet: an admission always answers yes, and a ledger that cannot be written is logged, kept as the meter's last error, and never refuses a call. The worker meter's hand-written guard (`WorkerSpendGuard`) is gone; the engine's ticket admits and settles every attempt, voided when the error came before any dispatch (a new optional `voided` flag on the ledger's settle row; format version stays 2), and the provider's late figure still corrects the settled call. ElevenLabs voice streams (`voice.transcribe_stream.start`, `voice.tts_stream.start`) are admitted through the same gate and settle when the stream closes, text to speech at the characters spoken. The evolution measurer runs on the daemon's gated engine instead of a private one, and `evolution.run` replays under the caller's scope with cause `evolution`. `InferenceMetered` records (`infer`, now also non-stream; `infer_stream`; the assistant loop) gain optional `agent` (the paying principal's meter key), `cause`, `call_id`, `cost_usd`, `cost_source`, `model_used` and `fallback_from`. A daemon `multi.*` sub-agent's row now names the payer in `agent` (it named the sub-agent) and the sub-agent in a new `sub_agent`, and gains the run's `cause` and the runner's self-reported figures a second time in new `reported_tokens_in`, `reported_tokens_out` and `reported_cost_usd`; its `tokens_in`, `tokens_out`, `cost_usd`, `duration_ms` and `tools` are as they were. When the sub-agent's model calls went through the daemon's `infer` — at least one `call_id`-bearing `inference_metered` row was written while the `multi.run_agent` callback was open — the row is also marked `summary_of: "infer"`, and `events.cost_by_agent` and `metrics_totals` skip it, so the payer's calls are counted once, on the `infer` rows, where before the runner's figures were added on top; a run with any `infer` row is marked, so the self-reported cost of calls it made directly beside them is not folded. A row for a run that made no `infer` call — a runner calling its provider directly, or only self-reporting — is not marked and folds under the payer, as the figures folded before. A multi row without the mark — one an older CAR wrote, or one an embedder with no daemon meter writes, which is the only record of its calls — folds under its `agent` as before. No other existing field changed. `workers.spend` keeps its shape and its rules (a call counts the whole prompt sent, cached parts included; a call that reached a provider and reported no usage counts the input it sent), with one change the design asks for: a request the router moved to a fallback model now counts each attempt as its own call, so the failed attempt appears on the meter too. The assistant's Parslee Studio tools (`generate_studio_image`, `generate_music`, `generate_jingle`, `generate_song`, `generate_voiceover`, `generate_video`, `produce_commercial`), which send paid generations over HTTP from the daemon outside car-inference, are admitted through the same gate under the request's scope before the request leaves CAR and settled when the call ends on any path, at an unknown cost because Studio reports none; a refusal, or a missing scope where one is required, ends the tool with the gate's message and nothing is sent. The on-device inference worker child's engine is restricted to on-device dispatch (`InferenceEngine::restrict_to_local_dispatch`): a request the parent admitted at $0 as local that resolves to a remote row inside the child is refused at the child's admission entry (`SpendRefused`, reason `remote_in_local_worker`), nothing is sent, and the daemon reports that typed error rather than a generic worker failure.

- `spend.costs` (role `operator`) is the CAR-wide cost view over the one spend meter: one row per key — agents, workers, labels, the operator, CAR's own background work, declarative agents and outside credentials — with today's, the last seven days' and this month's spend in dollars rounded up to the cent, this month's calls and tokens, and how many calls had no reportable cost, plus a total (labels left out, since their workers' calls are already counted on the worker rows) and the meter's health, clock state and zone source. Only model calls made through CAR are counted; caps and "at budget" arrive in a later version, so `capped` is `false` and `at_budget` is empty. A supervised agent's session gets the operator-only refusal every such method gives; an outside credential's session is refused (`-32003 car_wide_not_allowed`) and the refusal recorded in its own rows. `car spend --all [--json]` prints it; `car spend <agent>` arrives with `spend.status`. Reached from Node and Python through the generated daemon wrappers (`spendCosts` / `spend_costs`).

- Spend caps: work the daemon hands to a background task keeps the caller's spend scope. Chat and peer turns, Coder sessions, contract evaluation and revision, discussion turns and planning, the heal sweep, declarative agent chats, voice turns and the daemon's durable operations all run under the session that asked for them, so their model calls are charged to that session's agent and never refused for having no scope. `scripts/check-spend-gated-dispatch.sh` (CI `ffi-parity`) now fails when a new way to reach a model appears outside the gated table of `docs/proposals/spend-caps.md`, or when a daemon task (in car-server-core, car-server or car-engine, by `tokio::spawn`, any `.spawn(..)` with a future, `std::thread::spawn`, or `spawn_blocking` with `block_on`) is spawned without the scope and without a reviewed reason in `scripts/spend-spawn-allow.txt`.

- Spend caps build PR 2, prices (`docs/proposals/spend-caps.md`). A model call's cost is now one answer, `car_inference::billing::call_cost`, returning `CallCost { micros, source }` from the first source that can say: the provider's own dollar figure, then the call's usage times the catalog price of the model that actually served it (a router substitute is priced as itself), then $0 for a local model (`is_local()`, so a loopback vLLM server is not local), then $0 for a subscription model, then unknown, never a made-up $0. Prompt-size `pricing_tiers` now apply, by the call's whole prompt, cached tokens included. `CostModel` gains `audio_per_minute` and `per_1k_characters`. These speech price fields exist and will price speech-to-text and text-to-speech calls once those calls are metered (spend PR 3); nothing meters them yet. Both are omitted when unset, and no shipped catalog row sets them. OpenRouter requests now ask for usage accounting, and OpenRouter's `usage.cost` is read into the new optional `TokenUsage.reported_cost_usd` on one-shot and streamed calls; it is absent, not zero, when the provider sends no figure, and the `infer` and `infer_stream` results carry it in `usage` only when present. Each protocol handler declares `reports_cost()`, true for OpenRouter only, and `billing::price_known` answers before a call is sent whether its cost will be known. The Parslee managed provider reports no figure: its gateway path parses none, so its unpriced rows are unknown. The workers meter behind `workers.spend` prices through `call_cost`: a worker call charged without OpenRouter's figure (a stream cut off before its final usage chunk) is looked up by generation id for up to 10 minutes and raised to OpenRouter's figure, never lowered, and left as first charged when the lookup fails. `workers.spend` keeps its reply shape; its figures change only where a provider figure or a tier now applies. A provider figure, on the response or found late, that is negative, not finite, above 100 times the call's list price (but never a ceiling below $1), or above $1,000 for a call with no list price is treated as no figure and logged. The workers ledger's settle and correct rows gain `cost_source`. Nothing is refused: no cap exists yet.

- Stable promotion now relabels the installers inside `car-darwin-arm64.tar.gz`. Before, it copied the nightly's tarball verbatim, so v0.58.0's embedded CarHost and `.pkg` still said `0.58.0-nightly.20261015`. The stable tarball is now the nightly tarball with its CarHost zip and `.pkg` replaced by the stable's standalone assets, byte for byte, and every other member unchanged. A new label check fails the promotion before tagging if any stable zip, `.pkg`, wheel, or installer embedded in the tarball carries a version other than the stable's. The full verifier runs the same check in a new `stable labels` area, so it now reports 14 areas.
- `publish-appcast-entry.sh` refuses to publish a Sparkle item that Sparkle would never offer: one whose `sparkle:version` does not order above every item already in its feed. Lab builds use the build date as `CFBundleVersion` (`20261015`), and Sparkle orders that above any semantic version. So after v0.58.0, a laptop-built `0.58.1` would have reached no one.
- Promoted stable release notes state the crates.io exception once instead of twice.

- A lab-promoted stable's `CAR-setup-x64.exe` now carries the stable version instead of the nightly's label (v0.58.0 said `0.58.0-nightly.20261015` in its version resource and in Apps & Features). A new `promote-windows-installer` CI job rebuilds only the installer envelope on a Windows runner from the promoted nightly's already-signed binaries, which it first proves byte-identical to the stable's own `car-win32-x64-msvc.zip`, signs it through Azure Trusted Signing when the secrets exist (the nightly's installer is unsigned until they do, and so is the stable's), and replaces it on both stable releases. `scripts/check-stable-labels.py` now reads a Windows installer's PE version resource, and the promoted-stable verifier's `stable labels` area checks the published installer.

- The stable Windows installer rebuild no longer demands Azure Trusted Signing credentials that the repository does not have: it signs when `AZURE_CLIENT_ID` is set and otherwise ships unsigned at parity with the nightly, but still refuses to replace a signed installer with an unsigned one.

- Approving, or starting, an Agent-project build is now refused when the agent it replaces was removed and identical content registered again under its id. The content fingerprint could not tell the two registrations apart, so a stale build overwrote the new agent and kept its `registration_id`. `coder.projects.create` returns the recorded `agent_registration_id` and `coder.get`'s `agent_target` carries `registration_id`; a project or build saved before this records none and is checked by fingerprint alone, as before. Reopening the edit moves it onto the current registration.

- A wheel built for another version no longer counts as a nightly's wheel. The Linux and Windows catch-up lanes count as attached only when their wheel carries the nightly's own PEP 440 version, and the same applies to `car-release status`, promotion admission and the per-area verdicts of `scripts/verify-release.sh`. The macOS wheel checks in CI's publish gate (`preflight-macos-assets`), the lab's stage step and the verifier's Mac-side, nightly and stable scopes apply that version too. Promotion admission refuses a nightly that carries any wheel of another version (`REFUSED foreign-wheel`), and the lab's relabel step checks the fetched wheels again before it relabels one. Before this, a stale wheel could make a lane look attached and be relabelled as the new stable (car#2949, car#2944).

- CarHost Chat (macOS): when your first message already asks for work on a schedule or on repeat ("every Monday…", "each morning…", "whenever a new invoice arrives…"), Parslee Core no longer asks "Want me to make this a standing agent?". It says it is setting one up, adds "or say 'just this once' if you only want it now", and goes straight to its questions. Say "just this once" and it does the job now and proposes no agent. If repeating work only comes up in passing, it still asks first. As before, nothing is built until you choose **Build it** on the draft.

- `agents.start` (and the start half of `agents.restart`) now fails with `agent <id> not found` when a concurrent `agents.remove` and a re-add of an identical spec and token land while the start is in flight. Before the start installs its supervision loop, it now refuses to install one into the re-added agent, which it used to start although nobody had asked. After the install, it used to report the stopped replacement as a successful start, although the loop it installed had been torn down. An in-place `agents.upsert` landing after the install (a new spec or a rotated token) still returns the started agent with its current spec (car#3160).

- `agents.start` (and the start half of `agents.restart`) now fails with `agent <id> not found` when a concurrent `agents.remove` drops the agent while the start is in flight, including when an agent with the same id and a new token is added back meanwhile. It used to report success with a made-up `starting` agent that no slot held and no process backed, or, after a re-add, to run the removed agent's command, environment, secrets and token under the new agent's name. When an `agents.upsert` replaces the spec while a start is in flight, the start now fails with `agent <id> changed while it was starting; nothing was started. Start it again to run its current spec` instead of running the old spec. A stale start also no longer stops the process of the agent that replaced it, and the daemon's boot-time `start_all` no longer counts an agent removed or replaced under it as started (car#3136).

- A Stop sent right after a chat turn is accepted now stops it. The daemon keeps an `agents.chat.cancel` for a session with no route yet for 60 seconds and refuses that client's next `agents.chat` / `agents.invoke_external` under the id with `the turn was cancelled before it started`, and once an agent acknowledges a turn whose route a Stop already removed it sends `agent.chat.cancel` again. `car do --serve` registers a turn's stop flag as it reads the `agent.chat` request, and the daemon client now calls a request handler in the order frames arrive (only the future it returns runs on a task), so a cancel written right behind `agent.chat` is no longer handled first and dropped. The Node and Python bindings queue an `agent.chat` turn in that ordered part too, and a cancel for a turn still waiting for the agent's chat handler now drops that turn and reports it cancelled; the agent's own `agent.chat.cancel` handler still hears every cancel. `car do --serve` stops every turn it accepted when the daemon ends its connection, since no cancel can reach them after that (new `DaemonClient::register_connection_lost_handler`). CarHost sends Stop again once the call of a turn it stopped before acknowledgement returns.
- **API change (`car-server-core`):** `AssistantService::handle_turn_with_model` and `handle_goal_turn_with_model` take an `AcceptedTurn` from the new `AssistantService::accept_turn(session_id)` in place of `session_id: &str`; `handle_turn` and `handle_goal_turn` keep their signatures. `AssistantService::serve_chat` serves `agent.chat` the way `car do --serve` does.

- Sandboxed checks on macOS find the operator's Homebrew `python3` again. A credential store holding a symlink into installed software (gcloud's virtualenv in `~/.config` links to Homebrew's interpreter; `~/.docker/cli-plugins` links into Docker.app) made CAR deny the interpreter itself, so bash skipped it on PATH and checks ran Xcode's `python3` without the operator's packages. Store links into Homebrew's Cellar/Caskroom, app bundles under `/Applications`, and the OS's own directories are no longer treated as store data; links to dotfiles and anywhere else still are.

- Streaming generate keeps the credential cause when every model fails: a stream whose Parslee or work-context credential is signed out, unreadable, denied or needs renewal now leads its error with that cause and its sign-in, membership or retry step, as the non-streaming path already did. It no longer reports only the last fallback's error. A stream that runs out of models also gets the same setup and re-authenticate hints as non-streaming generate.
- A request bound to a work context (buffered generate, streaming generate and classify) whose context credential cannot be used now reports that credential as the cause when routing without Parslee then fails too. Before, Parslee was left out of routing and the caller saw only an unrelated "model not found" or fallback error. A request that succeeds on another model is unchanged.

- Dropping a `car_registry::supervisor::Supervisor` now explicitly unlocks its manifest lock. Before, the lock was released only when the descriptor closed. While another thread in the process was forking a child, that child's inherited copy kept the lock held, so a supervisor reopened over the same manifest in the same process could fail with `AlreadyRunning`.

- The supervisor no longer puts the daemon-wide `CAR_AUTH_TOKEN` (or `CAR_AUTH_TOKEN_FILE` / `CAR_HOST_TOKEN`) into the environment of a supervised agent on Linux, or, on any platform, of an agent whose spec has `method_allowlist` or `work_context`. The removal runs last, so neither the daemon's default env nor the spec's own `env` can restore it. `CAR_AGENT_ID` and `CAR_AGENT_TOKEN` are unchanged, and unscoped agents on the Mac still receive `CAR_AUTH_TOKEN`. On Linux, an agent that used `car keys list` or the daemon token must use its declared keys and its agent session. `method_allowlist` scopes the agent credential; on the Mac it does not confine a same-user process (Parslee-ai/car#3008).

- Supplying a contract no longer approves its network checks (#2025). A check in a `coder.start` contract, a `car code` or `car code-task` `--contract-file`, or the FFI `contract_json` that must reach the network now carries its own `"network_approved": true`, which approves that exact check name and command, the same flag `coder.confirm_contract` already reads. Without it the check runs with network denied; the daemon holds a recognized network check under Not verified, where `coder.confirm_contract` can approve it, and `car code-task` refuses a contract file that has one, naming the checks. Declared credentials in a supplied contract are still approved as before. Verifier probes and `car coder-ab` mining probes in a supplied session no longer get network: they are not contract checks, so no flag approves them. Sessions saved before this change keep the approvals they recorded. `car coder-ab` corpus rows gain `network_approved_checks: [<check name>]` for the same purpose; both arms and the offline re-score honor it, and a name that is not a check in the row is refused at load.
- A completeness critic that fails is now retried once (on another model when its reply was unparseable). If the retry fails too, the session stops with `failure_kind: "infrastructure"` before any work runs, instead of proposing a contract that never had its completeness review. `outcomes_step` reports the critic as `retrying`, then `finished` or `failed`; it no longer reports `skipped`.

- Building CAR from source on a Mac whose `swift` defaults to the native SwiftPM build system (Swift 6.3.3 / Xcode 26.6) now links the in-process mlx-swift-lm text backend instead of building it and then dropping it with only a cargo warning. car-inference's build script now passes `--build-system swiftbuild`, the build system whose output layout and compiled Metal shaders it already depended on; a toolchain that does not know that value fails the Swift build and the script reports that error.

- Project `.car/` files are now looked for only from the working directory up to the git repository root, and CAR's own `~/.car` (or `$CAR_HOME`) is never read as a project. Before, three readers walked to the filesystem root. A team `.car/connectors.toml` search under your home directory could pick up `~/.car/connectors.toml` and connect every server in it. The project `.car/update-prefs.json` override could pick up your own user `~/.car/update-prefs.json` and count its policy as a team's choice. Meetings could be written into a `.car/` above the checkout, or into the old `~/.car` after `CAR_HOME` moved the state root. A file in the repository root, or in a folder between it and the working directory, is still found, also when the working directory is a symlink into the repository.
- Outside any git repository, team connectors are read only from the working directory's own `.car/connectors.toml`, the same as project policies; a team file in a parent folder no longer applies.
- `car do` and the coder no longer take `~/.car` as a project when run from your home directory: its `tool-labels.json`, `production-gates.toml` and (with `CAR_HOME` relocated) `policies/` are not loaded as project configuration. The policy check run from your home directory no longer reads the operator's rules twice.

- A tenant-scoped proposal (`scope.tenantId`) is now checked against the state each of its actions actually executes against (car#3121). `state_write`, `state_read` and `assertion` actions read and write the tenant's own keys, so the admission gates, the strict transactional pre-check, per-action preconditions and `state_dependencies`, replan verification and the per-action `state_before` a trajectory records now read the tenant's keys for them. Previously these checks read the shared store, so a tenant proposal whose dependency was in its own tenant state was refused as an unmeetable dependency, and a key that existed only in shared state passed the checks even though the action could not read it. `tool_call` actions run on the unscoped path, so their checks still read shared keys, as before. A tenant `state_write` therefore no longer counts as setting a key a later `tool_call` depends on. Under a tenant, those checks no longer see other tenants' keys: previously a tenant `tool_call` could name another tenant's stored `tenant:<id>:<key>` in a dependency or precondition and learn from the refusal whether it existed and what it held. A replanner for a tenant proposal is handed the state under raw key names, as before, minus other tenants' keys. Unscoped proposals are unchanged. Policies are the deliberate exception: a `require_state` policy still reads the shared store for a tenant proposal, because policies are operator rules and a tenant must not satisfy one by writing its own copy of the key. `car_validator::validate_action` and `check_precondition` now take any `car_ir::precondition::StateView` (a `&StateStore` still works). `ScopedStateView` implements `StateView`, `car_state::tenant_key`, `car_state::key_visible_to_tenant`, `StateStore::visible_to` and `StateStore::versioned_snapshot_visible_to` are added, and `car_engine::verify_gate::blocking_errors` takes the proposal's tenant.

- The transactional pre-check of a tenant-scoped proposal (`scope.tenantId`) now reports a conflict between a `tool_call` declaring an effect on a key and a tenant `state_write` of that key (write-write), or a tenant `state_read` / `assertion` reading it (read-write), when no dependency orders them (car#3133). A tool call's callback `state_changes` land in the tenant's namespace, so its declared `expected_effects` count as writes to both the shared key and the tenant's copy, which is how the static-verification gate already modeled them. Previously the pre-check counted them as shared writes only, so both actions ran and execution order decided the surviving value. In strict mode the conflicting actions are rejected; in warn mode the conflicts are recorded as `TransactionConflict` events and the actions run. The pre-check now takes ordering from the proposal as written, as the executor does, so a tenant action that lists the key in `state_dependencies` after the tool call is a sequenced step and runs, unless that action also writes the key: the executor records only the last declared writer of a key, so that pair conflicts, as it does in an unscoped proposal. Two unordered tool calls declaring the same key now report two write-write conflicts (the key and the tenant's copy) and reject the same two actions as before. An explicit `write_set` or `read_set` on a tenant `state_write`, `state_read` or `assertion` is now read in the tenant's namespace, so it no longer conflicts with a `tool_call` declaring the same raw key in its `write_set`, which only the tool's handler writes, in the shared store. `car_verify::check_transaction_ordered_by` is added: `check_transaction` with the "runs before" relation taken from a separate list of the same actions.

- Test suite: six tests that ended in a discarded `matches!(...)` (so they could never fail) now assert, and CI rejects any new discarded `matches!` result. The `car code-task` tests no longer copy the developer's whole Cargo registry into every baseline check, and test builds optimize the Argon2id and curve/hash/AEAD crypto dependencies, so those tests run in seconds instead of tens of seconds. Release builds and every KDF parameter are unchanged.

- A turn CAR recovers from a thinking-only pass (`stop_reason: "thinking_recovered"`) now reports the tokens and time of both passes. The pass that thought until its token ceiling and answered nothing was dropped from `usage` and `latency_ms`, so a recovered turn looked like a 30-token answer that took a minute: measured decode speed for thinking models read up to 60x low (gemma-4 tasks on vllm-mlx showed 0.5 tok/s against a real 34 tok/s), and the billed tokens of the discarded pass were missing from the usage callers sum. `usage.prompt_tokens` stays the retry's, since it is the context the turn ended at.

- A recovered thinking turn (`stop_reason: "thinking_recovered"`) whose retry or first pass reported no usage no longer shows the other pass's provider charge as the cost of the whole turn. The missing pass's output is now estimated (a first pass that stopped at its limit counts that limit), and `usage.reported_cost_usd` becomes the known provider charge plus the catalog price of the estimated pass. When that pass has no catalog price, the known charge stands for the turn. A turn's figure (including the sum of two provider figures) that lands above the sanity ceiling CAR applies to provider figures is now held at that ceiling instead of being dropped for the list price. Billing checks the turn against the price of the model that answered, so when the two passes ran on differently priced models the figure can still end below the first pass's charge. When neither pass reported usage, the turn reports none, as before.

- A Parslee token that expired while Parslee's token service was rate-limited or down no longer reports the account as signed out. `parslee.capabilities`, `parslee.auth`, Parslee product gates, Studio, the jev client and queued feedback used to answer "not signed in — run `car auth login`" because the stale token drew a 401 on the session check. That 401 now triggers one token renewal: a renewed token loads normally, a renewal that is temporarily unavailable is reported as a service failure ("You are still signed in — retry shortly"), and only a renewal that needs sign-in still reports the account as signed out. `car auth status` and `auth.status` report the same outage instead of the 401 they were renewing. Parslee inference rejected for the same reason now fails as a transient error instead of the original 401, so `car do` no longer calls it a sign-in failure; a 403 (signed in, no access) keeps its access-denied answer. Automatic renewal inside the 30-second retry interval after the token service rejected the refresh token now reports that sign-in is needed, rather than a temporary outage.

- Security: tokio updated from 1.53.1 to 1.53.3 for RUSTSEC-2026-0386 (`AsyncFd::new` and related safe constructors accepted any raw file descriptor, which could lead to a use-after-free in tokio's I/O driver when a descriptor was closed and reused while still registered). CAR's own code never constructs an `AsyncFd`, so the exposure was through dependencies only.

- A certificate-verified Lattice connection with a colleague's CAR now replaces an older unverified one (a LAN knock or an invite code) with the same CAR, instead of being refused because a connection already exists. Once it is active, each CAR ends the unverified connection on its own side. An offer a CAR refuses without asking its owner is announced as a `lattice.offer_refused` event, so the person waiting for it sees what happened.

- Coder: a short `silence_window_secs` in `coder.toml` could mark a session Stalled while the verifier ran a busy check that printed nothing, because the verifier measured that check against a fixed 15-minute window instead of the session's own. The verifier now uses the session's configured window (a window of 0, which disables the watchdog, keeps the 15-minute default). `car code-task --silence-window-secs` is honoured the same way.

- Independent verification now stops when the session's time limit passes. Checking a built agent counts against the time limit of the build attempt it checks (10 minutes by default; a repair after a failed check starts a new attempt), and an ordinary coder session with `max_session_wall_secs` (or `car code-task --max-session-wall-secs`) has its cap applied to verification too. Before, a verifier or auditor model call that never returned kept running until the silence window (900 seconds by default) ended the session. At the limit, the in-flight model call is dropped, a running check is killed (a file copy already preparing a check's tree finishes in the background), and the session ends `budget_exhausted` with a `budget_exhausted` event, so the build card shows a build that ran out of time. Its workspace is kept. Verification that finishes before the limit is unchanged.

- `verify-release.sh` on a nightly now ends with a pass or fail line for each of five areas (Mac, Linux, Windows, pip, npm) and names every catch-up file it cannot find, including the catch-up markers, the Linux arm64 files, the Linux `.node` and the Linux and Windows wheels. It no longer passes while `car-release status` shows a lane pending, because both read what a lane contributes from one place. When a catch-up file is missing, the hint points at re-running the failed catch-up jobs rather than at a macOS backfill. `--scope catch-up` runs just those five areas.

- A model id written without a tag, such as `anthropic/claude-haiku-4-5` (how a declarative agent pin of `model: claude-haiku-4-5, provider: anthropic` is addressed), now names the catalog row tagged `:latest`. Such a pin used to find no row: generation refused it as model not found, a saved agent's run treated the model as unavailable (it stopped, or Parslee stood in), and the reasoning allowance fell back to the plain 2048-token answer, leaving no room for a saved medium or high reasoning effort on a legacy Claude model. It now runs on that row with its saved reasoning, exactly as the tagged id does. An exact id still wins where both exist, a tagged id is never replaced, and an id no row has is still refused (car#2751).

- Qwen3.8-27B now decodes about 35% faster on Apple Silicon (16 → 21.5 tok/s on an M5 Pro) through speculative decoding with its own MTP drafter. A `managed_vllm_mlx` catalog source can name a drafter as `draft_hf_repo`; CAR downloads it into the model's directory (253 MB for Qwen3.8-27B) and starts vllm-mlx with it attached. Retiring the model frees the drafter too. Rows without a drafter serialize exactly as before.
- Runtime compatibility: CAR now requires vllm-mlx 0.6.0 or newer and upgrades an older managed install the next time it needs the runtime. Versions before 0.6.0 load only Gemma 4 drafters. In the managed venv the upgrade changes only vllm-mlx and llguidance; mflux, ltx and mlx-vlm keep their versions.
- A generation that uses its whole `max_tokens` budget now reports `stop_reason: "length"` (so `was_truncated()` is true) even when the server says `"stop"`. vllm-mlx does this with a speculative drafter: it returns 2002 tokens on a 2000-token budget and calls the response finished. A caller's stop sequence and any other provider reason are left alone.

- Qwen3.8 and other `vllm-mlx` models whose prompt opens a `<think>` block now answer instead of returning their unfinished thinking. The first request gets half of `max_tokens`, or the caller's `budget_tokens` when it fits. If the model is still thinking when that runs out, CAR closes the thought in Qwen's documented words and asks for the answer with the remaining tokens, through `/v1/completions` over the same prompt the server rendered. Before this, a response cut off mid-thought came back as raw chain-of-thought presented as the answer, because vllm-mlx's non-streaming reasoning parser doesn't account for a `<think>` opened by the prompt. vllm-mlx's own `thinking_token_budget` can't be used instead, because it isn't applied while the MTP drafter is attached. A response that finishes within its budget is returned unchanged, with no second request.
- Tool calls in the XML form Qwen3.5 and later emit (`<tool_call><function=…><parameter=…>`) are now parsed, typed from the tool's JSON schema. Before, only Qwen3's JSON form was recognized. The thinking continuation above relies on this for answers that end in a tool call.
- OpenAI-compatible responses that carry `reasoning_content` (vLLM, vllm-mlx, DeepSeek) now report it as the result's thinking, as OpenRouter's `reasoning` already was.
- Chat templates that write values through `| tojson` (every Qwen3.5+ template does this for tool definitions) now render. They render as Python's `json.dumps` would, which is how the model saw them in training.

- `GenerateParams.thinking` now reaches `vllm-mlx` models. `Off` and `On` are sent as `chat_template_kwargs.enable_thinking`, and `Auto` leaves the model template's default. Before, vllm-mlx always rendered with thinking on. A caller asking for `Off` got thinking anyway, and the automatic "retry without thinking" after an empty, length-stopped answer repeated the same request. On Qwen3.8, a short question answered with thinking off takes 5 tokens where it took 54. Hosted providers are never sent the field.

- When a `vllm-mlx/*` model needs the vllm-mlx runtime and `uv` is not installed, the error no longer suggests `pip install vllm-mlx`, which often puts the runtime somewhere CAR does not look. It now says to install uv and run `uv tool install vllm-mlx`, or to set `CAR_VLLM_MLX_BIN` to the path of a vllm-mlx binary. When the installed runtime is older than CAR needs, the error names where it is and how to upgrade it: `uv tool install --upgrade vllm-mlx` for one uv installed (its tool environment holds uv's `uv-receipt.toml`), the `uv pip install` command for CAR's own runtime, and otherwise the tool that installed it (uv will not replace a program it did not install), or `CAR_VLLM_MLX_BIN`. The never-download refusal for an old runtime gives the same upgrade advice.
- CAR now also finds a vllm-mlx that `uv tool install` put in the directories named by `UV_TOOL_BIN_DIR`, `UV_TOOL_DIR`, `XDG_BIN_HOME` or `XDG_DATA_HOME`, when they are absolute paths set in CAR's own environment.

- Local vllm-mlx models: while a supervised vllm-mlx server loads, CAR no longer sends `GET /health` to its port until the server's own process group is listening there (macOS and Linux). The port is free for the whole model load, so another program could take it; CAR used to poll that program every half second, and a 2xx from it counted as the model being ready. Now the program sees no requests while CAR can see it holds the port, and the load fails with the server's exit instead of reporting a stranger as ready. Two gaps remain: the ownership check and the probe are separate steps, so a program that takes the port between them can still receive one probe; and when the system returns no listener information for the server's process group, CAR probes the port as before.

- On Linux, stopping a walled agent now stops everything it started. Each walled agent is started by a small helper process of CAR's own binary (`car-agent-init` in `ps`, the pid `car ls` shows), which stays outside the agent's wall and keeps every process the agent starts in its own tree, including one that detaches with `setsid` after its parent exits. Stop, remove, a stop that outlasts its grace period, the agent's own process exiting, and the daemon exiting or being killed each end that whole tree; before, such a detached process kept running. The agent's exit status is reported as before, a missing command is still an immediate start error, and the daemon still reaps only its own children, so no other process it runs loses its exit status. A walled agent still running when a new daemon starts is now stopped and started again instead of being taken back under supervision. Programs that embed CAR (the Node and Python packages) cannot provide the helper, so they refuse to start walled agents with a reason; run those agents under `car-server`. The Mac and CAR's own assistant are unchanged.
- The container image now runs `tini` as PID 1 (`ENTRYPOINT ["/usr/bin/tini", "--", "car-server"]`), so a process handed to PID 1 is reaped instead of lingering. A `command:` in `compose.yaml` still replaces only `car-server`'s arguments.

- `web_search` (Parslee Core's assistant tool): a result link inside any element marked hidden (`hidden`, `aria-hidden="true"`, or an inline style that hides it) is no longer reported, not only one inside a `.result` block or a result's title or snippet. A hidden formatting element that a browser opens again hides what follows up to the next end tag of its name in its scope (an end tag inside a table cell opened after it does not end it), and a page whose `html` or `body` is marked hidden reports no results. A result page whose result links are all hidden is reported as a failed read, not as a search that found nothing. A result, an instant-answer topic or an abstract source whose address is not an `http` or `https` URL (`javascript:`, `data:`, `mailto:`, a relative path) is no longer reported; such a result link still ends the result before it.
- The daemon's web search (`car_inference::search`): DuckDuckGo results skip a result block, link or snippet that is hidden (or sits inside a hidden element or a `template`) and leave hidden text out of titles and snippets, using the same reader of hiding markup as Parslee Core, now `car_inference::web_markup`. A DuckDuckGo page whose result links are all hidden is a parse error rather than an empty result. DuckDuckGo, Tavily and Parslee results whose url is not an `http` or `https` url are dropped.

- Several web searches in a row now return results instead of only the first one. Parslee Core's `web_search` and the keyless `search` capability ask DuckDuckGo's result page with a form POST, as its own search form does, rather than a GET it starts refusing after a search or two. Requests to DuckDuckGo are spaced at least 2.5 s apart across the process, each search has a 60 s budget for its waits and requests, and after DuckDuckGo throttles a request (a 202, 403 or 429 from any of its endpoints, or its challenge page in answer to a direct request) CAR leaves it alone for 60 s instead of extending the throttle. On macOS, when CAR's browser is already running, `web_search` first reads the result page in a separate background tab of it; the tab leaves the agent's page and the drawer's tabs as they were and refuses every request the page makes to another site. A search never starts the browser or opens a window. When DuckDuckGo refuses that browser (it serves a headless browser its challenge page) or the tab cannot read the page, the search asks DuckDuckGo directly, says so, and skips that browser for 30 minutes. DuckDuckGo's own ad links are no longer returned as results, and the keyless `search` capability now reports a throttled search as an error rather than as no results.

- Security: rustls-webpki updated to 0.103.16, and webpki-roots and webpki-root-certs to 1.0.10, for RUSTSEC-2026-0382 (certificate verification could accept distrusted intermediates) and RUSTSEC-2026-0381/0383 (the bundled Mozilla root set still carried `emSign ECC Root CA - G3`, so TLS connections could trust paths through distrusted emSign intermediates whose private keys are public).

- Windows CI no longer runs the coder tests that drive a builder child, which CarCoder refuses to start where it cannot confine writes; a Windows test now asserts that refusal instead.

- Windows CI is green again: a test helper that a Windows test uses is no longer compiled only on Unix, a test kept its temporary directory alive only on Unix, and `apps/host-windows/Cargo.lock` gained the TLS packages the workspace now pins.
- Windows: a spend-ledger append that failed part-way is now cut back as intended. The cut ran through the append handle, which on Windows has no write-data access, so it was refused and the torn line stayed: the next read of that agent's ledger failed, and a failed directory sync on a new ledger kept and charged the rows it should have dropped.
- Windows: when a declarative agent stops unfinished, a file it saved under two letter cases (`Transfers.csv`, `transfers.csv`) is named once, as on a Mac. File identity was read only on Unix, so NTFS's case-insensitive names counted as two files.
- Windows security hardening: `memory.persist` and `memory.load` now refuse a hard link to one of the daemon's own memory snapshots, as they already did on macOS and Linux. The check compared files by identity only on Unix, so on Windows a hard link under an export name was judged by that name alone and let through.

- Windows CI: the relay wire golden vectors check out with LF line endings, the Lattice end-to-end fixture starts its agent through an absolute `cmd.exe`, and the context-resolution test uses a path that is absolute on Windows. The public doc-link check accepts a 401/403 from the ChatGPT backend endpoint the docs name as the Codex credential's only destination.
- Windows: when a scheduled agent run has to start the daemon itself, the daemon inherits PATH as it is. It used to split PATH on `:` to put the macOS tool folders in front, which broke every `C:\...` entry.

- Release: stable promotion now opens the `microsoft/winget-pkgs` pull request for `Parslee.Car` itself (komac, using the `WINGET_PKGS_PAT` secret). It skips with a warning while the secret is unset and skips a version winget already has or has a pull request for.

- `wire-schema.json` (served by `server.schema` and published at car.parslee.ai/wire-schema.json) now describes JSON-RPC **params** as well as results (car-releases#98). Each `rpc.<method>.params` schema is generated from the Rust type the handler deserializes, and `coverage.rpc_params` names every dispatch method once: covered (91 methods, including `server.handshake`, every `host.*` approval method, `session.init`, `runs.start`, the `multiplayer.*` methods other than `submit_stage`, and the `mail.*` methods that take params), `not_read` (99 methods whose dispatch arm passes the handler no params, such as `host.subscribe` and `mail.accounts`), `typed_without_schema` (18 methods parsed into a type that does not derive a schema yet), or `unresolved` (the remainder, read field by field or through a path the generator does not follow). Params schemas follow serde's input rules, so optional and defaulted fields are not `required` and an object is closed only where the type uses `deny_unknown_fields`. The approval channel is covered end to end: `rpc.host.subscribe.result`, `notification.host.event.params`, and `type.host_approval_request` for the `approval.requested` and `approval.resolved` payloads. All six `mail.*` results are covered. `coverage.complete` remains `false`, because most params and results are still uncovered and server-to-client notifications other than `host.event` are not inventoried yet. Seven handlers that fall back to defaults on unparseable params (`mail.inbox`, `mail.mailboxes`, `meeting.list`, `registry.list`, `registry.reap`, `events.truncate`, `models.search`) are named in the schema's `limitations`, since for them the schema is stricter than the daemon. No method or protocol version changed. Fields that handlers used to read beside their parse are now part of the parsed params, so a mistyped one is refused instead of silently defaulted: `remote` on `multiplayer.*` (a non-string used to mean `origin`), `push` on `multiplayer.merge_check` (a non-boolean used to mean `false`), and `session_id` on `policy.register` (a non-string used to register the policy globally). `multiplayer.get`, `multiplayer.abandon` and `multiplayer.merge_check` report a missing `item` as an `invalid params` error.

- Workers, part two (`docs/proposals/orchestrator-workers.md`): workers are bounded, stoppable and visible only to their owner. Everything a worker writes lands under `<CAR_HOME>/workers/<handle>/` (logs, session journals, pid record, key-read and refusal rows, end reason), and on Linux its wall is its own `home/`. Stopping, removing or a for-good end of an orchestrator stops every worker it started within the stop grace period (the orphan rule); a worker's `time_limit_secs` is now enforced; each worker records how it ended. An orchestrator may `agents.start`, `stop`, `restart`, `wait` and `remove` its own workers (`agents.remove` takes `stop?: bool` and moves from the host role to the agent-or-host role), and any other worker handle is refused with `-32003 not_your_worker`. `agents.list`, `agents.health` and `agents.peers` show a worker only to the host and its own orchestrator. Removing a worker deletes everything that could start or resume it and keeps its records marked removed. Workers still start nowhere until the fence verdict is wired in.

- Workers, part one (`docs/proposals/orchestrator-workers.md`): an orchestrator's manifest can declare the worker bundles it may start (`[[workers]]`, `[workers.limits]`), a worker bundle (`[agent] role = "worker"`, `[worker] models`, `[report] counters`, `bundle_format_version = 2`) installs into the bundle store, and the new `workers.start` and `workers.report` methods start a labelled worker under a CAR-minted `wk_` handle and take its structured report. Every path that would run a worker passes one admission point (daemon auth, Linux with a passing network-fence self-test, label state, the author's caps over the tree), and a worker's session may call only `session.auth`, `server.handshake` and `workers.report`, with nothing pushed to its socket but its own responses. **No worker starts yet:** until the fence self-test is wired in, `workers.start` and the install or start of an orchestrator are refused with `-32013 fence_unavailable` (or `auth_disabled` under `--no-auth`). New error codes `-32013`, `-32014`, `-32015`; JSON-RPC errors may now carry `data.reason`. Unknown keys in the new manifest sections are refused, and CAR skips an installed manifest whose worker sections break the install rules.

- An orchestrator can now hand each worker its own secret values when it starts it: `workers.start { ..., values: { SOURCE_PASSWORD: "…" } }`, for example a short-lived token scoped to one tenant. Each name must be one the worker bundle declares in `secrets`. The worker reads the value in its environment under that name and through `secret.get` from its own session (now on the worker list, for declared names only), and it takes precedence over the CAR store for that worker alone; installed agents still read the one CAR-wide value per name. Each value must be at least 8 bytes. The values are kept owner-only in `workers/<handle>/values/`, a folder of their own that backups leave out, so the worker gets the same ones after a crash restart, `agents.restart`, a daemon restart or a boot resume, and they are deleted when the worker is removed or its start is refused. A worker whose handed value has gone missing is refused rather than started on the CAR store's value. A name the bundle does not declare is refused with `undeclared_value`; a name CAR controls, or a value equal to the daemon, host or a supervised agent's token, with `reserved_value` (the attempt is recorded without the value). No reply, error, event, record, export or daemon log line carries a value, and a value a worker prints is masked in its logs once its process ends. An orchestrator can tell a CAR takes `values` from the new optional handshake capability `workers.start-values.v1`.

- Workers, part three (`docs/proposals/orchestrator-workers.md`). **Workers start on Linux, without a network fence.** Where the file wall applies (Landlock) and the daemon is each worker's per-agent init, `workers.start` now starts workers; macOS, Windows and Linux hosts without the file wall still refuse with `fence_unavailable`. There is no network fence yet, so such a worker can reach any host, and every surface says so: worker rows in `agents.list` and `car ls --json` carry `network_fence_warning` ("Network fence: not built yet. This worker can reach any host."), shown in red by `car ls` and the dashboard, and by `car doctor` for workers running now; the `workers.start` reply carries it, and every start of a worker, restarts and boot resumes included, is a `worker_started` event in its orchestrator's and the host's event log (`events.query`) and a line in the worker's start records; a new session is given the newest 256 starts per worker and a `worker_starts_omitted` event counting the rest, and a start whose record cannot be written is logged and still delivered, with `record_error`. Workers may now call `infer`, `infer_stream` and `infer.cancel`: only listed request fields (`-32003 model_field_not_allowed` otherwise), pinned to the bundle's `[worker] models`, with a per-label value first in every cached prompt position, checked on the request bytes before each call is sent, so provider prompt caches never cross labels (any JSON schema shape is accepted), and usage reported without a cache split. The new `workers.spend` reports each label's and each worker's model spend per calendar day in the server's time zone, streamed calls included, every call that reached a model counted however it ends (cancelled, failed, timed out or cut off too) and a call refused before reaching one counted not at all, on the day it was sent, and local models at $0; the totals survive restarts and are not yet enforced as caps. At boot a worker comes back only beside its orchestrator (#3349), and a caller's refusal record caps the id it tried at 128 characters (#3350).

- **Remote access for outside orchestrators: the public door and outside credentials.** An agent installed from a bundle with `[transport] kind = "outside"` is an outside agent: CAR runs no process for it, and it reaches CAR only through an owner-issued credential. The host issues one with `credentials.issue` (`kind: "orchestrator"`, bound only to an outside agent; reserved ids and agents CAR runs are refused) and manages them with `credentials.list`, `credentials.revoke` (idempotent, never re-enabled) and `credentials.refusals`; refusals use the new code `-32017`. The token (`car_oc_` + 43 base64url) is shown once, and only its SHA-256 is stored, in an owner-only file under `<CAR_HOME>/credentials/`. A remove and reinstall of the agent ends credentials issued before it, and an expiring credential's open sockets close at `expires_at`. `car-server --public-bind <addr:port>` (`CAR_PUBLIC_BIND`) opens the door, behind a TLS terminator, and never under `--no-auth` or without a host token: it serves only `GET /v1/ws` (Bearer outside credential, checked before the upgrade; `400` for a `/v1/ws` request without `X-Forwarded-Proto: https`, JSON `401` bodies, `429` past 16 connections per credential, `503` past 256 connections not yet upgraded, 1 MiB frames) and `GET /healthz`. A door session is pre-bound, starts with `server.handshake`, refuses `session.auth`, and may call only `server.handshake`, `server.schema`, `workers.start`, `agents.list`, `agents.health`, `agents.stop`, `agents.wait` and `agents.remove` (its own workers) on every platform; close codes `4001` revoked, `4002` expired, `4004` shutting down (`4003` reserved). `session.auth` on the private port refuses any `car_oc_` token. Unset, nothing changes.

- **Workers: one `agents.wait` says how a worker ended, and daemon restarts bring the right workers back.** `agents.wait` takes the new target `"ended"` (stopped or errored), and a worker's reply now carries `end_reason`, CAR's own `end_message` (for example "stopped: kept crashing"), `last_error` (`{ kind: "exit" | "signal" | "timed_out" | "kept_crashing" | "spawn_failed" | "environment_failed", exit_code?, signal? }`) and its last structured `report`, so an orchestrator needs no log read; none of these is text the worker wrote. End states are sticky until the worker next starts, so a wait started after the end still gets it. A worker that keeps crashing is restarted with growing gaps up to its bundle's `max_restarts`, then ends `kept_crashing` with the last crash's code or signal. At a daemon's boot, a worker that was running is started again, through the admission point and under the same handle, name, labels and history, after a clean shutdown, when its process outlived the daemon (it is killed first), or when the host-instance key changed (Linux boot id plus the start time of PID 1; macOS `kern.boottime`; Windows `BootId`), meaning it died with its host or container. A walled worker's init now records why it ended the worker (`workers/<handle>/init-outcome`): one that died because CAR's daemon exited or crashed is started again, on the same host too, and one that ended on its own while its exit could not be recorded ends `interrupted_by_restart`, with the exit its init saw, and is not started again.
